# Sarasvatī Saṅlapa. Uncommon Dialogues

## Dedication

This book is dedicated to all the truthful followers of His Divine Grace Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura Prabhupada and most importantly to his dear disciple, His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada, who brought the teachings of his divine master to outside India and fulfilled the prediction of Srila Thakura Bhaktivinoda.

## Introduction

Much has been said or written about the life and teachings of His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura. Just as if one dives into an ocean and hopes to reach the end of it, the result will be futile, similarly if one tries to discuss the characteristics of agents of the Lord who have been sent from the spiritual abode for the purpose of disseminating spiritual knowledge, the result will always be baffling. Lord Ananta continually glorifies the Lord’s activities but has not reached the end, similarly the glories of the great devotees of the Lord, who share the same qualities as the Lord, can also have no end.

Those who were fortunate to have heard from Srila Sarasvati Thakura bear testimony to his divine characteristics. He was an uncompromising spiritual master who never tired of speaking the teachings of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu and Lord Krishna. Speaking topics of Krishna was his only reason for existence. He lived only for that. And when he realized that his mission had been accomplished as desired by Krishna, he closed his manifest activities to the mundane vision.

We have been privileged to capture some of Srila Sarasvati Thakura’s essential teachings. The central element of his teachings is often repeated throughout his many writings. His central theme has always been how a jiva sees himself in relationship to serving Krishna. Is Krishna to be seen as an object of enjoyment or the jiva as an object of Krishna’s enjoyment? In the mood of trying to practice bhakti, an aspirant often thinks of what is in it for him. The mentality is to see God as the provider of all needs in the guise of performing bhakti. But such a mentality is cheating if God is seen as the provider, if God is to provide all needs of existence. In that way, God is seen as the servant, rather than the served. God, the provider is not the object of unalloyed devotional service but rather, the sprit soul’s full surrender is for the enjoyment of Krishna.

Srila Sarasvati Thakura was the living embodiment of the teachings of the six Gosvamis of Vrndavana, who forsook everything and established the places of Krishna’s pastimes in Vrndavana and wrote volumes of bhakti scriptures for the benefit of humanity who needs to revive its eternal loving relationship with Krishna. Srila Sarasvati Thakura was thus a living encyclopedia of Vedic knowledge. All branches of knowledge were at the tip of his tongue. Lay people and scholars alike were always amazed by his erudition.

This compilation of his conversations includes some of the interactions Srila Sarasvati Thakura had with his magazine editors, eminent scholars of his time, and religious figures. Some of these conversations have been made available to the English readers as some were originally spoken in English by Srila Sarasvati Thakura. But the rest of these conversations are in Bengali, and were originally compiled by Sripad Sundarananda Vidyavinoda. It is these Bengali conversations that we have translated into English, so the people of the world can have this treasury of his teachings in their possession. We have also included the remaining seven chapters of the Vaktrtavali in this book. Those seven chapters were later discovered after our book, “Vaktrtavali” had been published. The readers are therefore given more ammunition in their light against any hindrances that may come while practicing bhakti.

Many of Srila Sarasvati Thakura’s teachings have been published in the English language including biographies. We however continue to discover more manuscripts which are almost getting lost due to circumstances that are often difficult for persons like myself to explain. But it has always been my prayer that as these manuscripts are discovered, the attempt will be made to translate and publish them. In this way, I am hoping to beable to render some insignificant service to the acaryas.

Aspiring servant of the servant,  
Isvara dasa,  
Kolkata, 2016

**A brief life sketch of Sri Srimad Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Gosvami Prabhupada**

Srila Sarasvati Thakura appeared at Sri Jagannath Puri on 6thFebruary 1874. Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura and Srimati Bhagavati devi received this son as a mercy of Lord Jagannatha. The Rathayatra festival arrived six months after Srila Sarasvati Thakura’s appearance. Lord Jagannatha remained stationary in front of his birthplace, Narayana Chata for three days upon His chariot. The child became overwhelmed to see the Lord of his life, Sri Jagannatha. Taking the weeping child on her lap, Bhagavati devi climbed on the chariot and after paying her obeisances, she offered the child at the lotus feet of the Lord of the universe. The child grabbed the lotus feet with so much happiness as if He was very familiar to him. Just then, a flower garland from Lord Jagannatha’s neck fell to the neck of the child. Seeing that Lord Jagannatha has given the remnants of His flower garland to the child, the brahmanas chanted “Hari Hari” with joy. Mother Bhagavati devi picked up the child along with the prasada garland.

Then the brahmana priests began to say, “Mother, this son of yours will be an exalted personality here, and he will preach the glories of Jagannatha all over the world.” On hearing such an auspicious blessings, Mother Bhagavati began to shed tears of joy and started thanking those brahmanas and Lord Jagannatha. On that same day, after the Rathayatra, the child had the first grain eating ceremony.

According to the name of Lord Jagannatha’s consort, Vimaladevi, the child was named Vimala Prasada. Vimala devi is the maintainer of Sri Jagannatha-Ksetra and the principal goddess of the temple.

At that time, Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura was as a high class magistrate under the British Government. Srimad Bhaktivinoda Thakura was a very pious and well behaved personality. His wife Sri Bhagavati devi too was a devoted lady. Other than the Lord’s prasada, they never allowed their children to eat any other unoffered foodstuffs. They never allowed the children to speak about worldly topics and associate with worldly people.

During that period, Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura inundated the land with the flowing of the Ganges water in the form of preaching pure devotional service as inaugurated by Gaurasundara. He composed and published many devotional literatures and inaugurated Prapannasramas and Namahattas at different places.

In the year 1881, a Deity of Kurma was found while digging the foundation of Bhakti Bhavan at Rambagan, Calcutta. Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura gave that Kurma deity to Sri Sarasvati Thakura along with the holy name and mantra and instructed him how to serve the Deity.

Sarasvati Thakura was very intelligent, and so he completed the study at Sanskrta college within a short time. He studied Sanskrta especially from a Vedic scholar, Sri Prthvidhara Sharma. Thereafter he himself started a school of astrology. Many students from high-class family studied in that school.

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura ordered Sri Sarasvati Thakura to worship Hari and vigorously preach the teachings of Sri Caitanya.

In his childhood, Sarasvati Thakura visited all the places related to the associates of Gaura situated within Gauda Mandala with Sri Bhaktivinoda Thakura.

In the year 1900, during the month of March, Sarasvati Thakura traveled with Bhaktivinoda Thakura to places like Balesvara, Remuna, Bhuvanesvara and Puri, and explained Sri Caitanya-caritamrta at different places under the order of Bhaktivinoda Thakura.

On 9thAsadha 1321 Bengali Era on Sri Gadadhara Pandita’s disappearance day, Srimad Bhaktivinoda Thakura Mahasaya entered into the Lord’s eternal pastimes. Before he entered into eternal pastimes he instructed Sarasvati Thakura to spread the literatures written by the six Gosvamis, to develop Mayapura, the birth place of Mahaprabhu and to preach Gaurasundara’s teachings everywhere.

One year after Sri Bhakivinoda Thakura’s disappearance, on that same day, Mother Sri Bhagavati devi returned to the eternal abode. She too, at the time of leaving the world, caught hold of Sarasvati Thakura’s hand and said, “You must preach the topics of my Gaurasundara everywhere in Sridhama Mayapura,” Sri Sarasvati Thakura accepted spiritual initiation from Sri Gaura Kisora Dasa Babaji Mahasaya. He never encouraged the mundane philosophy, sahajiyavada and Mayavada.

In the year 1918, 7th March, on the appearance day of Sri Gaura, He accepted Sannyasa at Sridhama Mayapura and also established the Sri Caitanya Matha on that same day. After Sannyasa, he became renowned as Sri Srimad Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Prabhupada. Srila Prabhupada restarted the propagation of Sri Gaura Krsna’s topics with great vigour all over the India.

Within a short time, Srila Prabhupada established about 66 pure devotional centers or mathas in Calcutta, Dhaka, Mayamansingh, Narayanaganj, Cittagong, Cuttack, Midnapura, Remuna, Balesvara, Bhuvanesvara,Puri, Alalanatha, Madras, Kabhura, Delhi, Bombay, Kuruksetra, Lucknow, Patna, Gaya, Kasi, Haridvara, Allahabad, Mathura, Vrndavana, and other important places in India as well as places outside India like Rengoon,London and awarded the renounced order of life or Sannyasa to 25 persons highly educated in eastern and western education. He edited and published about six daily, weekly, fortnightly and monthly spiritual magazines in English, Hindi, Oriya and Assamese language.

On 1937, 1stJanuary, Friday, during Brahma Muhurta, the sun-like Vaisnava acarya disappeared from this world. Like his predecessor acaryas, Sri Ramanuja, Sri Madhva, Sri Visnusvami and Sri Nimbarka, he was a matchless lion-like acarya for preaching the philosophy of acintyabhedabheda.

## Part 1 - Sri Sarasvati Sanlapa

## Conversation 1

**Srila Prabhupada and the Editor of The Gaudiya**  
**Tuesday, March 23, 1926**

\[Srila Prabhupada was dictating the purport of Srila Narottama Dasa Thakura Mahasaya’s Prarthana at Sri Gaudiya Matha, Ultadanga Junction Road, Calcutta. Gaudiya editor, Srimad Sundarananda Vidyavinoda, was sitting at Srila Prabhupada’s feet, taking dictation. At that time, the Gaudiya editor asked Srila Prabhupada some questions.\]

**The Gaudiya**: How do we understand Sri, Bhu, and Nila (or Lila), and how should we address them? Who are they in Gaura’s pastimes?

**Srila Prabhupada**: The opulent manifestation, the Absolute Truth, Lord Narayana, has three energies: Sri, Bhu, and Nila. Kamala, or Laksmi, is sri sakti; devotional service to Lord Visnu is bhu-sakti; and the basis or land on which Lord Narayana walks is nila- or lila-sakti, also known as durga-sakti, or the support of the universe. All three energies are present in Gaura-Narayana.

All incarnations are present in the body of the source of incarnations. So according to kaimutika nyaya \[“if this, then even more so that”\], Lord Narayana is automatically present within Sri Krsna, and therefore in Sriman Mahaprabhu, who is the Personality of Godhead, the one without a second, and the same son of the king of Vraja. Sriman Mahaprabhu contains all tattvas. This is why Thakura Vrndavana Dasa addressed Sriman Mahaprabhu as Ksirodakasayi. Srila Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami Prabhu stated, “The words of a devotee cannot be untrue,” and so he proved that in Sri Krsna, who is the source of all incarnations, all tattvas are included.

The pastimes Sri Gaurasundara displayed before going to Gaya mainly reveal His pastimes as Narayana. That is, in His householder pastimes, Sriman Mahaprabhu manifested only the form and characteristics of Narayana. Therefore, the householder pastimes of Laksmipriya and Gaurasundara should be understood to be the pastimes of Laksmi-Narayana in Vaikuntha. In text 43 of Sri Gaura Ganoddesa-dipika, Kavi Karnapura writes that King Janaka of Mithila appeared as Vallabhacarya in Gaura’s pastimes. Laksmipriya was Vallabhacarya’s daughter, and she was the combined form of Janaki and Rukmini.

When Sri Gaurasundara revealed His love and devotion \[openly, for the first time in Gaya\], Sri Laksmipriya disappeared. In other words, while Sri Visnupriya, who is the personification of loving devotional service \[bhakti\], was still growing up, Laksmipriya was present as the maidservant of Gaura-Narayana. Gradually, as that personification of loving devotional service grew to be old enough to serve Sri Gaurasundara, Sri Laksmideviwas disappearing. And from the viewpoint of tattva, Sri Visnupriya Devi is the personification of Bhu-sakti. In Sri Gaura Ganoddesa-dipika, Kavi Karnapura writes that King Satrajit appeared in Gaura-lila as Raja PanditaSanatana. The personified Bhu-sakti, mother of the universe, Sri Visnupriyaji, was his only daughter.

In Sri Caitanya-candrodaya-nataka, Kavi Karnapura calls Sri Visnupriya Devi “the form of the earth.” Visnupriya Devi supports Sri Gaurasundara’s loving devotional service. Sri Gaurasundara is the combined form of Radha and Krsna, so the mother of the universe, Visnupriya, who is affectionate to the devotees, can be called a maidservant of Radha and Krsna. She can be called a devotee, a companion, and a supreme goddess of the daughter of Vrsabhanu.

In the Lord’s adi-lila - that is, up until He went to Gaya - whatever characteristics Sri Gaurasundara displayed belonged to His form as Narayana. He accepted Sri Laksmipriya and Sri Visnupriya as His legitimate wives. Even after returning from Gaya, whatever pastimes He exhibited are a mixture; in other words, in both sets of pastimes one finds manifestations of opulence. For example, sometimes He displayed His four-armed Nrsimha form in Srivasa’s courtyard, His Varaha form at Murari Gupta’s house, and sometimes He climbed on Lord Visnu’s throne.

In the last part of His householder life, He revealed to the world the sweet katha of Krsna’s pastimes spoken by one absorbed in the mood of Radha. It is not that He did not speak krsna-lila in the middle part of His householder life, but after He returned from Gaya He called out loudly, “Gopi! Gopi!” This is because He was absorbed in the mood of a servitor, even though He himself is the object of service, the Personality of Godhead. He ordered Thakura Haridasa and Sri Nityananda to glorify Krsna’s pastimes door to door throughout the world.

**The Gaudiya**: If Sri Gaurasundara is Krsna and Sri Gadadhara Pandita Radhika, then are there conjugal mellows between them?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Sri Gaurasundara is the combined form of Radha and Krsna. He has Krsna’s body or form, but He is completely absorbed in the mood of the daughter of Vrsabhanu. That mood is so intensely and integrally present in Him that Radha’s bodily complexion covers Krsna’s blackish complexion.

Pandita Sri Gadadhara Gosvami, however, appears in Gaura-lila as Srimati Radharani’s sentiment, and Sri Dasa Gadadhara is manifest as Her complexion. In the texts 153 and 154 of Sri Gaura Ganoddesa-dipika, Kavi Karnapura writes:

*athava bhagavan gaurah svecchaya gatri rupatam*  
*atah sri radhika rupah sri gadadhara panditah*  
*radha vibhuti rupa ya candrakantih pura sthita*  
*sadya gauranga nikate dasa vamsa gadadharah*

**“By His own will Lord Caitanya appeared in this world in three different forms at the same time. In the same way, Srimati Radharani appeared as Gadadhara Pandita. Srimati Radharani’s expansion as Candrakanti Devi appeared as Gadadhara Dasa, a close associate of Lord Caitanya.”**

By His supreme will, Sri Gaurasundara, who appeared with Radha’s mood, Himself appeared as Krsna, Radhika, and Lalita. Sri Gadadhara Pandita is the form of Radhika’s mood; in other words, in order to manifest Her emotion, Srimati Radhika appeared as Sri Gadadhara Pandita. To manifest Her complexion, She appeared as Gadadhara Dasa. It is absurd to consider Mahaprabhu to be the embodiment of enjoyment, Krsna, and Gadadhara Pandita enjoying with Him, because Sri Gaurasundara is absorbed in Srimati Radharani’s mood. Being absorbed in the mood of asraya-radhika,Lord Gaurasundara is always searching for Krsna. Gadadhara, too, absorbed in the mood of the asraya, supports Sri Gaurasundara’s mood in the mellow of separation. Both are intoxicated with that mood and mellow of separation.

Still, in Gaura-Gadadhara bhajana we find that Gadadhara is considered sakti-tattva, or the Lord’s energy, while Gaurasundara is saktiman-tattva, or the supreme energetic. We should understand that Sri Gaurasundara has descended with the body of Vrajendra-nandana and the mood and complexion of Sri Radhika, while Sri Gadadhara is the manifestation of that same Sri Radhika’s mood and an expansion of Her body. Gadadhara Pandita is not Srimati Radhika as such; he is the energy of the asraya category, or the form of Her sentiment.

Trying to deny the different characteristics found in the pastimes of separation and conjugal love through some invention invites the fault of rasabhasa, or the overlapping of rasas. Because of this fault, philosophies like Gaura-nagarivada and others have cropped up.

**The Gaudiya**: Were there any sadhana-siddha devotee during Mahaprabhu’s time? If so, who were they?

**Srila Prabhupada**: There is no reason to think there were no sadhana-siddha devotees during Mahaprabhu’s time. Sarvabhauma Bhattacarya, who was previously Brhaspati, and who was controlled by the fruits of karma (see Gaura Ganoddesa-dipika, text 119), and Gopinatha Acarya, who was previously Brahma, the creator of karma (see Gaura Ganoddesa-dipika, text 75) were both sadhana-siddha devotees. According to the Lord’s associates they were nitya-siddhas - total absorption in the Lord’s service in a liberated condition is the symptom of a nitya-siddha devotee. But according to material eyes - through perverted vision – a nitya-siddha may appear to be a sadhana-siddha.

**The Gaudiya**: Who was Srila Thakura Haridasa? Some people say he was Brahma. Is he a sadhana-siddha devotee?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Some people say that Prahlada entered Thakura Haridasa. Sri Gaura Ganoddesa-dipika (text 93), asserts that Rcika Muni’s son, Jamadagni, the greatly ascetic Brahma, and Prahlada Maharaja appeared as Thakura Haridasa. Srila Murari Gupta writes in Sri Caitanya-carita that because Rcika Muni’s son did not wash some tulasi leaves after picking them, his father cursed him to take birth in a yavana family. “He has now appeared as the most devoted Haridasa.”

Those who are eternally inclined to serve Lord Hari are called nitya-siddha, and those who are eternally averse to serving the Lord but who have now become inclined to serve Him, by His mercy or the mercy of His devotees, are called sadhana-siddha devotees. Prahlada is eternally inclined to serve Krsna’s lotus feet.

**The Gaudiya**: Are Jagai and Madhai sadhana-siddhas or nitya-siddhas?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Jaya and Vijaya appeared as Jagai and Madhai during Gaura’s pastimes (Gaura Ganoddesa-dipika, text 115). Although they performed pastimes in the mood of neutrality, they are to be considered nitya-siddhas.

**The Gaudiya**: Thakura \[Narottama dasa\] Mahasaya writes, gaurangera sangi-gane, nitya-siddha kari mane, se yaya brajendra-nanda pasa: “Anyone who is preaching the sankirtana movement is an associate of Caitanya Mahaprabhu.” So whom should we accept as gaurangera sangi, Lord Gauranga’s associates or companions?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Only those who support Sri Gauranga’s mood of separation are actual associates of Gauranga; they are His sangi. Those who fulfill Gaura’s mission, who serve His inner desire, are His associates.Those who eternally remain present with Gauranga in order to serve Him are gaurangera sangi.

While traveling in South India, Sriman Mahaprabhu converted people in village after village to Vaisnavism, but those who did not engage constantly in His mission - who did not associate with Mahaprabhu at all times by dedicating everything to Him - how can they be called His “associates”?

The word sangi derives from the word sanga, which means “one who approaches completely.” Those who did not associate with the Lord constantly cannot be called His sangi. They can be called His bhaktas, but not His sangi. Another word for sangi is parisada. Even though Thakura Narottama did not appear when Sriman Mahaprabhu was having His pastimes, he is still Sriman Mahaprabhu’s sangi because he appeared in this world only to fulfill Mahaprabhu’s mission and is eternally absorbed in His service - totally overwhelmed by Mahaprabhu’s internal mood. He nourishes the Lord’s mood of separation and is therefore a nitya-siddha devotee.

**The Gaudiya**: What is the “indirect mood,” which relates to Kamsa, Jarasandha, and others, when it is manifest in Goloka Vrndavana?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Goloka is a fully pure, spiritual abode. There is no abomination there, no contempt, no inferiority complex, and no sense of the transitory. Therefore, there is also no violence or bloodshed in Goloka. But to nourish Krsna’s pastimes, there is an awareness of unfavorable things. That awareness enhances, in ever new ways, the favorable service attitude in the hearts of Krsna’s servants, such as Nanda, Yasoda, and their followers. So in Goloka Vrndavana there is only an idea that persons like Kamsa and Jarasandha exist. They are not actually there the way they are present in Krsna’s manifested pastimes in the material world.

**The Gaudiya**: Is the material propensity constitutional to the soul the way the eternal spiritual propensity is constitutional?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Maya does not constitutionally influence the spirit soul, so there is no material propensity in the soul. One finds the soul affected by the material propensity when it is conditioned, meaning dormant or inactive. When the soul is dormant, it is the mind, the reflection of spirit, that engages in material activities. In the soul’s constitution there is nothing but the propensity to serve Krsna. It is that propensity to serve Krsna that we call the “spiritual propensity.” In illusion a living entity mistakes the acts of the spirit’s reflection \[the mind\] for spiritual acts.

**The Gaudiya**: If a living entity is, by nature, always aloof from maya, and if matter has influence only over the conditioned soul’s material body and mind - isn’t this similar to what the Mayavadis say? What is the use of practicing Sadhana in such a condition?

**Srila Prabhupada:** This is not a Mayavada argument. Mayavadi's do not accept that the spirit soul is eternal or that the soul has the eternal propensity to serve Lord Hari. Sadhana-kriya \[pious, ritualistic practices\] is not eternal, because it does not act on the soul. Sadhana-kriya is effective only on the platform of the mind, the reflection of spirit, but not on the spirit soul itself.

There is a difference between sadhana-kriya, which is controlled by time and yet still may destroy aversion to serving Hari, and the eternal process of sadhana-bhakti. Sadhana-kriya refers to those limbs of practice one uses to destroy anarthas. For example, if dust has been accumulating for a long time on a mirror, you will not be able to see your face in the mirror. The mirror has not become useless or lost its capacity to reflect your face; the capacity to reflect is still fully present in the mirror, and as soon as the dust is wiped from it, you will see your face again. This act of wiping is called sadhana-kriya. One has to wipe off the “mind” covering, which we are calling the reflection of spirit, from the soul along with the illusion of accepting the reflection of spirit as the self. Then only can the soul’s spiritual propensity awaken. If a fully functioning engine stands stationary, it does not mean the engine has lost its ability to move. So even if the eternal propensity to serve is not currently active in one’s soul, still, it is there. When anarthas are eradicated, the propensity to serve Krsna will automatically manifest.

But sadhana-kriya does not work on the soul. Sadhana-bhakti, however, is eternally effective on the spiritual platform. When sadhana-bhakti matures, it transforms gradually into bhava-bhakti and then prema-bhakti, just as a mango has three stages of ripeness: green, half-ripe, and fully ripe. It is the fully ripe fruit that is useful in Krsna’s service.

Sadhana-kriya does not mature the soul to a state of prema-bhakti. For example, imagine there is fresh honey in a bottle. Suddenly some mud gets stuck on the outside of the bottle. The mud cannot touch the honey. Just because the bottle is muddy does not mean one has to wash the honey inside with water. One needs only to wash the glass jar that contains the honey. Similarly, no sadhana-kriya can touch the spirit soul; it can only be applied to the transformable mind, which is the reflection of spirit. This is why Srimad-Bhagavatam (11.23.45) states, sarve mano-nigraha-laksanantah: “Charity, prescribed duties, observance of major and minor regulative principles, hearing from scripture, pious works, and purifying vows all have as their final aim the subduing of the mind.”

As soon as mental speculation, or the propensity to perform sadhana-kriya, is checked, the spiritual propensity awakens. When one practices sadhana-bhakti from the platform of one’s spiritual propensity to serve Krsna, the fortunate soul gradually becomes elevated to bhava-bhakti and then prema-bhakti. Because people cannot understand the connection between sadhana-kriya and sadhana-bhakti as well as the differences between them, they concoct various philosophies and sadhanas. All these cause their anarthas to increase.

## Conversation 2

**Srila Prabhupada and Thakur Saheb Kusala Singh**  
**October 16, 1927**

\[This conversation was in English. Srila Prabhupada and his followers had returned to Srila Prabhupada’s residence after visiting the “Galta” hill in Jaipur, where Gaudiya Vedantacarya Srila Baladeva Vidyabhusana became victorious in debate. The landlord of Gijgarh, Sriman Thakur Saheb Kusala Singhji, had come to hear hari-katha from the crest jewel of the Gaudiya Vaisnava Om Visnupada Sri Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Gosvami Prabhupada. A two-hour conversation took place between Srila Prabhupada and Kusala Singh. Kusala Singh began by introducing himself as a disciple of the renunciant Gaudiya Vaisnava of Sridhama Vrndavana, Sriman Ramakrsna Dasaji.\]

**Kusala Singh**: Paramahamsaji, from your words I understood that the Gosvami family has not come down through seminal lineage. Is this a historical fact?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Gaura’s associates, the Six Gosvamis, who were the acaryas of the entire Gaudiya sampradaya, were not given the title Gosvami based on their connection with a worldly family or caste. Their worldly predecessors were not known as “Gosvami,” nor have they left behind any worldly familial line. Their only family are the renounced, akincana followers in disciplic succession who are fully surrendered to Krsna.

**Kusala Singh**: Did Sriman Mahaprabhu wear saffron robes?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Yes, He manifested the pastime of wearing saffron cloth when He displayed His pastime of accepting the renounced order of life. You can know this by reading books like Sri Caitanya-caritamrta.

**Kusala Singh**: Since I have not mastered Bengali, that book is closed to me. As far as I remember, all the Vaisnavas I met in Vrndavana wore white. Neither do I remember seeing a Sannyasa stick in anyone’s hand. That’s why I thought Mahaprabhu also wore white.

**Srila Prabhupada**: You heard about this from that professor at Ravenshaw College in Cuttuck, Mr. Sanyal. \[Srila Prabhupada was speaking to Kusala Singh after Kusala Singh had been in discussion with Professor Sanyal.\] During Sriman Mahaprabhu’s time, the original root of the desire tree of devotional service, Sri Madhavendra Puri, and his nine disciples, all wore saffron. Tridandi sampradayas headed by Sri Prabodhananda Sarasvati also wore saffron robes and accepted the renounced order. Since the Six Gosvamis were Paramahamsas, there is no evidence that any of them wore saffron or carried symbols of renunciation, like dandas. Their successor paramahamsa Vaisnavas did not feel the need to protect the prestige of accepting Vedic Sannyasa, which is based on the path of vidhi, regulative principles, because they were Paramahamsas on the path of raga, attachment.

**Kusala Singh**: I have heard that it is prohibited for Vaisnavas to wear red or saffron. Why then do you all wear saffron cloth?

**Srila Prabhupada**: For the supremely independent paramahamsa Vaisnavas of the raga-marga, no mutual obligation compels them to wear saffron cloth the way the followers of the maryada-marga are compelled. This is because Srila Sanatana Gosvami, who is an ideal example for the followers of the spontaneous raga-marga community, said, rakta vastra vaisnavera parite na yuyaya: “This saffron cloth is unfit for a Vaisnava to wear; therefore I have no use for it. I shall give it to a stranger.” Later, a few unqualified and apparently precocious persons unfit for the genuine renounced order tried to jump over their own position and achieve greatness by imitating the behavior of the naturally spontaneous Paramahamsas. Those persons became averse to the Vedas in the guise of being raga-marga followers.

This jumping over is never prescribed for Gaudiya Vaisnavas. This is especially true of the servants of paramahamsa gurus. Such disciples can never take their master’s position. In order to protect these polite disciples \[the maryadavadis\], who are not proud but are determined to rectify the misconception of such a faulty so-called Gaudiya sampradaya, and to protect them from adultery while remaining within the framework of varnasrama principles and Vedic injunctions, the pure Gaudiya Vaisnavas of the Sri Visva-vaisnava-raja Sabha teach against the foolishness of pseudo Vaisnavas supported by and based on the scripture.

**Kusala Singh**: Were there tridandi sannyasis among Sriman Mahaprabhu’s associates? Was the custom of tridanda current among previous acaryas?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Sriman Mahaprabhu benefited the world by manifesting two kinds of associates: householder Vaisnavas and renunciant Vaisnavas. Both served Krsna’s pleasure. Mahaprabhu’s householder Vaisnavas were different from householder sadhana-bhaktas; Mahaprabhu’s Vaisnava householders were Paramahamsas. That’s why it didn’t matter that they lived at home and not in the forest. They were all jagad-gurus. A householder Sadhaka, however, can never become a jagad-guru or acarya. Wherever an arrangement has been made for householders to serve as spiritual masters, it has been meant to uplift the extremely attached fruitive workers a little from their comparatively lower position.

Among Mahaprabhu’s associates we see three kinds of renunciants. They are all jagad-gurus. Just as the above-mentioned Vaisnava paramahamsa householders always engage in Lord Hari’s service by controlling mind, body, and speech, so the Vaisnava renunciants do the same. An example of a Vaisnava householder is Mahaprabhu’s devotee, Srivasa; among His renunciant devotees, there are the various gosvami associates, including Sri Rupapada and others. These gosvamis were exemplars of tridandi Sannyasa. All of them sing the song of the Avanti brahmana mentioned in Srimad-Bhagavatam. Among the renunciant gosvamis, Sri Rupapada, the origin of the rupanuga path, delines tridandi gosvami in the first verse of his composition, Sri Upadesamrta, and in a number of other verses, like the iha yasya harer dasye karmana manasa gira verse of the Bhakti-rasamrta-sindhu, he spoke of the ideal tridandi.

Among Sriman Mahaprabhu’s second group of renunciant gosvamis, Srila Prabodhananda Sarasvatipada, the origin of the Prabodhanandi path, was the spiritual master of the great acarya and compiler of the Vaisnava smrti, Srila Gopala Bhatta Gosvami. In the verse, dante nidhaya trnakam, which appears in Prabodhananda Sarasvati’s Sri Caitanya-candramrta, he instructs everyone to give up the mood of an attached householder averse to Sri Caitanya, including the sinful desire to become one with Sri Caitanya by accepting ekadanda Sannyasa. Instead, he writes, one should accept tridanda-sannyasa and surrender himself at the lotus feet of Sri Caitanya.

Among Mahaprabhu’s third group of renunciant gosvamis, Srila Gadadhara Pandita Gosvami Prabhu, the origin of the Gadadhari branch of gosvamis, preached the ideal of serving Krsna in this world by accepting tridanda-sannyasa even though he was enacting the pastime of being a brhadvati. Sri Madhava Upadhyaya of Trihuta accepted tridanda Sannyasa from Pandita Gosvami Prabhu and was then known as Madhavacarya. Sri Vallabha Bhatta, accepting the lordship of Gadadhara Gosvami Prabhu, accepted tridanda Sannyasa from this Madhavacarya, his own godbrother, thirty-nine days before his disappearance.

Srila Sridhara Svamipada, who was greatly honored by Mahaprabhu and adorned with the titles jagad-guru and bhaktyekaraksaka, “the only savior of the devotional cult,” was a Vaisnava tridandi sannyasi.

In the Vaisnava sampradaya founded by Visnusvami, which is one of the four authorized Vaisnava sampradayas, we find the names of seven hundred tridandi acaryas and 108 tridandi titles. Information about tridanda Sannyasa in the Vaisnava sampradaya of Sri Ramanujacarya is well known to all.

**Kusala Singh**: Is tridanda Sannyasa discussed in any authentic Gaudiya Vaisnava literature?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Apart from the acceptance of tridanda Sannyasa described in the Eleventh Canto of Srimad-Bhagavatam, which is the spotless king of evidence (pramana) for the Gaudiya Vaisnavas, no other system of Vaisnava sannyasa-asrama is mentioned in sastra. On the path of raga in the Sri Rupanuga-Gaudiya-Vaisnava sampradaya, the elevated position of accepting Sannyasa and changing one’s garb is confined to the exalted ragatmikas, or residents of Vraja. The rules and regulations set down according to the regulative path or regulative scriptures that Sri Gopala Bhatta Gosvami, a follower of Sri Rupa and a disciple of Tridandipada Srila Prabodhananda Sarasvati, introduced are written in the Vaisnava Smrtis Sri Hari Bhakti Vilasa, Samskara Dipika, and so on. Dhyanacandra’s Samskara-Candrika-Paddhati considers acceptance of tridanda Sannyasa a principal limb of Vaisnavism, and thus Vedic proofs have been quoted in favor of it. Ancient texts like the Bhagavata Candrika, Prameyamala, and Satadusana consider it extremely necessary to accept tridanda Sannyasa, and many considerations and scriptural proofs have been offered. Scriptural proofs for the acceptance of Sannyasa are clearly and widely found in innumerable places in the various branches of the Vedic literature, including the Javalopanisad, in many places of twenty Dharmasastra, in different places in Srimad-Bhagavatam, in all the systems of the Gaudiya Vaisnavas, in Sridhara Svami’s Bhavartha Dipika, in the Skanda Purana, in the Padma Purana, in the Satvata Samhita, in the Manu Samhita, and in the Ekadasi Tattva. In Srimad-Bhagavatam the Supreme Lord instructs that until one becomes an uttama-adhikara (Vaisnava paramahamsa) one has no alternative but to accept tridanda Sannyasa. Everyone must accept it.

Srimad-Bhagavatam (11.29.17, 19) states:

*yavat sarvesu bhutesu mad-bhavo nopajayate*  
*tavad evam upasita van-manah-kaya-vrttibhih*

**“Until one has fully developed the ability to see Me within all living beings, one must continue to worship Me by this process with the activities of his speech, mind, and body.”**

*ayam hi sarva-kalpanam sadhricino mato mama*  
*mad-bhavah sarva-bhutesu mano-vak-kaya-vrttibhih*

**“Indeed, I consider this process - using one’s mind, words, and bodily functions for realizing Me within all living beings - to be the best possible method of spiritual enlightenment.”**

The word danda means “to subdue.” To accept tridanda means to subdue or control the speech, mind, and body by engaging them in the service of the Supreme Lord. This is widely described in Sastra.

That the Paramahamsas on the raga path have no need to wear saffron or carry a danda, which is an external symbol of the vaidhi sannyasa-asrama, is proven by Srimad-Bhagavatam 11.18.28, where it says, *sa-lingan asramams tyaktva cared avidhi-gocarah*: “My devotee who is detached even from the desire for liberation neglects those duties based on external rituals or paraphernalia.”

In his commentary on this text, Srila Sridhara Svami writes, “My devotees, who do not care for liberation, should give up all symbols of the sannyasa-asrama along with their attachment for the principles of that asrama. Instead, they should follow whatever is appropriate for them.”

**Kusala Singh**: I am very pleased to hear your words. I now have to go to perform my daily rituals. I will come another time to hear from you.

**Srila Prabhupada**: Srimad-Bhagavatam (11.20.9) states:

*tavat karmani kurvita na nirvidyeta yavata*  
*mat-katha-sravanadau va Sraddha yavan na jayate*

**“As long as one is not satiated by fruitive activity and has not awakened his taste for devotional service by sravanam kirtanam visnoh, one has to act according to the regulative principles of the Vedic injunctions.”**

And from Srimad-Bhagavatam (1.2.8):

*dharmah svanusthitah pumsam visvaksena-kathasu yah*  
*notpadayed yadi ratim srama eva hi kevalam*

**“The occupational activities a man performs according to his own position are only so much useless labor if they do not provoke attraction for the message of the Personality of Godhead.”**

And Srimad-Bhagavatam (10.1.4):

*nivrtta-tarsair upagiyamanad*  
*bhavausadhac chrotra-mano-’bhiramat*  
*ka uttamasloka-gunanuvadat*  
*puman virajyeta vina pasughnat*

**“Glorification of the Supreme Personality of Godhead is performed in the parampara system; that is, it is conveyed from spiritual master to disciple. Such glorification is relished by those no longer interested in the false, temporary glorification of this cosmic manifestation. Descriptions of the Lord are the right medicine for the conditioned soul undergoing repeated birth and death. Therefore, who will cease hearing such glorification of the Lord except a butcher or one who is killing his own self?”**

**Kusala Singh**: Each of the nine processes of devotional service is equally important. Therefore, according to their taste, some engage in arcana, some in kirtana, and so on. How can kirtana be called the best?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Srimad-Bhagavatam (11.2.47) states:

*arcayam eva haraye*  
*pujam yah sraddhayehate*  
*na tad-bhaktesu canyesu*  
*sa bhaktah prakrtah smrtah*

**“A devotee who faithfully engages in the worship of the Deity in the temple but does not behave properly toward other devotees or people in general is called a prakrta-bhakta, a materialistic devotee, and is considered to be in the lowest position.”**

Also,

*yasyatma-buddhih kunape tri-dhatuke*  
*sva-dhih kalatradisu bhauma ijya-dhih*  
*yat-tirtha-buddhih salile na karhicij*  
*janesv abhijnesu sa eva go-kharah*

**“One who identifies his self as the inert body composed of mucus, bile, and air, who assumes his wife and family are permanently his own, who thinks an earthen image or the land of his birth is worshipable, or who sees a place of pilgrimage as merely the water there, but who never identifies himself with, feels kinship with, worships, or even visits those who are wise in spiritual truth - such a person is no better than a cow or an ass.” (Srimad-Bhagavatam 10.84.13)**

**Kusala Singh**: Does this mean you do not accept the worship of the Lord’s Deity \[as equal to kirtana\]?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Neophyte devotees worship the Lord’s Deity form simply out of some worldly formality or etiquette. Such worship does not actually reach the Supreme Lord. Since the mundane devotees live absorbed in their gross and subtle bodily concept, mixtures of karma and jnana remain in their hearts. These do not go away, because such devotees lack transcendental knowledge. Hence their worship is not pure. Every person duly initiated by the spiritual master through the pancaratriki process must engage in arcana, or Deity worship.

Arcana is mainly recommended for rich householders. If they remain attached to sravanam kirtanam visnoh smaranam like exalted akincana devotees, then they will not commit the fault of miserliness. But those whoappear to worship the Lord by hiring professional priests to serve the Deity only prove their attachment to material enjoyment; they are lazy and faithless. Sri Jiva Gosvamipada said that limbs of devotional service like Deity worship should be accompanied by kirtana. Even in the verse beginning tavat karmani kurvita \[“One should continue to perform the Vedic ritualistic activities until one actually becomes detached from material sense gratification and develops faith for hearing and chanting about Me” - Srimad-Bhagavatam 11.20.9\], it is clearly stated that a pure worshiper should not give up the worshipable Deity form of the Lord. For those who have the faith characteristic of a kanistha-adhikari, there is an arrangement for arcana in the kingdom of devotional service.

**Kusala Singh**: Didn’t Sri Rupa, Sri Sanatana, and others also engage in Deity worship?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Sri Rupa and Sri Sanatana and others did not perform Deity worship. Sri Sanatana Prabhu says:

*jayati jayati namananda-rupam murarer*  
*viramita-nija-dharma-dhyana-pujadi-yatnam*  
*katham api sakrd attain mukti-dam praninam yat*  
*paramam amrtam ekam jivanam bhusanam me*

**“All glories, all glories to the all-blissful holy name of Sri Krsna, which causes the devotee to give up all conventional religious duties, meditation, and worship. When somehow or other uttered even once by a living entity, the holy name awards him liberation. The holy name of Krsna is the highest nectar in my life and my only treasure.” (Brhad-Bhagavatamrta 1.1.9)**

Sri Rupa Prabhu says:

*nikhila-sruti-mauli-ratna-mala-*  
*dyuti-nirajita-pada-pankajanta*  
*ayi mukta-kulair upasyamanam*  
*paritas tvam hari-nama samsrayami*

**“O holy name, the tips of the toes of your lotus feet is eternally worshiped by the glowing effulgence of the Upanisads, the crest jewels of the Vedas. You are eternally adored and chanted by great liberated souls like Narada and Sukadeva Gosvami. O Harinama, clearing myself of all offenses, I take complete shelter of You.” (Krsna-namastakam 1)**

Sri Rupa-Sanatana, Sri Raghunatha, and others worship in the mode of pure goodness - the mode of worship of the maha-bhagavatas. This mode is not based on the kind of mundane faith expressed by kanistha-adhikaris. Rather, it is an expression of service in the mood of ecstatic love; it is direct seva. Srila Raghunatha Dasa Gosvami does not worship the gunja-mala and Govardhan-sila Mahaprabhu gave him in a mood of awe and reverence; he performs direct, intimate service to Gandharva-Giridhari on the exalted path of raga.

In Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Srila Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami specifically shows the mode of puja offered by the Deity worshiper, Sri Gunarnava Misra \[see Adi 5.168\], and how Raghunatha Dasa Gosvami worships the Govardhana-sila and gunja-mala he was given by Mahaprabhu. Srila Raghunatha Prabhu is the spiritual master of all knowers of Brahman and is supremely dear to Sri Svarupa, Rupa, and Gauranga. The brahmana Gunarnava Misra’s Deity worship is performed with the faith of a kanistha-adhikari, whereas the Govardhana-sila worship of the exalted personality, the great follower of Svarupa and Rupa, most dear to Gaura, and the spiritual master of all brahmanas, Srila Raghunatha Dasa Gosvami, is on the platform of pure goodness and is directly loving, intimate seva to Radha and Krsna.

**Kusala Singh**: Is there a difference between arcana and bhajana, or between puja and seva?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Not understanding the distinction between arcana and bhajana and puja and seva, many people equate them. Although bhajana is based on the nine processes of devotional service, and because arcana is included in those nine processes, arcana is accepted as a limb of bhajana. But one cannot equate a part of bhajana with full bhajana. Arcana is related to worship in a mood of awe and reverence. When one serves the Lord with great respect and the proper ingredients but with a mundane understanding, it is called arcana. The brilliant sunrays of awe and reverence are dim in comparison to the service that is offered with love and affection, so no one can deny the superiority of the sweetness of the cooling, soothing, sublime moon rays of the intimate seva performed in bhajana. In arcana, the relationship between the gross and subtle body is more or less entangled with one’s service.

In the kingdom of bhajana, the spirit soul, who is beyond the gross and subtle body, is directly engaged in the Lord’s service. The sense activities of those who perform bhajana and who are free from all bodily designations are not mundane. Rather, they are situated beyond the influence of time because of their direct proximity with the One without a second, who is beyond the jurisdiction of material thought. These devotees have spiritual senses, which are engaged in the service of the master of the senses. Only when liberated, akincana persons, fully devoted to worshiping the Supreme Lord, are free of association with those who have desires unrelated to Krsna, does their propensity for astakaliya-seva, or seva throughout the day and night, become awakened. Only the Vaisnavas can worship Krsna at all times, under all circumstances, and with unalloyed faith. In the material concept of life, constant bhajana is not possible. Only the self-realized, fully devoted Vaisnavas engage in astakaliya-seva, or constant service to Krsna.

**Kusala Singh**: Then, is it not proper for people like us, who are attached to the material body, to remember the Lord’s pastimes?

**Srila Prabhupada**: One serves the transcendental pastimes with one’s spiritual senses; they are not accessible to those who are attached to the body - who are full of anarthas. When one fails to understand the nature of Sadhana and siddha, or the living being’s perfection, due to being mistaken or illusioned, one ends up becoming a mundane sahajiya. Only liberated raganugis are qualified to hear transcendental rasa-lila or other such pastimes. Only those who are completely free of anarthas are eligible to meditate on Krsna’s pastimes. The waves of transcendental pastimes that flow in a heart illuminated by pure goodness, which lights the heart after it has surpassed all material thought, can never be imagined through mundane contemplation.

Those who try to convert the soul’s pure spontaneous nature into something artificial and material, or who try to attain the natural, spontaneous, spiritual mood through artificial means, as followers of the ascending path imagine they can do, do not end up knowing the spiritual pastimes; instead, they come to know their opposite. Those who are in favor of remembering Krsna’s pastimes in some artificial manner arising from the empirical process are certainly considered imitative, mundane sahajiyas by raganuga Vaisnavas. Such sahajiyas think that Krsna’s pastimes, which should be served with transcendental consciousness, a commodity for their enjoyment. They therefore misinterpret phrases like tat paratvena nirmalam \[which indicates that one should serve the Supreme Lord uncontaminated by karma and jnana\] and tat-paro bhavet \[which indicates that one who hears about the Lord’s pastimes becomes attracted to the Lord\]. Instead, they pollute transcendence with matter. It is a form of sense gratification to abstract wrong meanings from phrases like tadrsi krida. But transcendental rati is the proper and principal meaning of tadrsi. Those who are greedy to satisfy their material lust think the taste attained by practicing raga synonymous with the taste sense enjoyers full of anarthas and little qualification get from the regulative path. Such people, instead of attaining knowledge of their relationship with the Supreme Lord \[sambandha\] remain in their world of sense enjoyment, absorbed in material conceptions. Giving up sadhana-bhakti, they consider Krsna’s transcendental pastimes, such as the rasa-lila, to be the ideal of material enjoyment - something they themselves might perform - and thus cheat themselves into thinking that by hearing and singing about such topics their material lust will be vanquished. In order to stop this self-deceit, the speaker of Srimad-Bhagavatam, Sri Sukadeva, used the word Sraddha, and Mahaprabhu used the word visvasa. Sri Sukadeva Maharaja says in Srimad-Bhagavatam (10.33.30):

*naitat samacarej jatu manasapi hy anisvarah*  
*vinasyaty acaran maudhyad yatharudro ’bdhi-jam visam*

**“One who is not a great controller should never imitate the behavior of ruling personalities, even mentally. If out of foolishness an ordinary person does imitate such behavior, he will simply destroy himself, just as a person who is not Rudra would destroy himself if he tried to drink an ocean of poison.”**

**Kusala Singh**: What is the Siddhanta of Sri Sanatana, Sri Rupa, and other gosvamis, and how did they behave in accordance with that Siddhanta?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Sri Brhad-Bhagavatamrta (2.3.144) states:

*param srimat-padambhoja-sada-sangaty-apeksaya*  
*nama-sankirtana-prayam visuddham bhaktim acara*

**“If you really care for Sri Krsna’s lotus feet, then execute pure devotional service that is free from karma, jnana, and other mixtures, and is mainly based on chanting the holy name of the Lord.”**

Furthermore, Sri Brhad-Bhagavatamrta 2.1.21 states:

*ye sarva-nairapeksyena radha-dasyecchavah param*  
*sankirtayanti tan-nama tadrsa-priyatamayah*

**“Only those who have no concern for any type of Sadhana or sadhya, and whose only desire is to become a servant of Sriman Madana Gopaladeva’s most dear Sri Radha, can loudly and offenselessly chant the holy name of the enjoyer of the rasa dance, being controlled by extraordinary, supremely elevated, indescribable, spontaneous love of God.”**

In this statement Sri Sanatana Gosvami reveals the symptoms of those who desire to serve Sri Radha’s lotus feet. In other words, these persons constantly chant the holy names.

## Conversation 3

**Srila Prabhupada and Pandita Syamasundara Cakravarty**  
**January 11, 1928**

\[The editor of the periodical English magazine Servant, the renowned Pandita Syamasundara Chakravarty Mahasaya, arrived at Sri Gaudiya Matha at 1 Ultadanga Junction Road to hear Sri Prabhupada’s lecture. Pandita Syamasundara was brought to Srila Prabhupada’s bhajana room. He said, “Today I am very happy to have your darsana. I have heard your name for a long time, but until now I did not have the good fortune to meet you.”\]

**Srila Prabhupada**: I am an extremely poor and fallen person. You have done so much work for the country.

**Pandita**: Nothing tangible. Now I feel I have surely been walking on the wrong path. I cannot stand on any fixed conclusion. The sand is always shifting under my feet.

**Srila Prabhupada:** We are glad to hear such simple and innocent words from the mouth of an expert and experienced person like you.

**Pandita**: During our school days, we heard lectures of your Thakura \[Srila Bhaktivinoda\] a few times. He used to preach during Krsna-prasanna Sen’s time.

**Srila Prabhupada**: Sen Mahasaya was much younger than Srimad Bhaktsivinoda Thakura.

**Pandita**: That’s when your Thakura used to preach?

**Srila Prabhupada**: He began to preach much earlier than that.

**Pandita**: When was this Gaudiya Matha established?

**Srila Prabhupada**: About nine or ten years ago. Its origin is Sri Caitanya Matha in Sridhama Mayapur. By Sri Caitanyadeva’s will, the matha has opened branches in various places, such as Kasi, Naimisaranya, Kuruksetra, and so on.

**Pandita**: Where is Naimisaranya?

**Srila Prabhupada**: It is in the district of Sitapur. The Awadh and Rohilkhanda railways go to Balamau Junction via Lucknow. From Balamau Junction to the Sitapur branch line you find this Nimsara station.

**Pandita**: The other day I had a discussion on scriptural topics with Mahamahopadhyaya Laksmana Sastri.

**Srila Prabhupada**: There is gulf of difference between the ideas of the Naimisaranya School and those of the Benaras School. The followers of the Naimisaranya School are genuine Vedantists; they accept the natural commentary on the Brahma Sutra and reject any concocted or artificial commentaries.

**Pandita:** What is the natural commentary on the Brahma Sutra?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Srimad-Bhagavatam is the only natural commentary on the Brahma Sutra.

**Pandita:** Don’t the scholars of the Benaras School accept Srimad-Bhagavatam?

**Srila Prabhupada:** They treat Srimad-Bhagavatam as one of many books, one of many Puranas. They do not wholeheartedly take shelter of Srimad-Bhagavatam. Vedantists think there is no need for any literature other than Srimad-Bhagavatam. We only accept books that speak in favor of Srimad-Bhagavatam, and we don’t consider religion any ideas opposed to Srimad-Bhagavatam.

**Pandita:** Is there opposition to Srimad-Bhagavatam?

**Srila Prabhupada:** There is nothing in this world but opposition to Srimad-Bhagavatam. Every independent idea of every so-called independent living entity averse to God from time immemorial is against the Bhagavatam’s principles.

**Pandita:** But are there any persons who have openly opposed Srimad-Bhagavatam?

**Srila Prabhupada:** We can find examples of persons opposed to the principles of Srimad-Bhagavatam as far back as Satya-yuga. Madhu, Kaitabha, Hayagriva, Hiranyaksa, Hiranyakasipu were all against the principles taught in the Bhagavatam.

There are two types of people who oppose Srimad-Bhagavatam: those who hide their opposition and those who are open about it. Covered opposers are more dangerous than open opposers. The founder of the Brahma-samaj, Rama Mohan Roy, the founder of the Arya Samaj, Dayananda, and Kaviraja Gangadhara Sen - these all presented clear and open opposition to the principles of Srimad-Bhagavatam.

In the impersonal philosophy established by the Benaras School we see covered opposition to the principles of Srimad-Bhagavatam. Sri Caitanyadeva exposed the supremacy of the Naimisaranya School’s philosophy to the then leader of the Benaras School, Prakasananda Sarasvati, in front of his sixty thousand disciples. Prakasananda had come to the wrong conclusion through the Benaras School’s philosophy, so after hearing from Caitanyadeva he immediately joined the Naimisaranya School.

**Pandita**: Can’t truth be found in any school other than the Naimisaranya School?

**Srila Prabhupada**: In other schools, or philosophies, truth is mixed with illusion, but at the very beginning of the Naimisaranya School’s commentary on Vedanta it is said, dhamna svena sada nirasta-kuhakam satyam param dhimahi: “I meditate on Sri Krsna, who is eternally existent in the transcendental abode, which is forever free from the illusory representations of the material world.” Adherents of the Naimisaranya School meditate on the Absolute Truth, free from any kind of cheating.

The word dhimahi is plural, and it indicates Vedantists, beginning with the son of Sri Vyasadeva: there are many meditators, the Absolute Truth is one, and the act of meditation is eternal. The word dhyana does not refer to a godless, conditioned human being’s independent thought in the form of restless mental speculation. Rather, the Supreme Absolute Truth is factual, inconceivable, and transcendental to the material senses.

**Pandita**: How can an object upon which one meditates be “inconceivable”?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Our previous acarya Sri Rupa Gosvami writes in the Bhakti-rasamrta-sindhu:

*vyatitya bhavana-vartma yas camatkrti-bhara-bhuh*  
*hrdi sattvojjvale badham svadate sa raso matah*

**“That which is beyond the imagination, laden with wonder, and relished in the heart that shines with goodness is known as rasa.”**

Only those situated in the mode of pure goodness can actually meditate on the Absolute Truth, Vasudeva. The modes of passion and ignorance, or even mixtures of the two, do not create pure goodness, because pure goodness is not a thing of this material world.

*sattvam visuddham vasudeva-sabditam*  
*yad iyate tatra puman apavrtah*  
*sattve ca tasmin bhagavan vasudevo*  
*hy adhoksajo me manasa vidhiyate*

**“I am always engaged in offering obeisances to Lord Vasudeva in pure Krsna consciousness. Krsna consciousness is always pure consciousness, in which the Supreme Personality of Godhead, known as Vasudeva, is revealed without any covering.” (Srimad Bhagavatam 4.3.23)**

The word adhoksaja means the Supreme Lord, who is beyond the reach of the material senses. Godhead is He who has reserved the absolute right to not be exposed to the mundane human senses.

**Pandita**: If God is only like this, why is the word manasa, used?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Srimad-Bhagavatam (1.7.4) states:

*bhakti-yogena manasi samyak pranihite ’male*  
*apasyat purusam purnam mayam ca tad-apasrayam*

**“Thus he fixed his mind, perfectly engaging it by linking it in devotional service (bhakti-yoga) without any tinge of materialism, and thus he saw the Absolute Personality of Godhead along with His external energy, which was under full control.”**

The hearts of ordinary people are bound by the mind’s tendency for sankalpa-vikalpa. But the hearts of those who are constantly engaged in serving Krsna, having given up the mentality of both an enjoyer and one who renounces enjoyment, are places in which the Lord’s pastimes manifest. We call this the pure mind. This is why Sri Gaurasundara says:

*anyera hrdaya—mana, mora mana—vrndavana,*  
*‘mane’ ‘vane’ eka kari’ jani*  
*tahan tomara pada-dvaya, karaha yadi udaya,*  
*tabe tomara purna krpa mani*

**\[Speaking in the mood of Srimati Radharani, Caitanya Mahaprabhu said,\] “For most people, the mind and heart are one, but because My mind is never separated from Vrndavana, I consider My mind and Vrndavana to be one. My mind is already Vrndavana, and since You like Vrndavana, will You please place Your lotus feet there? I would deem that Your full mercy.” (Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Madhya 13.137)**

Our predecessor acarya Sri Thakura Mahasaya also says:

*visaya chadiya kabe suddha habe mana*  
*kabe hama heraba sri-vrndavana*

**“When will my mind be freed from the contamination of material enjoyment and I shall be purified? Then it will be possible for me to understand Vrndavana.”**

Form, taste, smell, sound, and touch are called visaya \[sense objects\]. That mind that thinks itself the enjoyer of these is materially attached and impure. Such a mind never realizes the Absolute Truth. The eternal reciprocation of service between the eternally worshipable complete sac-cid-ananda and the eternal worshiper, the minute sac-cid-ananda, is what cleanses the contamination from a restless mind and, as the heart fills with devotion, that same mood of service brings about samadhi, or trance. The service-inclined heart depends neither on sense objects nor the renunciation of them \[to perceive the Lord\]. Rather, it perceives the Supreme Lord with the transcendental vision born naturally of its eternal serving propensity.

On the bank of the river Sarasvati, in a place call Samyaprasa, at Badarikasrama, Sri Vyasa, following Sri Narada’s instructions, could see in his heart purified by pure devotional service the Supreme Lord Krsna with His spiritual potency, the external energy, Mayadevi, who is dependent on Him. He could also see how the living entities, by nature pure spirit souls, servants of Krsna, could free themselves of the anarthas they have created when they began to consider themselves enjoyers of matter. Devotional service rendered to the transcendental personality, Sri Krsna, he realized, is the process that can free them. The purusa purnam referred to in the Bhagavatam is the omnipotent Supreme Lord only. You cannot see the omnipotent Supreme Lord by cultivating karma or jnana. Karma gives us only what we are destined to achieve by our particular action. We cannot obtain anything more or beyond that. If we cultivate jnana, or knowledge of the impersonal, we cannot expect to see the omnipotent Supreme Lord, because with that understanding, the seer, that which we are seeing, and the act of seeing itself is attacked. In each of the four types of liberation (salokya, etc.), there is a relationship between the servant and the object of service, but in the fifth, sayujya, there is no such relationship.

The Bhagavad-gita (18.55) says:

*bhaktya mam abhijanati yavan yas casmi tattvatah*  
*tato mam tattvato jnatva visate tad-anantaram*

**“One can understand the Supreme Personality as He is only by devotional service. And when one is in full consciousness of the Supreme Lord by such devotion, he can enter into the kingdom of God.”**

**Pandita**: What is maya?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Miyate anaya iti maya - maya is that which can be measured. The Supreme Lord, who cannot be measured, controls that which can be measured, maya. If something can be measured, it is maya, not the Supreme Lord. Ma+ya = maya, or “that which is not.” In Christian theology, the Godhead and Satan are separate. The maya referred to in Srimad-Bhagavatam is not like that. In the opinion of the Bhagavata school, Mayadevi is situated in the omnipotent Supreme Lord in a condemned state to award punishment to the minute spirit souls who are fit to come under her control.

The Bhagavad-gita (7.4—5) states:

*bhumir apo ’nalo vayuh kham mano buddhir eva ca*  
*ahankara itiyam me bhinna prakrtir astadha*  
*apareyam itas tv anyaih prakrtim viddhi me param*  
*jiva-bhutam maha-baho yayedam dharyate jagat*

**“Earth, water, fire, air, ether, mind, intelligence, and false ego – all together these eight constitute My separated material energies. Besides these, O mighty-armed Arjuna, there is another, superior energy of Mine, which comprises the living entities who are exploiting the resources of this material, inferior nature.”**

The inferior energy is maya. Sometimes in the form of godless Kapila’s “philosophy of twenty-four elements” \[Sankhya\], sometimes as Vaisesika’s atomism, sometimes as Jaimini’s Karma-mimamsa (“material prosperity”), sometimes as Gautama’s “philosophy of sixteen elements,” sometimes as Patanjali’s “opulence of liberation \[through yoga\],” and sometimes on the pretext of “searching after Brahman,” this material energy misleads, misguides, and bewilders the living entities who have been averse to God since time immemorial. Mayadevi actually makes them misunderstand things.

**Pandita**: Why does she do that?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Because the living entity has free will and is choosing to misuse it.

**Pandita:** The Bhagavad-gita (18.61) states:

*isvarah sarva-bhutanam hrd-dese ’rjuna tisthati*  
*bhramayan sarva-bhutani yantrarudhani mayaya*

**“The Supreme Lord is situated in everyone’s heart, O Arjuna, and is directing the wandering of all living entities who are seated as on a machine, made of the material energy”**

How do we understand this statement?

**Srila Prabhupada:** This Bhagavad-gita statement says the following: Lord Visnu alone controls and directs all living entities. Whatever action the living entities perform, the Supreme Lord awards them accordingly. It is by the Lord’s inspiration that the living entity’s propensity to act according to their previous karma becomes active. The living entity is the doer, and the Supreme Lord the director. The Supreme Lord’s authority as director is present in every act jivas perform; that direction takes the form of the Lord making the jivas enjoy the fruits of their past and present activities, and preparing them to perform future activities based on their continued conviction that they are the prime actors. The Supreme Lord bestows results, and the living entity enjoys those results.

**Pandita:** Why are the living entities given independence in the first place?

**Srila Prabhupada:** The living entity is a minute particle of the super-conscious Supreme Lord. The qualities present in ocean water are present in each drop of that water, although in minute quantity. Since the supremely cognizant Lord is supremely independent, the living entities also have minute independence.

**Pandita:** Does the Lord inspires how living entities use or misuse their minute independence?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Had how the jivas choose to use their independence been up to the Lord’s inspiration, the jivas would be serving God instead of forgetting Him.

**Pandita:** Then how can we understand that everything depends on the Supreme Lord’s will and yet the living entities are free to exercise their independence? I am not asking you these questions just for the sake of argument; you are a learned scholar and a great devotee, and so I ask you these questions. Once, I read a statement by Tukarama, which was quoted in Tilaka’s Hindi edition of the Bhagavad-gita. The purport of the statement was “O Lord! If my karma is going to deliver me, then what use are You?”

**Srila Prabhupada**: This is answered in Srimad-Bhagavatam (10.14.8):

*tat te ’nukampam susamiksamano*  
*bhunjana evatma-krtam vipakam*  
*hrd-vag-vapurbhir vidadhan namas te*  
*jiveta yo mukti-pade sa daya-bhak*

**“My dear Lord, one who constantly waits for Your causeless mercy to be bestowed upon him and who goes on suffering the reactions of his past misdeeds, offering You respectful obeisances from the core of his heart, is surely eligible for liberation, for it has become his rightful claim.”**

Only one who still thinks he has time before he retires from the material world thinks he will be safe by somehow dumping his faults on the most auspicious Supreme Lord. But such a person can never become liberated, because he has no service mood. Only those who, because of their transcendental inclination to serve the Supreme, accept all inconveniences as His mercy become attracted to Him and are therefore eligible for liberation.

**Pandita**: Can we say, then, that the sin we commit is also a form of mercy coming from the Lord?

**Srila Prabhupada**: No. Being averse to Hari, we misuse our independence and thereby open ourselves to committing sin, hoping to enjoy the fruits of our actions. For example, when the grain ceremony is performed for a baby, the parents place money, rice paddy, and a copy of Srimad-Bhagavatam in front of the child in order to ascertain its future proclivities, and the child grasps whatever it likes. An acarya, too, tries to ascertain the mentality of a disciple before he invests him with the sacred thread. The understanding of those who think God merciless is averse to devotion. If we take whatever happens to us only as punishment from the Lord, it shows our lack of serving temperament or even attraction for God.

Actually, the Supreme Lord is our shelter. He tests the sincerity of those who approach Him in hopes of achieving His shelter by placing many obstacles before them. For example, when we go to a doctor, he prescribes medicine and diet to cure our disease. He also lances our boils. If we become angry with the doctor because of that, or we try to beat him, or we say that he is merciless and not our benefactor, then we are wrong. We would be mistaking a real benefactor, a person of mercy, for someone who is harmful or cruel.

The Lord’s illusory energy, Mayadevi, places all kinds of tempting items before us. There are many kinds of bait; there are hooks, traps, nets, and chains. All these are meant to entangle us in the network of illusion. We are tempted by such things, and then hooked by them, and so we sometimes become pious workers (karmis) in the name of doing good for others or search after impersonal Brahman. These traps lead us to sometimes appreciate the atheistic philosophies of Buddha, Kapila, Sankaracarya, or others.

Mayadevi has traps for those who are marching forward in their religious understanding deceived by the two ulterior motives of karma and jnana. She realizes their mentalities and qualifications. Living entities are spiritually benefited only by discussing bhagavata-katha. There is no other way to find one’s spiritual fortune than by chanting such katha. The Supreme Lord does not interfere with any jiva’s independence. He does not kill one’s spiritual propensity or constitutional nature. Had He interfered in the characteristics of spirit - our natural serving propensity and our relationship with Him - it would have proved Him merciless. Instead, He simply discloses the facts about the temporary and eternal functions of the spiritual nature to the spiritual nature.

As Sri Caitanyadeva, He instructs us not to become involved in the idea of material prosperity as preached by the sage Jaimini or to search after the impersonal Brahman as preached by Dattatreya, Sankara, and others. That is not the proper use of our spiritual nature or our free will. “Act only to serve God; do not act in a way that will displease God”: Caitanyadeva says these things to benefit the living entities lost in the bodily concept of life in order to revive their spiritual consciousness.

Nobody works induced by a desire to suffer. A grief-stricken mother cries for her dead child, beating her breast and smashing her head against a wall in order to destroy her suffering. A patient vomits voluntarily by inserting a linger into his throat just so he can get instant relief from his illness. The fruitive workers, who desire to enjoy the fruits of karma, are also trying for quick relief from their suffering through various means. The inner desire of all karmis is to get instantaneous relief from pain, and so they rush toward any illusory mirage, duped by something that appears pleasing to them.

What gives them instantaneous relief? The idea that “I will become the emperor of this world! I will become Indra of the heavens! I will enjoy! And I will bestow much happiness on this world,” and so on. But this is just aversion to God. It is godlessness. And searching after the impersonal Brahman is the other side of the endeavor to secure instant relief from pain. We need some fees in some shape or other. We are part and parcel of the Godhead. The moment we think ourselves disassociated from Him, we run to enjoy life independently.

Then we think we need to use our canine teeth \[to eat flesh\] or we need sex or we need to bring other people into civilized order - all these come from forgetfulness of God and are symptoms of the propensity to enjoy the world. The Bhagavad-gita (3.27) states:

*prakrteh kriyamanani gunaih karmani sarvasah*  
*ahankara-vimudhatma kartaham iti manyate*

**“The spirit soul bewildered by the influence of false ego thinks himself the doer of activities that are in actuality carried out by the three modes of material nature.”**

The spirit soul is transcendental to matter and superior to Maya. He serves the Supreme Lord. But Maya is much more powerful. The aptitude and inclination of the living entity averse to serving Krsna is to become entangled with Mayadevi - to take the bait in her trap the way a fish is caught. The caught jiva collects and saves fuel with so much hardship, even at the risk of ruining his invaluable life providing enjoyment for his wife, children, grandsons, great grandsons, great great grandsons – most of whom he will never even meet. I may plant a palm tree, but others, whom I will never meet, will enjoy the fruits. We work so much for those persons who will one day spend all our hard-earned money for their own sense gratification.

There is a verse in Sri Caitanya-caritamrta (Madhya 22.16) in this regard:

*kamadinam kati na katidha palita durnidesas*  
*tesam jata mayi na karuna na trapa nopasantih*  
*utsrjyaitan atha yadu-pate sampratam labdha-buddhis*  
*tvam ayatah saranam abhayam mam niyuhksvatma-dasye*

**“O my Lord, there is no limit to the unwanted orders of lusty desires. Although I have rendered them so much service, they have not shown any mercy upon me. I have not been ashamed to serve them, nor have I even desired to give them up. O my Lord, O head of the Yadu dynasty, recently, however, my intelligence has been awakened, and now I am giving them up. Due to transcendental intelligence, I now refuse to follow the unwanted orders of these desires. Now I come to You to surrender myself at Your fearless lotus feet. Kindly engage me in Your personal service and save me.”**

People who are interested in karma accept God indirectly, and the impersonalists desire to become God. But we do not keep any such ulterior or sinful motive. Our desire is that we may always remain shoe-carriers of the servants of Hari. There is a verse in Sri Padyavali:

*jnanavalambakah kecit kecit karmavalambaka*  
*vayam tu hari-dasanam pada-tranavalambakah*

**“Among religious-minded people, some follow the path of fruitive activities and some follow the path of mental speculation, but our only resort is to carry the shoes of Lord Hari’s servants.”**

We have no knowledge and intelligence of our own. As servants of the spiritual master, we repeat his words and message. We do not invent or propose anything new. We speak only what is favorable for attaining the Absolute Truth. When we first hear from the spiritual master, his ideas appear revolting. We may even dare to correct the spiritual master and make up his inadequacy with our empiricism. But the flow of thought of the external world cannot attack the object “guru.” He is able to keep such things millions of yojanas away. Because his position does not shift, he is “guru,” or the heaviest object.

In the beginning we think that because the spiritual master does know the reality of the external world or is unaware of things around us he is simply mean-minded or narrow-minded in his approach. We think, “Let me expand his narrow-mindedness by explaining to him the topics discussed by of the empiric kingdom.” Such a mentality is extremely sinful and belongs to the empiricistic school. Our spiritual master is not narrow-minded. Rather, my spiritual master is the servant of the Absolute Truth, not the servant of partial truth.

**Pandita:** What is anartha?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Anartha is that which is blocking us, separating us from our artha, or the ultimate goal of our life, Krsna. That thing that is blocking us is making us its slave.

**Pandita:** When will anartha be vanquished?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Only when we give up serving our material senses and turn our face toward the service of the master of the senses.

**Pandita:** How do you deline “serving the material senses”?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Whatever we can measure with our senses, whatever appears nice to our senses, whatever our senses consider beneficial or a duty to perform - these are all considered sensory activities. Smoking tobacco, planting trees \[as a pious activity\], serving animals, serving the community or country, the desire to be known as learned and intelligent, the desire to accumulate fame through the guise of a devotee - these are all serving the material senses. All the endeavors of the karmis, jnanis, yogis, and anyabhilasis are sense gratification and therefore adverse to serving Krsna.

**Pandita:** How can you say all these acts are adverse to serving Krsna?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Lokasyajanato vidvams cakre satvata samhitam: “But the mass of people do not know \[that the material miseries of the living entity, which are superfluous to him, can be directly mitigated by the linking process of devotional service\] and therefore the learned Vyasadeva compiled this Vedic literature, which is in relation to the Supreme Truth.” (Srimad-Bhagavatam 1.7.6) People do not know this, and no one is interested in it. Non devotees are always busy serving those things that are not Krsna or related to Krsna. To help such persons, the merciful incarnation Sri Vyasadeva compiled spiritual literature for their benefit. In this transcendental literature, he glorifies as the supreme religion of the living entities unadulterated service to the transcendental Personality of Godhead, performed by giving up all kinds of sense gratificatory activities.

**Pandita:** What is devotional service, bhakti?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Bhakti is the eternal natural propensity of the spirit soul. It is the only eternal and spontaneous constitutional nature of the living entity, the living entity’s actual position. There is no othernatural propensity, nature, or duty in the living entity’s constitution. All other so-called propensities are inferior and constitutionally unnatural to the living entity. Therefore they are the adverse to the living entity’s nature and are changeable and temporary. Performing devotional service removes the living entity’s lamentation, illusion, and fear. Fear, lamentation, illusion, and so on, come from absorption in matter, and absorption in matter is something other than absorption in Krsna and Krsna’s devotees. Srimad-Bhagavatam (3.9.6) states:

*tavad bhayam dravina-deha-suhrn-nimittam*  
*sokah sprha paribhavo vipulas ca lobhah*  
*tavan mamety asad-avagraha arti-mulam*  
*yavan na te ’nghrim abhayam pravrnita lokah*

**“O my Lord, the people of the world are embarrassed by all material anxieties. They are always afraid. They always try to protect wealth, body, and friends, they are filled with lamentation and unlawful desires and paraphernalia, and they avariciously base their undertakings on the perishable conceptions of ‘my’ and ‘mine.’ As long as they do not take shelter of Your safe lotus feet, they are full of such anxieties.”**

The desire to lord it over matter, which develops from the mentality of trying to measure everything with the senses, is opposed to bhakti. As a person who has worms in his intestines never becomes healthy no matter how nourishing his food, so one who has a propensity for karma or jnana finds the natural propensity of the soul obstructed.

**Pandita**: How does one develop devotional service to Krsna?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Simply by hearing krsna-katha with attention and a service attitude from non duplicitous devotees who worship the Supreme Lord and who have no duty other than to constantly glorify Krsna. Lord Visnu maintains the entire universe through the mode of goodness. He is Sri Krsna Caitanya because He inspires the whole world to become Krsna conscious. He is Visvambhara because He is knows about the visva, the universe, and enacts the pastime of maintaining it. Visvambhara accepted the renounced order to bring the unconscious living entities to God consciousness, but still we have not come to our senses. Other than selfless service to God no other endeavors are natural to the soul. On the pure spiritual platform there is no service to anarthas; we serve only God. One of our spiritual masters \[Srila Narottama dasa Thakura\] sings:

*gora pahun na bhajiya mainu*  
*prema-ratana-dhana helaya harainu*  
*adhane jatana kari dhana teyaginu*  
*apana karama-dose apani dubinu*  
*sat-sanga chadi’ kainu asate vilasa*  
*te-karane lagila je karma-bandha-phansa*  
*visaya-visama-visa satata khainu*  
*gaura-kirtana-rase magana na hainu*  
*kena va achaye prana ki sukha paiya*  
*narottama dasa kena na gela mariya*
