**“O Lord Gaura! I have never worshiped You, and thus my negligence has robbed me of the jewel of divine love. Although in need of this wealth, I still worked hard to reject the great opportunity I had to receive Your riches. Instead, I submerged myself in an ocean of sinful reactions. Avoiding the association of saintly persons, I sported merrily in the company of materialistic rascals. For that reason I have become strung up in the noose of my own fruitive activities. I perpetually drank the horrible poison of mundane sense gratification, having never absorbed myself in the divine mellows of the kirtana glorifying You.”**

Ksatriyas, Vaisyas, Sudras, and others are busy with external material affairs, but those who know Brahman are different. Their only business is to serve Lord Hari. Ksatriyas, Vaisyas, and others should therefore offer their work to help the brahmanas, because the only duty of every living entity is to engage in the Lord’s service.

**Pandita**: But people don’t seem to be interested in that.

**Srila Prabhupada**: It doesn’t mean many people will come. The number of postgraduates will be even less. Krsna says in the Bhagavad-gita (7.3):

*manusyanam sahasresu kascid yatati siddhaye*  
*yatatam api siddhanam kascin mam vetti tattvatah*

**“Out of many thousands among men, one may endeavor for perfection, and out of those who have achieved perfection, hardly one knows Me in truth.”**

In Sri Caitanya-caritamrta (Madhya 19.144-49) Sri Caitanyadeva says:

*tara madhye ‘sthavara’, ‘jangama’—dui bheda*  
*jangame tiryak-jala-sthalacara-vibheda*  
*tara madhye manusya-jati ati alpatara*  
*tara madhye mleccha, pulinda, bauddha, sabara*  
*veda-nistha-madhye ardheka veda ‘mukhe’ mane*  
*veda-nisiddha papa kare, dharma nahi gane*  
*dharmacari-madhye bahuta ‘karma-nistha’*  
*koti-karma-nistha-madhye eka ‘jnani’ srestha*  
*koti-jnani-madhye haya eka-jana ‘mukta’*  
*koti-mukta-madhye ‘durlabha’ eka krsna-bhakta*  
*krsna-bhakta—niskama, ataeva ‘santa’*  
*bhukti-mukti-siddhi-kami—sakali ‘asanta’*

**“The unlimited living entities can be divided into two different groups: those that can move and those that cannot move. Among living entities that can move are birds, aquatics, and animals. Although the living entities known as human beings are very small in quantity, that division may be still further subdivided, for there are many uncultured human beings like mlecchas, pulindas \[and other varieties of outcasts\]. Among human beings, those who are followers of the Vedic principles are considered civilized. Among these, almost half simply give lip service to the Vedic principles while committing all kinds of sinful activities. Such men do not care for the prohibitions of scripture. Among the followers of the Vedas most are following the process of karma, distinguishing between good and bad work. Out of many such sincere karmis, there may be one jnani, who is actually wise. Out of many millions of such wise men, one may become liberated. And out of many of such liberated persons, a pure devotee of Krsna is very dfficult to find. Because a devotee of Lord Krsna is desireless, he is peaceful. Fruitive workers desire material enjoyment, jnanis desire liberation, and yogis desire material opulence; therefore they are all lusty and cannot be peaceful.”**

Srimad-Bhagavatam (6.14.5) states:

*muktanam api siddhanam narayana-parayanah*  
*sudurlabhah prasantatma kotisv api maha-mune*

**“O great sage, among many millions who are liberated and perfectin knowledge of liberation, one may be a devotee of Lord Narayana, orKrsna. Such devotees, who are fully peaceful, are extremely rare.”**

Sri Caitanya-caritamrta (Madhya 19.151) also states:

*brahmanda bhramite kona bhagyavan jiva*  
*guru-krsna-prasade paya bhakti-lata-bija*

**“Out of all living entities wandering throughout the entire universe, one who is fortunate gets an opportunity to associate with a bona fide spiritual master by the grace of Krishna. By the mercy of both Krishna and the spiritual master, such a person receives the seed of the creeper of devotional service.”**

Duplicity, hypocrisy, is the main characteristic of the external world. The brahminical nature is superior to the ksatriya nature. The word brahma means “great,” “broad,” “expansive.” Ksatriya principles are more or less narrow-minded, as are the principles of vaisyas and sudras. Suren Babu finally descended from brahmana principles to sudra principles, but unadulterated Brahminical policy is Vaisnava dharma. The understandings of those who are forgetful of Krsna are different from those of the Vaisnavas.

**Pandita:** How does the Vaisnava religion benefit the world?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Even if one tries for thousands of yugas through politics, one will not be able to accomplish one millionth the portion of the benefit Vaisnavas offer the world. We are not advocating that we become narrow-minded like politicians.

**Pandita:** How many people know about Vaisnava dharma?

**Srila Prabhupada:** How many people becomes postgraduates? How many people become Newtons? Since not many are becoming J. C. Boses, we should stop talking about science - is that a proper response?

**Pandita:** Vaisnava dharma may benefit some individuals, but how does it actually benefit the world?

**Srila Prabhupada:** It’s not like that. Those who perform arcana and who do not apply themselves to performing kirtana think like that. Those who worship a deity benefit only themselves, whereas those who perform kirtana benefit the whole world - the whole universe – including the beasts, birds, demigods, human beings, and even the trees, creepers, and stones.

**Pandita:** Is Vaisnava dharma then meant for everyone?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Vaisnava dharma is the only religion for all spiritual beings. It is the only dharma that is natural to the living entities’ constitutional position. There is no need to become a Christian or a Muslim or a Hindu. Simply become a Vaisnava. That’s all. There is no need even to become a tree, a bird, an animal, a stone, a demigod, a demon, or a human being. Simply become a Vaisnava and follow your constitutional dharma.

This is what Mahaprabu did. When He traveled throughout South India He chanted the holy name loudly, and all who saw Him became Vaisnavas. When He traveled through the Jharikhanda jungle, the trees, creepers, grass, animals, birds, and stones could no longer preserve their respective conditioned states of being. Rather, they all became Vaisnavas. The Saivites, the Durga worshipers, the atheistic Hindus, the Pathanas, the Buddhists, the Mayavadis, and all the people He met who wanted liberation or material enjoyment, the yogis, the ascetics, the learned, the foolish, the healthy, the sick - all became Vaisnavas. Mahaprabhu’s only “weapon” was krsna-kirtana. And those who became Vaisnavas in turn, by Mahaprabhu’s order, acted as gurus and converted everyone they met in all directions to Vaisnava dharma.

We read in Sri Caitanya-caritamrta (Madhya 7.128) how as He traveled, Mahaprabu instructed everyone:

*yare dekha, tare kaha ‘ krsna’-upadesa*  
*amara ajnaya guru hana tara ’ ei desa*

**“Instruct whoever you meet in the science of Krsna. Teach them theinstructions of Krsna in the Bhagavad-gita, and the teachings about Krsna in Srimad Bhagavatam. In this way, on my order, become a guru and liberate everyone in the land.”**

Sri Caitanya-caritamrta (Adi 9.41) states:

*bharata-bumite haila manusya-janma yara*  
*janma sarthaka kari ’ kara para-upakara*

**“One who has taken his birth as a human being in the land of India should make his life successful and work for the benefit of all other people.”**

And Sri Caitanya-bhagavata (Adi 16.289-90) states:

*keha apanare matra karaye posana*  
*keha va posana kare sahasreka jana*  
*duite ke bada, bhavi ’ bujhaha apane*  
*ei abhipraya guna ’ ucca-sahkirtane”*

**“One person may maintain himself while another may maintain a thousand people. Of the two, consider carefully who is better. This is the superior characteristic of loud chanting.”**

Again from Sri Caitanya-bhagavata (Adi 16.280-81):

*pasu-paksi-kita-adi halite na pare*  
*sunilei hari-nama ta’ra saba tare’*  
*japile sri-krsna-nama apane se tare*  
*ucca-sankirtane para upakara kare*

**“The animals, birds, and insects cannot chant the holy name, but by hearing the holy name chanted they can benefit. Chanting the japa of the holy name of Krsna purifies oneself, but the loud sankirtana of the holy name of Krsna benefits all living beings.”**

There has never been, nor will there ever be, any greater benefactor than Mahaprabhu and His devotees. The pretention natural to all other welfare work is gravely harmful. The welfare work rendered by Mahaprabhu and His devotees is eternal, supreme welfare. This is not welfare offered for a few days; it is no temporary benefit. Nor is it a proposal of welfare that will cause harm after some time - not some kind of welfare that will inconvenience others while it benefits some.

For example, anything good done for my country will inevitably be bad for another country. I may be happy riding on a horse-drawn cart, but inevitably the horse is inconvenienced. My temporary happiness causes distress to another, and when others are happy, I may become distressed. Mahaprabhu and His devotees never cheated anyone by proposing such welfare. What they proposed and then gave was beneficial for all time and in all circumstances. Mahaprabhu’s welfare can be applied to all countries and to all people at all times. This welfare is not confined to any particular country or harmful to any other country. It is good for the whole universe.

Therefore, Mahaprabhu and His devotees never propose any narrow-minded, sectarian, temporary benefit. What Mahaprabhu offers never produces any ill effect in anyone, Therefore Mahaprabhu s mercy is called amandodaya, or “that compassion which does no harm.” Mahaprabhu is maha-vadanya, and His devotees are maha-maha-vadanya, These are not stories, not literary fictions; we are speaking about the highest truth, reality.

**Pandita:** What does it mean to serve Visnu?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Visnu is the transcendental object, and I cannot measure or enjoy Him with my senses. Visnu is that Absolute Truth by whom I am enjoyed. Serving His sense gratification is called seva, or service. To pretend to serve Visnu in order to earn one’s livelihood is not visnu-seva. Nowadays, there are people who are trying to enjoy Visnuiu the name of serving Him. River water, the fruit of trees, the beauty of nature, the cool breezes - the idea that all of these are meant for my enjoyment is a sample of the mentality of trying to enjoy Visnu. As if Visnu was our domestic cook and will have to do whatever we say. As if Visnu was our gardener. I want to smell fragrant flowers, so Visnu will have to prepare a bouquet and bring it to me.

Who are those who do not want to perform devotional service? Only those who say “I want to be king” or “I want to remain a subject”; “I want to farm” or “I want to be in politics or become a soldier” - these people do not want to perform devotional service.

**Pandita:** Do you mean that performing devotional service requires giving up all our other activities?

**Srila Prabhupada:** It means we will do everything as Vaisnavas. We will not adopt the path of fruitive activities while giving up the path of Vaisnavism. This is what our spiritual master Sri Rupa Gosvami says:

*iha yasya harer dasye karmana manasa gira*  
*nikhilasv apy avasthasu jivan-muktah sa ucyate*

**“A person who acts in the service of Krsna with his body, mind, intelligence, and words is a liberated person, even within the material world.”**

*anasaktasya visayan yatharham upayunjatah*  
*nirbandhah krsna-sambandhe yuktam vairagyam ucyate*

**“Relating everything in the world favorably to Krsna, as much as possible, without attachment, is called yukta-vairagya.**

**Pandita:** What is the duty of a Vaisnava?

**Srila Prabhupada:** The Narada Pancaratra states:

*laukiki vaidiki vapi ya kriya kriyate mime*  
*hari-sevanukulaiva sa karya bhaktim icchata*

**“One should perform only those activities - either worldly or those prescribed by Vedic rules and regulations - that are favorable for the cultivation of Krsna consciousness.”**

*surarse vihita sastre harim uddisya ya kriya*  
*saiva bhaktir iti prokta taya bhaktih para bhavet*

**“All the activities prescribed in the scriptures to please the Lord are called sadhana-bhakti, or upaye-bhakti. Through sadhana-bhakti, parabhakti or sadhya-bhakti (upeya-bhakti) is attained.”**

This cultivation is called inaction, or naiskarma. Whatever we do we must do in service to Hari. Salvationists desire to give up all activities, however, including activities done in service to Hari**.**

**Pandita:** How does one serve Hari?

**Srila Prabhupada:** One can serve Hari in three ways: by the actions of one’s body, mind, and speech.

**Pandita:** How can one serve with body, mind, and speech?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Snimad-Bhagavatam (7.5.23-24) answers this question:

*sravanam kirtanam visnoh smaranam pada-sevanam*  
*arcanam vandanam dasyam sakhyam atma-nivedanam*  
*iti pumsarpita visnau bhaktis cen nava-laksana*  
*kriyeta bhagavaty addha tan manye ’dhitam uttamam*

**“Hearing and chanting about the transcendental holy name, form, qualities, paraphernalia, and pastimes of Lord Visnu, remembering them, serving the lotus feet of the Lord, offering the Lord respectful worship with sixteen types of paraphernalia, offering prayers to the Lord, becoming His servant, considering the Lord one’s best friend, and surrendering everything unto Him - these nine processes are accepted as pure devotional service.**

One who has dedicated his life to the service of Krsna through these nine methods should be understood to be the most learned person, for he has acquired complete knowledge.”

After Hiranyakasipu heard from the mouth of his young son Prahlada how to serve Visnu, he was astonished and said, “You are saying some-thing new which we have never heard in Buddhism.”

**Pandita:** Shouldn’t those who serve Hari serve Him by serving His jivas?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Lord Hari is the complete whole, and His servants are the real servants of the living entities. Those who, being overwhelmed by the external appearance of the suffering living entities, consider service to Hari’s external limbs \[those suffering living entities\] service to Hari, are in illusion and as such cannot serve Hari. Rather, they end up serving Hari’s external limb, Mayadevi. We can neither benefit others nor ourselves by forever serving Maya. To think that Lord Narayana is a poor man is not service to Narayana or even service to Narayana’s servants, the living entities; it is only service to Mayadevi.

Serving illusion, serving the mirage, serving a shadow - these are not serving reality. Krsna alone is the Absolute Truth, and the living entities are His associated counterparts. We should serve Hari and Hari’s devotees. We should also serve those who are unable to understand Lord Hari by helping them understand Him. We serve such people intellectually and physically. We serve those who are envious of Hari by ignoring them. The Lord’s servants are our best friends; we should make friends with them. To those friends of mine who are not envious but who lack the power to understand devotional service - who act like ksatriyas, vaisyas, and sudras, and so on - I will preach about serving Lord Visnu. As for those who are envious or outcaste, who are agnostic or Epicurean, we will not cooperate with them.

**Pandita:** You said something about jiva daya, or showing compassion to all living beings. How do you show such compassion? Do you distribute food and clothing?

**Srila Prabhupada:** If anyone in this life or the next develops faith in God or worships Hari, then we will help him by providing food or clothing. We will feed and clothe poverty-stricken people to encourage them to worship. We will give them real, everlasting benefit. Otherwise, what is the use of nourishing a poisonous snake with milk and bananas? That is not compassion. Rather, it is guiding people to godlessness by entrapping or tempting them. Sri Caitanyadeva’s compassion is amandodaya, com-passion that does no harm.

Sri Ksanada-gita-cintamani states:

*heloddhulita-khedaya visadaya pronmilad-amodaya*  
*samyac-chastra-vivadaya rasadaya cittarpitonmadaya*  
*sasvad-bhakti-vinodaya samadaya madhurya-maryodaya*  
*sri-caitanya daya-nidhe tava daya bhayad amandodaya*

**“O ocean of mercy, Sn Caitanya Mahaprabhu! Let there be an awakening of Your auspicious mercy, which easily drives away all kinds of material lamentation. By Your mercy, everything is made pure and blissful. It awakens transcendental bliss and covers all gross material pleasures. By Your auspicious mercy, quarrels and disagreements arising from different scriptures are vanquished. Your auspicious mercy causes the heart to feel joy and pour forth transcendental mellows. Your mercy always stimulates devotional service, which is full of joy. You always glorify the conjugal love of God. May transcendental bliss be awakened in my heart by Your causeless mercy.”**

Sri Rupa Gosvami prays to Mahaprabhu as follows:

*namo maha-vadanyaya krsna-prema-pradaya te*  
*krsnaya krsna-caitanya-namne gaura-tvise namah*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto the Supreme Lord Sn Krsna Caitanya, who is more magnanimous than any other avatara, even Krsna Himself, because He is bestowing freely what no one else has ever given- pure love of Krsna.”**

Our Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami says:

*sri-krsna-caitanya-daya karaha vicara*  
*vicara karile citte pabe camatkara*

**“If you are indeed interested in logic and argument, kindly apply it to the mercy of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu. If you do so, you will find it to be strikingly wonderful.” \[Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Ad 1 8.15\]**

**Pandita:** What was that sloka - sri-krsna-caitanya-daya?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Kaviraja Gosvami Prabhu asked us to make a comparative study between Caitanyacandra’s mercy and all the other forms of so-called mercy, which are all incomplete. Wherever there is no eternal contribution to the welfare of others, there is bound to be inadequacy, defects, and cheating. If anyone does an impartial, comparative study, he will understand that Caitanyacandra’s mercy is complete, and all other incomplete forms of mercy are limited and full of duplicity. This is why Sri Kaviraja Gosvami appeals to everyone to make this study.

Matsya, Kurma, Varahadeva, and even Krsnacandra showed mercy only to Their devotees, but they annihilated Their opponents. By comparison, Mahaprabhu gave mercy to His opponents as well. Think, for example, of the Chand Kazi. He did not even hesitate to bestow His all-auspicious mercy on the Buddhists, and He converted the worshipers of Lord Rama known as the Ramayets into pure Vaisnavas.

**Pandita:** Are the Ramayets not Vaisnavas?

**Srila Prabhupada:** The followers of Ramanandi sampradaya are called the Ramayets. They do not exactly belong to the Ramanuja sampradaya. Since many of the Ramayets are aiming for liberation, pure Vaisnavas consider them pseudo Vaisnavas. Raghunatha Bhatta Gosvami Prabhu brought before Mahaprabhu in Pun one Rama yet Vaisnava by the name of Ramadasa, who taught the Kavya Prakasa. Although Ramadasa had humility, a service attitude toward Vaisnava brahmanas, and other such qualities, because he also had a desire for liberation in his heart, Mahaprabhu ignored him a little. Mahaprabhu teaches (in Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Madhya 19.176):

*bhukti-mukti-sprha yavat pisaci hrdi vartate*  
*tavad bhakti-sukhasyatra katham abhyudayo bhavet*

**“The material desire to enjoy the material world and the desire to be-come liberated from material bondage are considered to be two witches, and they haunt one like ghosts. As long as these witches remain within theheart, how can one feel transcendental bliss? As long as these two witches remain in the heart, there is no possibility of enjoying the transcendental bliss of devotional service.”**

And in Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Madhya 19.167:

*anyablnlasita-sunyam jhana-karmady-anavrtam*  
*anukulyena krsnanu-silanam bhaktir uttama*

**“To serve Krsna in a favorable mood by all the senses is called krsna-bhakti. Being free from all desires except the desire to increase devotion to Krsna, without worshiping any other personality or devata as another lord; being devoted to Krsna alone, giving up all practice of jnana and karma, and serving Krsna with all the senses in a favorable mood, is called pure devotional service.”**

**Pandita:** What do you think of the Buddhists?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Buddhist is actually another name for Vaisnava. Yet in the hearts of the Buddhists they lack knowledge of the characteristic or constitutional position of the Vaisnava. For example, Rama’s worshipers are called the Ramayets, Nrsimha’s worshipers the Narasimhis, Varaha’s worshipers the Varahis, and Krsna’s worshipers the Karsnas. Similarly, the worshipers of Visnu’s incarnation Buddha are called Buddhas or Buddhists. For example, although the Aula, Baula, Karta-bhaja, Neda, Daravesa, Sain, Sahajiya, Sakhibheki, Smarta, Jata-gosain atibadi, Gauranga-nagari, and other such sects accept Gauranga in name only and have fallen away from Gauranga’s actual teachings, so the Buddhists call themselves “worshipers of Buddha” but have fallen from Lord Buddha’s actual teachings. Instead, they are bewildered by Visnu’s illusory energy, maya.

When the Buddhists realize themselves as Vaisnavas and submit to the feet of pure Vaisnavas, they will understand their original constitutional position. The Buddhists who met Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu understood their constitutional position because they received Mahaprabhu’s mercy. We see evidence of this in Kaviraja Gosvamis writings. When unauthorized sampradayas like the Aula and Baula give up their respective materially designated dharmas and worship Gaura-Krsna under the direction of a pure Vaisnava, then we will accept them as devotees of Gaura.

**Pandita:** Do the smartas worship Visnu?

**Srila Prabhupada:** The smarta’s worship Visnu by worshiping Ganesa, Surya, Durga, and other devatas. But by doing so they fail to worship Lord Visnu as the Supreme Lord. To try to worship Visnu as one of five gods is to make Visnu’s unique position equal to that of the demigods- that is, to count Visnu as one of the demigods. There is no one greater or equal to Lord Visnu. Mahaprabhu says:

*yas tu narayanam devam brahma-rudradi-daivataih*  
*samatvenaiva vikseta sa pasandi bhaved dhruvam*

**“A person who considers demigods like Brahma and Siva to be on an equal level with Narayana is to be considered an offender and an atheist.”**

Srila Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami talked about atheistic Hindus in Sri Caitanya-caritcimrta (Adi 17.203). These atheists do not consider the holy name of Krsna to be the only goal of life and the only way to achieve the goal of life. They consider Krsna to be equal to the demigods and chanting the holy name of Krsna to be equal to inferior processes of sadhana, such as yoga, austerity, meditation, taking vratas, and so on. But Mahaprabhu says:

*koti asvamedha eka krsna nama sama*  
*yei kahe, se pasandi, dande tare yama*

**“One who says that ten million asvamedha sacrifices are equal to the chanting of the holy name of Lord Krsna is undoubtedly an atheist. He is sure to be punished by Yamaraja.” (Sri Caitanya Caritamrta Adi 3.79)**

All these topics are vividly described in the invaluable Brahma-samhita, which teaches the conclusion of Vaisnava understanding and which Mahaprabhu brought with Him from South India to give to the world. Although there is worship of Visnu as one of the five gods, Visnu is not satisfied. It is just like demigod worship and therefore illegal.

**Pandita:** Why do you say “illegal”?

**Srila Prabhupada:** The Supreme Lord Himself called this type of worship illegal in the Bhagavad-gita (9.23):

*ye ’py anya-devata-bhakta yajante sraddhayanvitah*  
*te ’pi mam eva kaunteya yajanty avidhi-purvakam*

**“Whatever a man may sacrifice to other gods, O son of Kunti, is really meant for Me alone, but it is offered without true understanding.”**

**Pandita:** Then even if it is illegal, it is still worship of Krsna.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Krsna alone is the undisputed emperor of the en-tire universe along with the two-storied Vaikuntha beyond the universe. No one can obstruct His enjoyment and everyone is \[ultimately\] worshiping Him. But if the worship is illegal or done without true understanding, then the worshiper gets no benefit. Yes, those who worship Suryadeva, Ganesa, Durga, and the other of the five gods are also worshiping Krsna, because no one has any separate existence from Krsna, but they are worshiping Krsna’s shadow potency. Thus they cannot become self-realized. Knowledge of their relationship with the Lord cannot manifest. The day they revive awareness of their relationship with the Lord is the day they come to know that Krsna is the only Lord and master and all living entities are His eternal servants and that serving Him is their eternal duty.

**Pandita:** You mentioned the Brahma-samhita. What did you say it contained?

**Srila Prabhupada:** The Brahma-samhita refutes five-god worship and propounds worship of the controller of all, Sri Krsna, as the eternal business of the living entities. All the demigods are Visnu’s servants. It is their duty to carry out the order of Govinda. Those who imagine the demigods as different names or forms of Visnu without knowing that the demigods are actually Lord Visnu’s servants can never become liberated.

The positions of the five gods are nicely described in these following five verses of Sri Brahma-samhita:

*yac-caksur esa savita sakala-grahanam*  
*raja samasta-sura-murtir aseya-tejah*  
*yasyajhaya bhramati sambhrta-kala-cakro*  
*govindam adi-purusam tam aham bhajami*

**“The sun, who is the king of all the planets, full of infinite effulgence, the image of the good soul, is the eye of this world. I adore the primeval Lord, Govinda, in pursuance of whose order the sun performs his journey, mounting the wheel of time.” (5.52)**

*yat-pada-pallava-yugam vinidhaya kumbha-*  
*dvandve pranama-samaye sa ganadhirajah*  
*vighnan vihantum alarn asya jagat-trayasya*  
*govindam adi-pwusam tam aham bhajami*

**“I worship Govinda, the primeval Lord, whose lotus feet are always held by Ganesa upon the pair of tumuli protruding from his elephant head so that he may obtain the power to perform his function of destroying all the obstacles on the path of progress of the three worlds.” (5.50)**

*srsti-sthiti-pralaya-sadhana-saktir eka*  
*chayeva yasya bhuvanani bibharti durga*  
*icchanurapam api yasya ca cestate sa*  
*govindam adi-purusam tam aham bhajami*

**“The external potency, Maya, who is of the nature of the shadow of the cit potency, is worshiped by all people as Durga, the creating, preserving, and destroying agency of this mundane world. I adore the primeval Lord, Govinda, in accordance with whose will Durga conducts herself.” (5.44)**

*ksiram yatha dadhi vikara-visesa-yogat*  
*sanjayate na hi tatalh prthag asti hetoh*  
*yah sambhutam api tatha samupaiti karyad*  
*govindam adi-purusam tam aham bhajami*

**“I worship Govinda, the primeval Lord. Just as milk changes into yogurtwhen mixed with a yogurt culture but is actually constitutionally nothingbut milk, so Govinda, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, assumes theform of Lord Siva for the special purpose of material transactions.” (5.45)**

*diparcir eva hi dasantaram abhyupetya*  
*dipayate vivrta-hetu-samana-dharma*  
*yas tadrg eva hi ca visnutaya vibhati*  
*govindam adi-purusam tam aham bhajami*

**“When the flame of one candle is expanded to another candle and placed in a different position, it burns separately, and its illumination is as powerful as the original candle’s. Similarly, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Govinda, expands Himself in different Visnu forms, who are all as luminous, powerful, and opulent as He. Let me worship that Supreme Personality of Godhead, Govinda.” (5.46)**

**Pandita:** What is the difference between Krsna and Visnu?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Whatever Krsna is, Visnu is also. Both Their forms are made of pure goodness. Visnu is equal to Krsna as His manifestation. Krsna is the origin and Visnu His plenary portion. Sixty of Krsna’s opulences are fully manifest in His vilasa-murti Narayana. In Sri Krsna, those sixty qualities - and four more extraordinary and unique qualities- are present in a wonderful way. Sri Krsna is svayam-rupa and the original lamp. From Him innumerable lamps in the form of Visnus have been illuminated. From the original lamp, Sri Krsna’s vilasa-murti emanates Maha-visnu, Karanodakasayi, Garbhodakasayi, Ksirodakasayi, and plenary portions like Lord Rama and other incarnations. They are all separate individual lamps.

**Pandita:** What is the difference between a Vaisnava and a brahmana?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Those who worship the personal form of Visnu are called Vaisnavas, and those who worship the impersonal form are called brahmanas. The three manifestations of the non dual knowledge (or substance) are called Brahman, Paramatma, and Visnu. One who knows Brahman is called a brahmana, and one who worships the Supreme Lord, and who therefore already knows Brahman, is called a Vaisnava. Bhagavan is the fully manifest Absolute Truth, and Brahman is the Absolute Truth in an incomplete manifestation. Therefore only brahmanas who are endowed with knowledge of their relationship with God and who therefore worship God can become Vaisnavas.

The five kinds of worship of Brahman the illusioned impersonalists imagine is not the full Absolute Truth. While trying to pose as knowers of Brahman, Mayavadis decide that brahmanism is confined only to material realization. But constitutionally within the living entities, knowledge of Brahman is eternally present. By the mercy of Visnu, if one is saved from the clutches of Mayavada thought, he becomes a pure brahmana, or a Vaisnava. In the Bhakti Sandarbha (177) Sn Jiva Gosvami Prabhu quotes Vyasadeva’s statement in this regard:

*brahmananam sahasrebhyah satra-yaji visiyate*  
*satra-yaji-sahasrebhyah sarva-vedanta-paragah*  
*sarva-vedanta-vit-kotya visnu-bhakto visisyate*  
*vaisnavanam sahasrebhya ekanty eko visisyate*

**“Out of many thousands of brahmanas, one who performs sacrifice for Visnu is best. Out of thousands of such yajnika-brahmanas, one who knows the meaning of Vedanta is best. But of millions of such Vedantists, adevotee of Visnu is best. And out of thousands of Visnu bhaktas, one whois an unalloyed devotee of Visnu is the best.” (Hari-bhakti-vilasa 10.117)**

**Pandita:** Are Vaisnavas also brahmanas?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Yes, Vaisnavas are also brahmanas - you just heard that in the verse I quoted. Being a brahmana is the lowest step on the ladder of becoming a Vaisnava. Vaisnavism is much greater than brahmanism. Brahmanas are servants of the Vaisnavas. For example, one who has got a lakh of rupees automatically also has one thousand rupees. In that same way, one who is a Vaisnava is already a brahmana. Brahmanism is included in Vaisnavism.

**Pandita:** At present, no one thinks like that. As soon as we speak about Vaisnavas, people think of something other than a brahmana.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Because people have forgotten all these things, and because the highest position, that of a Vaisnava, has demonstrated to be “abominable” by those who do not discuss, follow, or preach \[Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s actual teachings\], the Gaitanya Matha has come into existence, by the will of the Supreme Lord. Sri Gaudiya Matha wants to reestablish Vaisnava dharma for those human beings who have forgotten their brahminical qualities, who have forgotten that their duty is to serve the Vaisnavas, and who are rushing to live out the mundane propensities of ksatriyas, vaisyas, sudras, or something outside the varnasrama system. We want to reestablish the principle of transcendental varnasrama-dharma. That is why the Gaudiya Matha has come forward. The Gaudiya Matha wants to reestablish the true face of varnasrama culture. In Sri Caitanya-caritamrta (Madhya 8.128) Mahaprabhu says:

*kiba vipra, kiba nyasi, sudra kene naya*  
*yei krsna-tattva-vetta, sei ‘guru’ haya*

**“Whether one is a brahmana, a sannyasi, or a sudra - whatever he is - he can become a spiritual master if he knows the science of Krsna.”**

A non-brahmana can never become guru. Guru means brahmana. One who laments for material things or runs after material life is not guru. But sudra or sannyasi - if he knows the science of Krsna, he is brahmana, guru. One who knows the science of Krsna - in other words, one whois aware of the complete realization of the Absolute Truth - already has knowledge of Brahman within him. He is certainly not a non-brahmana \[because of his lower birth\].

This point is discussed in Srimad-Bhagavatam (7.11.35):

*yasya yal laksanam proktam pumso varpabhivyanjakam*  
*yad anyatrapi drsyeta tat tenaiva vinirdiset*

**“If one shows the symptoms of being a brahmana, ksatriya, vaisya, or sudra, as described above, even if he has appeared in a different class, he should be accepted according to those symptoms of classification.”**

In his commentary on this verse, Sridhara Svami writes, “The proper way to ascertain different varnas, such as whether one is a brahmana, is to observe the person’s qualities. Is he peaceful? The way a brahmana is ascertained by caste in the general way of things is not the only way to make that determination. To confirm this fact, Srimad-Bhagavatam has manifested this verse. If qualities like peacefulness are seen in a nonseminal brahmana or a person not known as a brahmana, then such a person should not be confined to his birth caste. Rather, his varna should be determined according to his qualities and symptoms. Otherwise, the determination will be wrong.”

When Advaita Acarya lived in Nadia, many pious brahmanas also lived there. There was no dearth of Misras, Cakravartis, and Bhattacaryas in Navadvipa. We find this stated plainly in Sri Caitanya-bhagavata. Yet Advaita Prabhu, who was the foremost of acaryas, did not find even asingle qualified brahmana to whom to offer the sraddha plate. Finally, He offered it to Thakura Haridasa, who appeared in a yavana family, and that is how he honored His ancestors. He told Haridasa, “If you eat, I will have fed millions of brahmanas.”

**Pandita:** But why at present do we not find such good behavior in your Vaisnava society?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Everything is lost by the influence of time. What-ever Mahaprabhu taught is now distorted. Nowadays, people are passing off as Vaisnava dharma religion, business, adultery, cheating, and deceiving people. Once, while speaking this truth, the author of Sri Caitanya-bhagavata, Thakura Vrndavana, manifested the pastime of being harassed. (What to speak of him, even Thakura Vrndavan’s worshipable

Lord Nityananda Prabhu, appeared to be harassed.) We find a hint of this in Thakura Vrndavana’s writings. He does not always describe everything elaborately, but often he gives some hints when he tells of different incidents. Because Nityananda Prabhu preached pure Vaisnava dharma, there was no shortage of people willing to criticize even Him. That is why every now and then Thakura Vrndavana writes, tabe lathi maron tara sirera up-are: “I kick on the head of such a person.”

One can just imagine how much harassment Lord Nityananda received for Thakura Vrndavana to write statements like raksasah kalim asritya \[“Demons, taking advantage of Kali-yuga, have taken birth in brahmana families”\] and sva-pakam iva nekseta \[“If a person born in a brahmana family is an avaisnava, a non devotee, one should not see his face, exactly as one should not look upon the face of a candala, or dog-eater”\] as he described the characteristics of Sri Caitanyadeva. Those people even leveled baseless accusations against Thakura Vrndavana. Even though Thakura Haridasa appeared in a yavana family, the Lord’s devotees respected him as the spiritual master of the brahmanas. This is why Yadunandana Acarya, Ramananda Vasu, and others, who appeared in aristocratic families, did not hesitate to accept initiation from Haridasa. Advaita Acarya did not hesitate to offer Thakura Haridasa the sraddha plate or to accept Mahaprabhu’s remnants with Haridasa in His own home in Santipura. When Haridasa left his body, Mahaprabhu Himself took him on His lap and danced in ecstasy, and then had all the devotees drink the water that had washed Haridasa’s lotus feet. Those who do not have the eyes to see these dealings clear the path to hell for themselves by considering Vaisnavas to be non-brahmanas and belonging to a particular caste.

**Pandita:** Your words will remove many persons’ superstitions and bring about unlimited benefit to the world of Vaisnavas.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Mahaprabhu’s teachings can benefit the whole world because they are not ordinary teachings. Mahaprabhu’s teachings contain no narrow sectarianism. His teachings can award the highest benefit to all living entities and bestow the supreme goal of life on all.

**Pandita:** I am greatly pleased and thrilled to see your immense learning and genius.

**Srila Prabhupada:** This is not our credit. Personally, we have no qualification. These are the instructions of our spiritual master. I simply repeat the words and teachings of eternal truth that have come down in disciplic succession from Krsna to Brahma to Narada to Vyasadeva to Sukadeva to my spiritual master.

## Conversation 4

**Srila Prabhupada and Professor Dr. P. Johanns**  
**Friday, April 20, 1928**

\[This conversation was originally in English. The most senior professor at St. Xaviers College, Calcutta, and a religious instructor for the Roman Catholic Jesuit sect, Dr. P. Johanns, arrived at 1 Ultadingi Junction Road’s Sri Gaudiya Mai ha. to hear about Vaisnava dharma from Srila Prabhupada. A preceptor of religion, Dr. Johanns was a regular and attentive reader of the Harmonist. He also mastered Sanskrit in order to study the philosophical works of the Indians. Sitting in the bookstore at the Gaudiya Matha, he listened to Srila Prabhupada’s instructions while making faithful inquiry.\]

**Professor:** I regularly read the Harmonist, edited by you. Western philosophy is established only on a Western philosophical foundation. I have studied Sankara, Ramanuja, Madhva, and Baladeva’s philosophical doctrine.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Have you studied Baladeva’s original?

**Professor:** No, I have read only the English translation of his commentary.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Unless one reads the original, one often fails to grasp the true purport. And especially, if we do not study these books with a Vaisnava acarya, we cannot understand their real meaning.

**Professor:** You are completely right. I have a great desire to study Sri Jiva Gosvami s texts. His philosophy is of a very high standard. I have tried to read some of them, but it is difficult to enter them. I saw an advertisement in the Harmonist that you are publishing Sri Jiva Gosvami s Bhakti Sandarbha. I have a great desire to get a copy of that.

**Srila Prabhupada:** There is no difference between Baladeva and Sri Jiva. Baladeva was Sri Jiva’s follower, his disciple. Both of them explained the conclusion of acintya-bhedabheda, as was approved by Sri Caitanyadeva.

**Professor:** Sri Jiva’s philosophical work is incomprehensible. It would be nice if I could receive some texts based on his philosophical conclusions written in simple language.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Thakura Bhaktivinoda - who founded the magazine Sajjana-tosani, has written books that are easy and simple translations and explanations of Sri Jiva Gosvami s philosophical conclusions. By reading Thakura Bhaktivinoda’s books you can have easy access to Sri Jiva Gosvami s entire siddhanta. But along with such study of Thakura Bhaktivinoda’s books you must hear from a living source.

**Professor:** True. Without hearing from a living source, just by reading books, one cannot understand everything.

**Srila Prabhupada:** What to speak of not being able to understand everything, by not hearing from a living source one may understand the purport of the text upside down or backward.

\[After saying this, Srila Prabhupada gave the professor a few of Bhaktivinoda Thakura ’s English texts, such as Life and Precepts of Caitanya Mahaprabhu and Nama-bhajana.\]

**Professor:** Thank you so much. You have honored me. I am very much interested in these subjects. If you permit me, I would like to meet and discuss these things with you from time to time.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Our only duty is to glorify Harris topics. Those who are interested in it are our special friends.

**Professor:** Are there any texts composed by Caitanyadeva?

**Srila Prabhupada:** No, He did not write any books. But He spoke some verses. Among them, eight principal verses are renowned as Sri Siksastaka.

**Professor:** Yes, I have read the Siksastaka and its explanation in the Harmonist.

**Srila Prabhupada:** The Siksastaka describes the supreme glories of transcendental sound vibration. Sri Caitanyadeva said that transcendental sound is not ordinary sound born of the material sky; it is manifested from the spiritual sky. Therefore it can provide us with information about the spiritual sky - about the directly spiritual, variegated Supreme Brahman. About twenty-live years ago, I traveled by train from Ranaghat to Krishnanagar with the leader of bhakti preaching in the present era and the founder of Sajjana-tosani, Thakura Bhaktivinoda. At that time, a Christian missionary preacher named Reverend Butter also boarded the train and joined us in our compartment. He too was going to Krishnanagar. We had our chanting beads in our hands at the time and, turning to me, Reverend Butter asked who we were. I told him, “We are preachers of religion, like you. We preach the religion and teachings of Sri Caitanyadeva.” Reverend Butter asked, “Why, in the religion of Sri Caitanyadeva, is there prescription for uselessly chanting God’s name? In our religion, there is an instruction not to chant God’s name uselessly. And why is idolatry encouraged by Caitanyadeva?”

**Professor:** I too am glad to hear your answer on these points.

\[Srila Prabhupada then gave Professor Johanns a copy of Sri Bhaktivinoda Thakura’s Siksadasakamulam, written in Sanskrit and. Published, by the Gaudiya Matha.\]

**Srila Prabhupada:** Please read this book. In it you will had Sri Caitanyadeva’s teachings beautifully summarized. Sri Bhaktivinoda Thakura has written all his books under the shelter of Srila Jiva Gosvami’s scriptural conclusions. All his books are written as supplements to the books of Srila Jiva and Sri Baladeva. We are also trying to follow in Sri Jiva’s footsteps in our present endeavors.

**Professor:** In your philosophy, you firmly accept the idea of completes urrender to the Supreme Lord. I don’t think such deep discussions on surrender to God are found anywhere else. I have been very happy to read the English translation of Saranagati in the Harmonist.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Srila Rupa Gosvami, who was one of Sri Caitanyadeva’s dear most associates. It was Sri Rupa in whom Sri Caitanyadeva invested all His potencies. Rupa Gosvami wrote about the six kinds of saranagati in his book the Bhakti-rasamrta-sindhu, which is a book about the science of devotional service. The analysis of devotional service found in that book is unique.

\[Srila Prabhupada then presented Professor Johanns with a copy of Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura’s Tattva-sutra.\]

**Srila Prabhupada:** This book describes Vaisnava dharma in code form. As the purport of the Vedas is summarized in the Brahma-sutra, so the conclusion and purport of Srimad-Bhagavatam is summarized in the Tattva-sutra. Srimad-Bhagavatam is the natural commentary on Vedanta. Unless one studies Srimad-Bhagavatam under a Vaisnava acarya who is following the principles of the Bhagavata, one cannot understand the real purport of the Brahma-sutra, because Srimad-Bhagavatam is the natural commentary on the Brahma-sutra.

All of Sri Jiva Gosvami’s books are based on Srimad-Bhagavatam, as are the books of the other Gosvamis. Srimad-Bhagavatam is the original commentary on the Vedanta-sutra - Sri Jiva Gosvami, especially, dis-closed this. Sankara's commentary is foreign to the Bhagavatam, and since the Bhagavatam is a commentary written by the same author who wrotethe Sutras, it is the only real commentary. We can only get the real purport of the Vedanta from Srimad-Bhagavatam. Although Srimad-Bhagavatam includes many histories and incidents, it is the best and only philosophical book in the world.

**Professor:** When you say bhagavata, are you talking about the Caitanya-bhagavata?

**Srila Prabhupada:** The Caitanya-bhagavata is a different book. I am talking about Srimad-Bhagavatam. It has been translated into French. There is only a partial translation of it in English.

**Professor:** I have read a portion of Srimad Bhagavatam in French.

**Srila Prabhupada:** The Caitanya-bhagavata that you have just mentioned is currently being translated into English, and some portions of it have already been published in the Harmonist.

**Professor:** Yes, I have seen that. It is a wonderful translation. I always read it with full attention. I need a copy of the Caitanya-bhagavata.

**Srila Prabhupada:** The original edition of our Caitanya-bhagavata has been completed. Now, we will publish a new edition with elaborate commentary. Once it is published, you can have one.

**Professor:** Kindly explain Sri Caitanyadeva’s philosophy in brief.

**Srila Prabhupada:** We find a brief description of Sri Caitanyadeva’s philosophy in an ancient verse like this one:

*aradhyo bhagavan vrajesa-tanayas tad-dhama vrndavanam*  
*ramya kacid upasana vraja-vadhu-vargena ya kalpita*  
*srimad-bhagavatam pramamam amalam prema pum-artho mahan*  
*sri-caitanya-mahaprabhor matam idam tatradarah na parah*

**“God is fully manifest only as Sri Krsna. Sri Krsna manifests in one of three aspects before His respectively qualified devotees. Each of these three features is complete and perfect. They are not partial or incomplete manifestations of Krsna like Paramatma or Brahman. These three complete features manifest as perfect, more perfect, and most perfect and show Themselves in Dvaraka, Mathura, and Vrndavana. In Dvaraka, Sri Krsna is perfect; in Mathura, He is more perfect; and in Vraja He is most perfect.”**

We live on earth, which is one planet in the fourteen worlds. The four-teen worlds consist of seven lower planetary systems and seven upper planetary systems. Of the seven upper planets, the earth is the first. The Bhu, Bhuvah, and Svar areas of planets are where pious but materially attached householders live for enjoyment. Above these three are the Mahar, Jana, Tapa, and Satya planets, destinations for those who have given up household life. Among those renunciants, the Upakurvanas, or those who reside in the guru’s asrama for a fixed period of time and then return home after offering their gum his due daksina go to Maharloka. Naisthika brahmacans, who live in the gurukula throughout their lives, practicing strict celibacy, attain Janaloka. Vanaprasthis go to Tapaloka and sanyasis to Satyaloka.

However, those who are devotees of the Supreme Lord, who have no sinful desire to enjoy life in this world or to merge into the impersonal Brahman, attain the rare Vaikuntha-loka.

Above Vaikuntha is Dvaraka, above Dvaraka is Mathura, and above Mathura is Goloka-Vrndavana. These spiritual abodes are manifest by the sandhini-sakti, or the supporting aspect of the Supreme Lord’s internal potency. All the abodes in the spiritual sky exist in this material world. There is nothing in the material world that does not already exist in the spiritual world.

The manifestation of Vrndavana’s aprakrta pastimes is Goloka. As a lotus remains in the lake without being touched by the water, so Goloka exists in this world without coming in contact with matter. Those whose hearts are not inclined to the Lord’s service cannot understand the transcendental nature of the Lord’s abodes descended into the material world. Ayodhya, Dvaraka, Sri Purusottama Ksetra - these are all different regions of Vaikuntha.

The happiness experienced in Ayodhya is superior to the happiness experienced in Vaikuntha, and the happiness experienced in Dvaraka issuperior to that experienced in Ayodhya. But the happiness relished by the inhabitants of Goloka is the crest jewel of all happinesses. This gradation is based on the variety of rasas available in each place. Even the distress in Goloka dances on the heads of all other happiness, and the lamentation there, too, repeatedly dances on the heads of all other forms of happiness. Happiness, distress, lamentation, and other feelings in Goloka simply nourish one’s supreme bliss.

Sri Caitanyadeva demonstrated the supremacy of rendering service to the Lord of Vrndavana or Gokula. The Supreme Personality of Godhead Sri Krsna is the fountainhead of all Visnu tattvas, Visnu incarnations. That same Krsna manifests as the Lord of Dvaraka, Mathura, and Gokula. Sri Caitanyadeva spoke of Krsna, the Lord of Gokula. All live principal rasas are present in that Krsna; He is Himself the ocean of transcendental mellows.

**Professor:** Could you deline rasa?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Sri Rupa Gosvami gives us a definition for rasain his Bhakti-rasarmrta-sindhu. The rasa we are discussing is not mundane rasa. Mundane rasa is the perverted, abominable, and incomplete reflection of spiritual, transcendental rasa. His definition of rasa is as follows\[as quoted in Sri Caitanya-caritamrita, Adi 10.58\]:

*vyatitya bhavana-vartma yas camatkara-bhara-bhuh*  
*hrdi sattvojjvale badham svadate sa raso matah*

**“That which is beyond imagination, heavy with wonder, and relished in a heart shining with goodness is known as rasa.”**

This rasa has two alambanas, or supports: asraya-alambana, or the support of shelter; and visaya-alambana. or the support of the object. He who is the recipient of rati is called the visaya-alambana, and the one who is the shelter of that rati - the one in whom the rati lives - is the asraya-alambana. In the material world, there are many visayas and asrayas, but in our original source, there is only one object of service without a second, one visaya, and He is none other than Sri Krsna, All others are His servants. Among His subordinates Krsna is neutral to some, the master of others, the friend of some, the son of some, and for some He is the beloved.

Spiritual objects, such as the land of Vrndavana, the Yamuna, the kadamba trees, the forests on the banks of the Yamuna, and Krsna’s flute, cows, stick, and horn are almost unconscious and are the shelter of santa-rasa. Krsna is the Lord and master of His subordinates, such as Raktaka, Patraka, Madhukantha, and other boys, and they are His eternal servants.Sri Krsna is friends with the cowherd boys. In Vraja, Sridama, Sudama,Vasudama, and others are His dear friends.

The manifestation of Godhead is of two kinds: aisvatya (opulence)and madhurya (sweetness). The appearance of supreme opulence, disregarding His pastimes in human form, is called aisvatya. For example, Sri Krsna displayed His four-armed form to His father Vasudeva and mother Devaki, and He showed His mystic opulence to Arjuna. These are His opulent manifestations. If in the manifestation of supreme opulence or other-wise, His pastimes in a human form are not transgressed, then we call that madhurya. For example, when Sri Krsna was killing Putana, He appeared with the tendency of a human child, sucking Yasodas breast. Although Yasoda could not bind Krsna with a long rope, He still appeared to be afraid of His mother. During His childhood pastimes He felled the giant handcart with a kick of His small foot. Even though Sri Krsna’s supreme opulence was on display in each of these pastimes, it did not cover His human like activities.

Moreover, although Sri Krsna is full of all opulence, sometimes Heconceals it and acts like any ordinary human boy. His stealing of yogurt and milk are examples of this. Although all the scriptures praise Sri Krsnaas the Supreme Lord, Yasomati treats Him like her own child. Nanda and Yasoda consider Sri Krsna, who is the maintainer of all other universal maintainers, a dependent child. His friends enjoy various sports with Himby climbing on His shoulder with great affection. The damsels of Vraja consider Sri Krsna their lover even though they see the demigods offeringHim respects. Everything is full and perfect only in Sri Krsna’s character. He is the origin of all.

The perverted reflection of this supremely relishable original reality is the temporary, abominable, mundane rasa. We cannot attribute any abomination to Sri Krsna. Sri Krsna’s pastimes with the gopis of Vrndavana are not a product of this material kingdom. We cannot realize His pastimes as long as our intelligence is absorbed in even a tinge of matter.

**Professor:** This is extremely difficult subject matter. It requires special attention.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Not understanding the purport of Krsna’s pas-times, some Westerners think them obscene, and so try to convert the obscenity into decency by calling His pastimes fairy tales. But their attempt both fails and is of no use. Krsna’s character gives a deathblow to knowledge derived from the senses. Absorption in so-called, mundane morality is a stumbling block for those who wish to attain Krsna’s lotus feet. Krsna is fully independent, self-willed, and self-manifest. There can be no indecency in Him. Everything about Him is decent and supremely beautiful. The determination of what is decent and what is indecent can only be applied to the living entities under His control. But Krsna is the all-powerful, absolute, transcendental Personality of Godhead.

**Professor:** The way you have described Krsna and Vrndavana – your idea is very good.

**Srila Prabhupada:** I am not offering some idea but fact, reality. This is not something available in discussions on ordinary poetry or mundane literature - after all, ultimately, all types of poetry and literature emanate from Sri Krsna’s toenail. Vrndavana is the abode of all transcendental poetry and literature. Worldly people are overwhelmed, astonished, and spellbound when they discuss even a small branch of mundane poetry or literature, but what is available in the world is but an incomplete, partial, limited, perverted reflection of the complete and unlimited variety avail-able in the realm of transcendental literature.

**Professor:** Kindly say something about how best to worship Krsna.

**Srila Prabhupada:** The worship performed by the damsels of Vraja is the best form of Krsna worship. You have heard that Krsna is absolute and supremely independent. The omniscient Lord, the absolute energetic, possesses complete saktis. That energy works in three ways: (1) it gives Krsna pleasure, (2) it enables Krsna to dominate and enjoy, and (3) it sup-ports and maintains everything in existence. The names of these potencies are hladini, samvit, and sandhini, respectively. All the objects meant for Krsna’s enjoyment are manifestations of His sandhini potency; the sandhini potency serves Krsna by manifesting all the ingredients for Krsna’s enjoyment, such as His abode, the forms of His devotees, the places of His pastimes, and all spiritual opulence.

The samvit potency serves Krsna by helping Him realize His positionas the supreme doer, the only enjoyer of bliss, and the supreme Absolute Truth without a second.

The hladini potency manifests Herself in a variety of forms to in-crease Krsna’s pleasure and to allow Him to enjoy various transcendentalrasas in newer and newer ways. The hladini-sakti takes the form of the damsels of Vraja, who are the bodily expansions of Sri Radha, who is Herself the embodiment of love for Krsna. Sri Radhika enchants Krsna; She is the original shelter (asraya) of all His supernatural power. When the hladini-sakti takes the form of Sri Radha with Sri Krsna, together They become the Divine Couple. Although appearing as two - the enjoyer and the enjoyed - They are one. Mahaprabhu has come to teach us how toper form devotional service by subordinating ourselves to Srimati Radhika.

**Professor:** This is very high philosophical truth.

**Srila Prabhupada:** This is not just one of many worldly philosophies, so in that sense, it cannot be compared to any of them by saying it is superior and they inferior. It is a unique, transcendental, philosophical truth. In his Sat Sandarbha, Srila Jiva Gosvami delines adhoksaja like this: The God-head, Krsna, is He who has reserved the absolute right to not expose Him-self to mundane human senses. Adhoksaja refers to that which cannot be measured by one’s material senses. When that transcendental knowledge reveals itself to our service-inclined senses, only then will we realize it; Otherwise, even the best students in the world cannot even partially know the science of Krsna. That science cannot be learned through sadhana, by the best forms of intelligence, or the best powers of deliberation.

Despite this, many people rush to discuss it with an enjoying spirit, considering it a mundane philosophy or text. The Absolute Truth is never revealed to such people. We cannot measure objects of the fourth or fifth dimension with our present material sense devises. Being unable to do so, we often heave a sigh of relief with phrases like “To the infinity.” Vaikuntha, or the transcendental object, is of the fourth dimension. Because we are unable to perceive Him, we want to turn Him into something “impersonal.” But the transcendental Absolute Truth is never impersonal.

**Professor:** Yes, yes! (with great joy). This is a wonderful consideration.

**Srila Prabhupada:** The more the transcendental Absolute Truth reveals Himself by concealing His transcendental qualities, the more He is considered impersonal, formless, and so on. Thinking like this is just an-other kind of idolatry. We should know that the transcendental Absolute Truth is supremely independent. He is not something invented by human knowledge or intelligence. An idol is some form of God that we have in-vented or imagined. Whatever the living entities deem “personal,” “impersonal,” or “formless” by the power of their imagination is all idolatry. The transcendental personality, Sri Krsna, or the Supreme Brahman, is neither “personal” nor “impersonal”; neither “with form” or “formless.”

We will never be able to approach the transcendental Absolute Truth with a challenging attitude. Such an attitude is called argumentation. In-stead, we have to submit humbly before the Lord. Godhead Himself will take the initiative. Thousands of attempts on our part will never lead to Him. Whatever we can measure with our senses, we can use to consider and conclude things like “This is not it.” Or we can ascertain a thing’s limits - what is incomplete and partial, for example. When some people say,“God has a form,” or “God is formless,” or “At hist God has a form, but ultimately, He is formless,” they confuse the transcendental object with material time, place, and person. We can never master the transcendental Personality of Godhead at any time. We cannot think that what we can see - visible time, place, and person - has anything to do with the supreme spirit, the Supreme Lord.

So the knowledge we gather with our senses is the main impediment on the path of God realization. This is why Srila Jiva Gosvamipada said that “He is God by whom all sensory knowledge is refuted.” Lord Caitanya’s real followers never encourage idolatry of any kind, be it gross or subtle. They do not try to realize the transcendental Absolute Truth with man-made theories or philosophies shaped by the imagination.

**Professor:** What kind of philosophical method do the followers of Caitanya adhere to?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Although there are innumerable philosophical ways of thought in this world, they can be divided into two classes: developed through aural reception or developed through empiricism (personal experience). Many persons present themselves as followers of what they have heard through the disciplic succession, although they actually accept the empirical path. Mahaprabhu called these persons unauthorized, pseudo adherents to learning through the disciplic succession. When Caitanyadeva had a philosophical discussion with the Vedantist Sarvabhauma Bhattacarya, He proved the Mayavadis to be such pseudo followers of the disciplic succession, or covered atheists. He says (in Caitanya-caritamrta, Madhya 6.168):

*veda na maniya bauddha haya ta ’ nastika*  
*vedasraya nastikya-vada bauddhake adhika*

**“The Buddhists do not recognize the authority of the Vedas; there-fore they are considered agnostics. However, those who have taken shelter of the Vedic scriptures yet preach agnosticism in accordance with the Mayavada philosophy are certainly more dangerous than the Buddhists.”**

One cannot gain transcendental knowledge through the empirical process. Just as the river Ganges descends from the Himalayas through the hole shaped like a cow’s mouth called Gomukhi, so the flow of transcendental knowledge about the Absolute Truth must pass through the mouth of the acarya. The acarya is God’s messenger. He brings us news of the transcendental world. His words may sound like the ordinary words of this mortal world. The empiricist’s eyes, nose, tongue, and skin cannot approve them. News of Vaikuntha, as it comes from the spiritual master’s mouth, can be received only through the ears. We lack spiritual qualification at the present, and therefore our other four senses do not yet have the capacity to grasp transcendental experience. This is not so different from the ordinary news of the world; news of Calcutta must be heard from a person who has experience of Calcutta. Instead of challenging such an experienced person, we will have to hear from him submissively if we wish to understand what Calcutta is like.

However, an honest tendency to inquire with an unhypocritical spirit is not evil the way the ego-driven challenging spirit is. Proper inquiry reveals a thirst for hearing. That is why it is written in our scripture that the spiritual master should be given at least one year to assess whether or not an aspiring disciple has approached with a challenging attitude or is genuinely wanting to take shelter of him. One has to approach a spiritual master and hear from him submissively, not debate with or challenge him. We should be careful about our intentions when asking questions. Honest inquiry with a desire to hear from an experienced acarya is called the descending path; the path adopted by empiricists is called the ascending path.

Those who adhere to the ascending path of empiricism approach teachers highly experienced in material science, and if needed, they invite those teachers to debate with them. This is a dangerous approach in which people with thirty years of experience try to defeat people with fifty years of experience, and long experience is completely useless in the face of new scientific discoveries. Likewise, 20,000-year-old civilizations prove backward in the face of 10,000-year-old civilizations. But the empirical process, which is based on the experience gathered with the material senses, can never actually provide one with complete knowledge.

Therefore, Caitanyadeva’s devotees do not think the experimental process to be of much value. Apart from those who actually study material science, everyone is more or less forced to accept and respect the empirical path and what its adherents say is true. But if we want to know the Absolute Truth, we should not maintain the challenging attitude of a chemist ora physicist. Rather, we should humbly and patiently hear with service-inclined ears the transcendental message spoken to us by the acarya. And do not try to adjust transcendental philosophical truth to agree with the philosophical truths of horses and asses, and do not introduce mundane truth into transcendental truth. If we follow such an ascending process, Sri Caitanya’s unadulterated philosophical truth will not manifest in our hearts.

While preaching the truth, Sri Caitanyadeva debated with many so-called philosophers and used His weapon of spotless authorized philosophy to smash into pieces all the unauthorized philosophies they presented based on their empirical knowledge. Hearing with attention and patience from the fully devoted followers of Caitanya, we will be supremely benefited and astonished to see in the character of Sri Caitanya a complete spiritual synthesis and a wonderful solution to all problems.

**Professor:** I have a strong desire to hear about Caitanyadeva from you. If you kindly instruct me in this matter when you have time, I will be very glad and grateful.

**Srila Prabhupada:** We have no other business than to give these teachings. We are always ready to serve you. You are a professor of philosophy; therefore you also need to hear proper, and authorized philosophy. All mundane philosophies are unauthorized, wrong, and are an impediment to God realization. Unless unauthorized philosophies are refuted with authorized philosophy, no one can realize God.

**Professor:** Yes, what you say is totally correct.

**Srila Prabhupada:** I will read you an article that was written by Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura. Please hear it carefully. This explains briefly but beautifully the Vaisnava philosophy. \\ Srila Prabhupada reads the article “To love God.” This article was later published in the Harmonist, in April1928.\]

**To Love Cod**

(Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura)

It was Christ Jesus who hist said “Love God with all thy heart, with all thy mind, with all thy soul, and with all thy strength, and love man as thy brother.” This is an Absolute Truth indeed; but different men put different interpretations to this noble expression. The expressions of all great men are nice but somewhat mysterious - when understood they bring the truth nearest to heart; otherwise, they remain mere letters that “kill.” The reason of the mystery is that men, advanced in their inward approach to the Deity, are in the habit of receiving revelations, which are but mysteries to those that are behind them. The stages of progress are very much the same as the circles of spiritualism, which, though not true them-selves, explain a great deal about the gradual development of the soul.

We have understood some spiritualists to maintain that matter, when sublimated, converts itself to spirit. This theory is indeed against any inward conviction. Matter is matter, and spirit is spirit; one of them cannot form the other. Spirit is certainly of superior existence; though we cannot fully understand in our present state of material imprisonment what relation spirit bears, exactly, to matter, space, and time. Meta-physics apart, we decide that the human soul rises higher andhigher and can understand things of which we have no idea at present. Subject to this important rule, Christ Jesus of Nazareth received and uttered these words quoted above.

To readers who are a little above the scale of ordinary men, these expressions of Jesus teach that man should love God with all his heart (meaning the affections of the heart perceivable in all children, as opposed to hate), with all his mind (meaning the intellect which knows, as opposed to igorance of good things), with all his soul, (meaning the principle of the human constitution which worships the Almighty and feels its own immortality), and with all its strength (meaning all active work). To the inspired, however, more things and better and more sublime meanings appear beneath these holy words of the inspired Jesus. He teaches man to love God and not to know, inter, hate, or think of God. He tells us that man in his absolute state is not the intellect or the body but is the pure soul itself.

The essence of the soul is wisdom, and its action is love absolute. The absolute condition of man is his absolute relation to the Deity in pure love. Love then alone is the religion of the soul and consequently of the whole man. The pupil asks here “What have I to do with the heart? My heart loves to seethe “sun smile, to eat the sweetest dishes, and to see a dance.”Jesus profoundly replies, “Yes, you must love God with all thy heart. Your heart now runs to things other than God, but you must, as you train a bad horse, make your feelings run to loving God.” This is one of the four principles of worship, or what is known in Vaisnava literature as santa-rasa.

Then the pupil says, “My Lord, the intellect takes me elsewhere from God, i.e., it wants to take me to Positivism. Please instruct me what am I to do.”

“Yes,” replies Jesus, “you must love god with all your mind, i.e., when you perceive, conceive, remember, imagine, and reason, you must not allow yourself to be a dry thinker but must love. Love alone can soften the dryness of the intellect. You must develop the intellect on all good and holy things by means of the love of truth, spiritual beauty, and harmony.” This is the second phase of Vaisnava development, which passes by the name of dasya-rasa.

The pupil then inquires whether the development of the affections and the intellect is quite enough for him. Then says the Lord, “You must love God with thy soul also, i.e., you must perceive yourself in spiritual communication with the Deity and receive holy revelations in your sublimest hours of worship.” This is called the sakhya-rasa of the Vaisnavas - the soul approaching the Deity in holy and fearless service.

The disciple apprehends that he will be lost in such a position and will be unable to act. Then the Saviour tells us these words: “You must love God with all thy strength, or will – you are wrong to conclude that you will lose your active existence – you will get it the more. Work for God and to God, proceeding from no self-interested views but from a holy free will(which is alone the strength of man), and identifying itself with pure love, it will fully engross your attention.” This description is of bhakti in general.

Then Jesus proceeds to tell us, “You must love man as thy brother.” From this is inferred the fourth phase of love, which is a feeling that all men are brothers and God is their common Father. This is vatsalya-rasa in its hist stage of development.

Bhakti (love) is thus perceived in the very hist development of the man in the shape of heart, then in the shape of mind, then in the shape of soul, and lastly in the shape of will. These shapes do not destroy each other but beautifully harmonize themselves into a pure construction of what we call the spiritual man, or the Ekanta of Vaisnava literature.

But there is another, more sublime, truth behind this fact, which is revealed to a few who are prepared for it. We mean the spiritual conversion of the soul into a woman. It is in that sublime and lofty state that the soul can taste the sweetness of an indissoluble marriage with the God of love. The fifth, or the highest, stage of Vaisnava development is this, which we call madhurya-rasa, and on this alone the most beautiful portion of the Vaisnava literature so ably expiates. This phase of human life, mysterious as it is, is not attainable by all, nay, we should say, by any but “God’s own.” It is so very beyond the reach of common men that the rationalists and even the ordinary theists cannot understand it, nay, they go as far as to sneer at it as something unnatural.

O God! Reveal Thy most valuable truths to all so that Your own may not be numbered with the fanatics and the crazed and that the whole of mankind may be admitted as “Your own.”

**Professor:** Simply wonderful! Kindly publish this article in the Harmonist magazine.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Once, I met Reverend Ridley De. After he quietly listened to my lecture for two or three hours, he said, “Since you are saying things that are so similar to Christianity, why don’t you declare yourself a Christian?” To this I said, “Christianity is only a partial representation of - or a step on the ladder of - Vaisnava dharma. Apart from that, we have more, which supplements what Christianity teaches. Things that have not been said in Christianity because qualification is there in a fuller extent in Vaisnavism. Vaisnava dharma is the only supreme religion for all living entities. The other religious systems are either stages of Vaisnavism or perversions of it. To the degree that they are stages, they can be accepted for particular classes of people; if they are perversions, they should be rejected.

Christ is an empowered incarnation (saktyavesa-avatara) of a portion of Krsnacandra’s plenary portion, Lord Visnu. Considering the capability and qualification of particular countries or societies, and in order to benefit them, Lord Visnu, invested a portion of His energy in an exalted living being and sent him to them as the Son of God. Christ, therefore, did not have the qualification to reveal the highest transcendental mellows of ecstatic love - the complete sweetness of the supreme Absolute Truth Krsnacandra’s transcendental pastimes, which only Krsnacandra Himself, who is the ocean of all transcendental mellows, can distribute in the world. Even those who live in the first of the two-storied Vaikuntha cannot understand Krsnacandra’s sweetness. In Vaikuntha there are only two and half rasas, santa, dasya, and some portion of sakhya in awe and reverence. *Kandhecade kandhe cadaya kare krida rana* (Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Madhya19.223): Krsna climbs on the shoulders of His friends, carries His cowherd friends on His own shoulders, and enjoys sporting activities. This kind of friend ship with love and affection does not exist anywhere but Goloka Vrndavana. It is not even present in Vaikuntha. In Vaikuntha one has to stand before the Lord with respect, awe, and reverence. Ramanuja could not realize the sweetness of sakhya-rasa played out with love and affection. He could not understand vatsalya-rasa or madhurya-rasa. It is true that a small portion of dasya-rasa, sakhya-rasa, and vatsalya-rasa, each mixed with opulence, touched the hearts of Christ’s true devotees, but rasas mixed with pure love and affection, or madhurya-rasa, shine brightly only in Vraja-dhama.

The moonlike personality of Navadvipa, the son of Sacidevi, distributed that sweet, confidential rasa. Although Ramanuja thought sakhya-, vatsalya-, and madhurya-rasa mixed with love and affection were detrimental to the proper execution of service to God, we say something different: until the thorns of awe and reverence, or aisvarya, are removed, one cannot serve God properly. This is because this material world is the perverted reflection of the transcendental spiritual world. What is faultless and unlimitedly variegated in Vaikuntha, where the living entities are all situated in their constitutional position is, in this material world, faulty, limited in scope, temporary - only a perverted reflection.

In this perverted reflection of a world, we find both the shelters\[ as ray as\] and objects of affection \[visavas\] in the five rasas, or relation-ships, santa, dasya, sakhya, vatsalya, and madhurya. Had the shelter and object of these five rasas not existed in the two-storied Vaikuntha, they would not exist in this world. The only difference between the shelters and objects in this world and those in the spiritual world is that in the spiritual world they are faultless, complete, eternal, and situated in their original constitutional position. In the perverted world, everything is the opposite, so here they are faulty, incomplete, temporary, and perverted. In this world, the jivas play out innumerable scenes in which they lord it over material nature. In the transcendental abode, even though the real objectis one without a second, He manifests Himself through His inconceivable power as innumerable objects of service according to the service desires of His servants. Whatever is extremely abominable here is most relishable there.

Sri Caitanyadeva distributed those highest, most relishable, transcendental mellows to fortunate souls. No one but Sri Caitanyadeva could have revealed this highest spiritual rasa, because this rasa is Krsna’s personal asset. No one but Him has the right to distribute it.

There is another difference between Christianity and sanatana-dharma, We followers of sanatana-dharma do not believe in the theory of the jivas having only one life. The Christians say, “If people do not accept the idea that they live only one life, then they will not become attached to God.” But in the East, other than the atheist Carvaka, everyone accepts the idea of reincarnation. Even sects like the Buddhism and Jainism accept the idea that jivas are reborn.

There is a religion in the West called “pessimism,” which is similar to Buddhism and Jainism. The only difference between pessimism and the other two is this belief in reincarnation. Although both Western pessimism and Indian Buddhism and Jainism accept the concept of the soul’s complete disengagement from matter, pessimism preaches the theory of complete disengagement from matter based on the theory that we live only one life, whereas Buddhism and Jainism preach the same ideas with the understanding that we reincarnate. Persons like Schopenhauer and Hertman are proponents of the theory of disengagement from matter based on the single-life theory. The Buddhists say that after cultivating compassion, renunciation, and other qualities over many lifetimes, Sakyasimha first attained the platform of Bodhisattva and then ultimately became a Buddha, or enlightened one. The Jains say that after practicing honesty, com-passion, renunciation, and other such qualities for many, many births, the living entities gradually advance to the positions of Narada, Mahadeva, Vasudeva, Paravasudeva, Cakravarti, and ultimately, they attain complete emancipation.

Those who do not accept the concept of reincarnation have to concoct things. Lord Sri Krsna says in the Bhagavad-gita (2.22):

*vasamsi jirnani yatha vihaya navani grhnati naro ’parani*  
*tatha sanrani vihaya jirnany anyani samyati navani dehr*

**“As a person puts on new garments, giving up old ones, the soul similarly accepts new material bodies, giving up the old and useless ones.”**

And, in texts 4.5-6:

*bahuni me vyatitani janmani tava carjuna*  
*tany aham veda sarvani na tvam vettha parantapa*  
*ajo ’pi sann avyayatma bhutanam isvaro ’pi san*  
*prakrtim svam adhisthaya sambhavamy atma-mayaya*

**“Many, many births both you and I have passed. I can remember all of them, but you cannot, O subduer of the enemy! Although I am unborn and My transcendental body never deteriorates, and although I am the Lord of all living entities, by My internal energy I still appear in every millennium in My original transcendental form.”**

The living entity takes birth in this world under the control of the Supreme Lord’s illusory energy. As a result, he cannot remember his past life. The living entity takes birth supported by the subtle body, which he has developed according to his karma.
