By contrast, the Supreme Lord’s appearance in different forms depends completely on His independent will. Unlike the jivas, His pure spiritual body is not covered by gross and subtle bodies. Instead, He effortlessly manifests in this world His eternal, transcendental Vaikuntha form. If one were to argue, “How can spiritual truth manifest in the material world?” we would answer, ‘The Supreme Lord’s potency is inconceivable and beyond all material thought. Therefore, we cannot use material reasoning to understand what can be accomplished by spiritual truth. We should simply accept the fact that the Supreme Lord, who possesses inconceivable power, is not subject to material rules and regulations. If He wishes, He can easily manifest all spiritual truth in the material world in pure form, or He can transform all of matter into spirit. There is not even a tinge of doubt that the Saccidananda form of the Supreme Lord is beyond the jurisdiction of matter, and although He appears in the material world, He is absolutely pure. It’s true, that maya, by which the living entity becomes bewildered, is also the Supreme Lord’s energy, but when we speak of the Lord’s energy, we are referring to His spiritual energy. The Lord’s energy is one, but to the Lord, it is spiritual energy, and to those who are bound by their karma, it appears to be material energy.

**Professor:** What is Vaikuntha, actually?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Vaikuntha is the place where there is no anxiety, no room for measuring things with our senses. Vaikuntha is two-storied. In the first story, the Supreme Lord is present as the supremely opulent personality. Above that, in the second story, despite the Lord being fully opulent, He covers His opulence with the power of His sweetness by His inconceivable potency and eternally manifests as the most nectarean personality, the ocean of all transcendental mellows.

Vaikuntha is not a limited place like heaven, nor is there any fault there, nor is it a place of enjoyment for demigods. After going to heaven by the strength of our pious deeds, we will again husk paddy - that is, wewill enjoy life in the company of a wife, children, and so on, just as we do here. This is not the Vaisnava’s idea of Vaikuntha, where Krsna is the only Lord and enjoyer, and all others are eternally engaged in His various services. According to the Vaisnavas, that mundane, demigod heaven is as illusory as imaginary flowers in the sky (*tridasapura akasa puspayate*).

We are all servants of God; God is the only object of service. We follow the philosophy of simultaneous oneness with and difference from God. We will choose to become one with God and thus abolish our spiritual existence. We will choose to place obstacles in the way of living our eternal spiritual propensity. We will choose to commit spiritual suicide or become intoxicated by worldly happiness and prosperity, or to enjoy life in the heavenly planets along with relatives. All these are against the nature of the spirit. We Vaisnavas do not have any such desire. We are associated counterparts of the Supreme Lord. The present-day thoughtful persons are averse to hearing scriptural conclusions, and they think the path of argumentation is more valuable. They are suitably rewarded or punished by their very argumentation. But we accept the path of descending knowledge. The Supreme Lord and His devotees bring us the news of Vaikuntha by mercifully descending into this world. The Vaisnavas are not karma-kandis (material elevationists) or jnana-kandis (Salvationists); the yare neither acidvadis, acinmatravadis, nor cinmatravadis. Instead, they are cid-vilasavadis.

**Professor:** What are those philosophies - acidvada, acinmatravada, cinmatravada, and cid-vilasavada?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Acidvada means materialism: we will become the India of heaven, enjoy sense gratification in this world, eat ghee even if we have to borrow money as Carvaka suggests with no need to repay the debt later, make all arrangements for a comfortable stay in this world, earn, maintain good health in order to better enjoy life, use our canine teeth for eating fish and meat, become intoxicated by sex, etc.

On seeing that acidvada, or materialism, has limits and that our enjoyment of it is temporary and accompanied by various inevitable miseries, which we blame on the nature of consciousness, or existence, we try to destroy consciousness forever. This is called acinmatravada, If existence itself is the source of distress, then it is better to stop this state of existence all together. Sakyasimha, the atheist Kapila, and others propound this philosophy. And to try to destroy the very existence of the minute spirit soul by becoming one with Brahman is called cinmatravada, Sankaracarya and, before him, Dattatreya, preached this philosophy.

Cidvilasa-vaicitrya is the minute jivas, who are parts and parcels of the Supreme Conscious, remaining attracted to the super conscious, and exhibiting their attraction and attachment for the Supreme Conscious through their variegated spiritual service desires. According to this philosophy, the soul eternally enjoys pastimes with the Supersoul in the most sublime way. In this, there is no sin or offense; the souls are not covered, as they are in acidvada. Nor are the soul or Supersoul denied, as they are in cinmatravada. Nor is there spiritual suicide, as in cinmatravada, Here, there is complete and perfect development, beauty and meeting, between the soul and the Supersoul.

**Professor:** You have spoken some highly philosophical truths. I will need a lot of time to realize all that you have said.

**Srila Prabhupada:** You will not only require a lot of time, but you will have to hear these discussions again and again from the mouth of genuine, realized persons. Some days ago, Mr. Chapman (the librarian of the Imperial Library of Calcutta) came to the Gaudiya Matha and listened to Sri Caitanyadeva’s philosophical conclusions for two or three hours. Ashe was leaving he said, “This philosophical truth is so incomprehensible that even a learned, experienced, wise person like me is unable to enter into it.” Therefore, unless one hears it repeatedly with a heart inclined to service, it is impossible to understand this subject matter, because it is not an ordinary topic. That is why Sri Caitanyadeva instructed us to hear and chant the topics of the Supreme Lord with “humility like that of a blade of grass” and “tolerance like that of a tree.”

We have a desire to preach about Sn Caitanyadeva’s compassion in the Western countries, but we don’t know how much it will be appreciated there.

**Professor:** I hope all the gentlemen of the Western world will respectfully accept your words. I am particularly happy to hear these discussions about Caitanya’s philosophy from you.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Have you ever been to Rome? We will also go there sometime.

**Professor:** No, personally I have not gone there as yet, but that is the place of our preceptor. If you go there, you will certainly be warmly welcomed. I have a lot of contacts there. When Dr. Ravindranatha Thakura went to London, it seems he did not mix much with our Roman Catholic sect.

**Srila Prabhupada:** I met Ravibabu just the other day. I told him, “If we distribute a portion of the treasure Sri Caitanyadeva bestowed on us in the Western countries, then the thoughtful among them will accept it with great appreciation and start to consider it. Such unadulterated katha has not been preached in the Western countries as yet.” At hist, Ravibabu thought that the perverted sentiments found in the books of the Buddhists and the mundane sahajiyas, which are presently passing in society as Vaisnava literature composed by Candidasa and Vidyapati, are the ultimate conclusion of Vaisnava dharma. But I told him that we will preach in the West the transcendental, ecstatic, topmost philosophical truth as propagated by Sri Caitanyadeva.

**Professor:** You should preach this highly philosophical spiritual truth in London.

**Srila Prabhupada:** If God wishes, it will be done for sure. Caitanyadeva and His living devotees desire to wake up the entire world. In His religion, there is no function of matter in the form of material enjoyment based on sense gratification. Taking advantage of His religion, those functions of matter that have come into existence - Sri Caitanyadeva has no connection with them. Let the people of the whole world worship Caitanyadeva with their spiritual propensity. This alone will bring them supreme benefit.

\[Being very pleased to have heard Srila Prabhupada’s words, Professor Johanns began to say as he took his leave that he had met many Hindus, saints, sannyasis, and learned scholars in India, but all of them, more or less encourage anyabhilasa, or the desire for material enjoyment, and that their honesty and. learning is borrowed - that is, it is only book knowledge used to impress people. But today he was very, very glad to have met with a genuine and practical pandita and saintly person. “Srila Prabhupada spoke from the heart,” he said, “and it was clear that he believed his own words so much that without being a self-realized soul such self-confidence could, not arise. I also found in him the unique capacity to make others believe the truth he has realized. This is possible only to a great personality who is empowered by God.”\]

## Conversation 5

**Srila Prabhupada and Mr. N. Bardaloi**  
**October 8, 1928**

\[Being invited by the local pious people, Srila Prabhupada went to the Shanti Bhavan house of the famous public leader and high-court lawyer, Mr. N. Bardaloi, in Gauhati, Assam. Mr. Bardaloi asked Srila Prabhupada a few questions.\]

**Bardaloi:** At present, it is more essential to remove our country’s pitiable poverty than it is to preach dharma or religion.

**Srila Prabhupada:** How do you define “dharma” and “religion”?

**Bardaloi:** To try to merge with God through meditation and comprehension.

**Srila Prabhupada:** It will be of great benefit if what you call “dharma” is not preached. Another name for your dharma is violence to the living entities. From this impersonal conception, groups such as fruitive workers, sense enjoyers, and dry renunciants have come into existence. Instead, by preaching the supreme religion as taught in this world by Sri Caitanyadeva, everyone will be benefited by all means and attain supreme auspiciousness. Sri Caitanyadeva said:

*bharata-bhumite haila manusya-janma yara*  
*janma sarthaka kari ’ kora para-upakara*

**“One who has taken his birth as a human being in the land of India (Bharata-varsa) should make his life successful and work for the benefit of all other people.” (Caitanya-caritamrta, Adi 9.41)**

Sri Caitanyadeva spoke about the benefit of one and all, but His “oneand all” is not limited, narrow, contradictory, based on envy, temporary, changeable, or as imaginary as flowers in the sky, which is how the religion of the so-called social welfare activists is. Sri Caitanyadeva’s welfareis para, the best, not apara, narrow or temporary. But people with tiny brains will never be able to bring any real benefit to one and all with what-ever public welfare system they have invented or will invent in the future. All they have are proposals for how to achieve temporary relief.

Mahaprabhu revealed the welfare system of the Srimad-Bhagavatam: vedyam vastavam atra vastu sivadam tapa-trayonmulanam (Srimad-Bhagavatam 1.1.2), “The highest truth is reality distinguished from illusion for the welfare of all. Such truth uproots the threefold miseries.”The Srimad-Bhagavatam’s welfare process was discovered and refined by Sri Caitanyadeva, and it is alone auspicious, because it alone removes all three forms of material misery. The benevolence systems invented by the thoughtful people of the world can award only temporary relief or enjoyment; they cannot award true auspiciousness, the goal of life.

Moreover, they do not uproot the threefold material miseries. Miseries are the effects of a cause, and effects cannot be destroyed until the tiling that caused them is destroyed. You can cut off a banyan tree’s branches and twigs a thousand times, but if you don’t uproot the tree, they will grow up again. The thousands of man-made ways to help people are just like trying to bail the water from the ocean by hand. Even if thousands of people bail for ages, they will never empty the vast ocean. It may seem like they are making progress when they see lakes of seawater moved from the ocean to some other place, but they will never empty the ocean. One can never dry up the ocean of threefold miseries by using one’s palm. If you think you can, you are either cheating people or being cheated yourself.

So other than following the process prescribed in Srimad-Bhagavatam, we cannot uproot the threefold miseries. The threefold miseries take innumerable forms. They cannot be eradicated by some concocted means.

The covering and throwing potencies of nescience, which appear in the form of forgetting God, are the main causes of misery in all its varieties. Unless we destroy that cause, the effect cannot be destroyed.

If we don’t teach people how to serve God, we cannot alleviate the country’s distresses. And if we preach service to God everywhere, then all countries and all persons will attain benefit at all times.

The Supreme Lord is a fully spiritual object. He is not impersonal, formless, or something meant for the enjoyment of the embodied beings’ senses. Whatever we can access with our mundane senses cannot then be transcendental. Our senses belong to the third dimension and cannot perceive anything beyond that third dimension. The attempt to bring the Supreme Lord into our material purview through hypothesis – imagining Him to be impersonal or formless, for example, - is simply the attempt to measure the transcendental personality as if He were a small object accesible to us. It is simply an attempt to equate Him with maya. Maya is “that which can be measured.” Whatever is not the internal spiritual potency is maya. The spiritual potency is the Lord’s internal prowess. Whatever is presumed to be overwhelmed by the Lord’s external potency, may it be gigantic, powerful, unchangeable, or impersonal, is all mundane idolatry.

**Bardaloi:** When we cannot be certain God exists - there is no confirmation that He does - then what good will propagating service to God do?

**Srila Prabhupada:** This same doubt is there, in the background or openly stated, in the hearts of those who identify themselves as accomplished karmis, jnanis, and yogis. The desire to become an accomplished kami or jnani is born from the attempt to either enjoy or reject this world. Enterprising men imitate the supremely independent Supreme Lord, who is full of spiritual variegatedness, and try to become small editions of Him. Therefore, they are eager to deny His existence. Since their objective is hiranya (gold or money) and kasipu (soft bed or women), they deny the existence of Lord Visnu, who is both the origin and ocean of all existence. Sometimes, proud jnanis, maintaining the sin of considering the original source, Lord Visnu, something for them to enjoy, say He has no hands or feet.

When the Vaisnava servants of Lord Visnu preach about Him, whois transcendental, fully independent, eternal, full of knowledge and bliss, and who possesses a spiritual form, enterprising karmis and jnanis dare to measure Him with their sensory knowledge following in the footsteps of Hiranyakasipu. They say, “O preachers of Visnu’s religion! You are subordinate to us - inferior to us in all respects. You have no experience at all. Therefore, we will harass you in every way. We will deny the root of your intuition and have you chastised by empiricists. Why are you rejecting Sanda and Amarka’s authority and instead allowing yourselves to be directed by the natural propensity of the soul? If you cannot show us Visnu, who we should be able to see with our senses, in the pillar of this palace, then we will not believe that He exists at all.”

When human beings develop this acme of sin, then, in order to enhance His devotees’ pleasure, the Lord manifests His sublime transcendental form before them. At the same time, He totally uproots the mundane conceptions of those who are averse to Him by manifesting His formidable, fierce-looking form. Great fruitive workers and mental speculators who deny the existence and inconceivably supreme will of Lord Visnu engage in severe penances to remove the world’s obstacles, dangers, and natural calamities. Great fruitive workers consider Brahma to be the bestower of benediction and thus collect their material enjoyment from him by pleasing him through austerity. As an ideal example for others, Hiranyakasipu performed severe penance in order to enjoy the world. He wanted to be given the position of ruling the earth and, ultimately, the position of Brahma. Through his direct sensory knowledge and realization, Hiranyakasipu thought of whatever causes of death he could and prayed to Brahma to mitigate them. His words are recorded in Srimad-Bhagavatam(7.3.35-38) as follows:

*bhutebhyas tvad-visrstebhyo mrtyur ma bhun mama prabho*

**“O my lord, please let me not meet death from any of the living entities created by you.”**

*nantar bahir diva naktam anyasmad api cayudhaih*  
*na bhumau nambare mrtyur na narair na mrgair api*

**“Grant me that I not die within any residence or outside any residence, during the daytime or at night, nor on the ground or in the sky. Grant me that my death not be brought by any being other than those created by you, nor by any weapon, nor by any human being or animal.”**

*vyasubhir vasumadbhir va surasura-mahoragaih*  
*apratidvandvatam yuddhe aika-patyam ca dehinam*  
*sarvesam loka-palanam mahimanam yathatmanah*  
*tapo-yoga-prabhavanam yan na risyati karhicit*

**“Grant me that I not meet death from any entity, living or nonliving. Grant me, further, that I not be killed by any demigod or demon or by any great snake from the lower planets. Since no one can kill you in the battle field, you have no competitor. Therefore, grant me the benediction that I too may have no rival. Give me sole lordship over all the living entities and presiding deities, and give me all the glories obtained by that position. Furthermore, give me all the mystic powers attained by long austerities and the practice of yoga, for these cannot be lost at any time.”**

But Visnu appeared in order to preach His inconceivable power, His omnipresence, and His supreme will by assuming a transcendental, inconceivable form - a form so inconceivable that Hiranyakasipu, an ideal example of success for both karmis and jnanis, could not ever have imagined it. Hiranyakasipu thought that death is possible only when another living being created by Brahma kills us; he thought death possible only under certain circumstances or at certain times on some planet in this world. But Visnu, surpassing the limits of all of the wisdom and sharp intelligence of the great karmis and politicians even of Hiranyakasipu’s caliber, defeated all the considerations of the third dimension and disregarded all man-made possibilities and impossibilities. He displayed His inconceivable power by appearing as Nrsimhadeva. He then shattered the heart of Hiranyakasipu and uprooted the atheistic mentality. Nrsimhadeva revealed that the human beings and animals are never equal to Lord Visnu. Lord Visnu’s position is inconceivable, and no created being can measure it with their senses. He is transcendental to all material senses.

Transcendental truth is that by which all sensory knowledge is refuted. Nrsimhadeva appeared from a pillar and disclosed that Visnu is complete; He can fully manifest His transcendental and immeasurable name, form, qualities, and pastimes within an atom and remain there. This is impossible for human knowledge to comprehend. At the same time, He can also remain fully present outside each and every atom. Unless the material conception inherent in a mundane mentality is uprooted by Nrsimhadeva’s sharp nails, Lord Visnu, who is the ocean of pure goodness, will continue to appear to us to be an ordinary living being created by Brahma, or something for us to enjoy with our mundane senses.

**Bardaloi:** How is service to Visnu service to the world?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Visnu is all pervading. He is the Supreme Brahman, the almighty God. Serving Him means serving everything situated in Him. The servant of a particular horse is not the servant of all horses or all other animals. The servant of a particular country is not a servant of all countries. The servant of a particular time is not the servant of all times. If someone kills a goat or fish and serves his tongue by eating it, then that is one-sided service - he is serving only his own happiness - but the goat or fish is not happy at all. Similarly, if one particular person or country is served, then other persons or countries will be harmed. But when serving Visnu everything is served and everyone becomes pleased. Sn Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s mercy is all-auspicious; and it does not yield any harm. It is beneficial for all persons at all time and in all countries.

**Bardaloi:** Our Sankaradeva’s mood is dasya-rasa and yours is sakhi-bhava.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Sri Caitanyadeva is not just one of any thousands of preachers from one religious sect or another. He did not preach the concocted, imaginary philosophy of mental speculators. Even though other preachers may be eager to speak His teachings, more or less, they cannot properly describe them. Some of these preachers, using imagination and a willingness to concoct, teach a perverted and incomplete imitation of Sri Caitanyadeva’s philosophy. But whatever Sri Caitanyadeva preached about the Absolute Truth, He revealed that same truth in the heart of Brahma at the beginning of creation.

Brahma preached that same truth to others in due course of time. But over time, those teachings were attacked. Even though the real truth, spoken by Brahma, was current among saintly persons through the chain of disciplic succession, the knowledge was transformed in various ways by the influence of the three material modes. The occasional temporary show of dasya by those who do not accept the eternity of Lord Visnu, His servants, or His service, cannot really be called dasya. Rather, it isa completely unauthorized path. On the Vedic path, om tad visnu paramam padam sada pasyanti surayah: Lord Visnu is supreme, eternal, and self-manifest. His many servants are eternal and transcendental, and their propensity to serve Him is also eternal. When pure dasya-rasa is free from all considerations of awe and reverence and is enriched with the attachment of a servant for his master, the affection of a friend for his friend, the excessive affection of a parent for his child, and the love that comes when serving the beloved with everything one has for the beloved’s ultimate pleasure, it is called kanta, or madhurya-rasa.

We and Sri Caitanyadeva’s teachings in an ancient verse like this:

*aradhyo bhagavan vrajesa-tanayas tad-dhama vrndavanam*  
*ramya kacid upasana vraja-vadhu-vargena ya kalpita*  
*srimad-bhagavatam pramanam amalam prema pum-artho mahan*  
*sri-caitanya-mahaprabhor matam idam tatradarah na parah*

**“The Supreme Personality of Godhead, the son of Nanda Maharaja, is to be worshiped along with His transcendental abode, Vrndavana. The most pleasing form of worship for the Lord is that which was performed by the go pis of Vrndavana. Srimad-Bhagavatam is the spotless authority on everything, and pure love of God is the ultimate goal of life for all men. These statements, for which we have the highest regard, are the opinion of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu.”**

**Bardaloi:** You worship Radha with Krsna, but in Sankaradeva’s opinion, only Krsna should be worshiped.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Worship of Krsna without Radha is not worship but pride. It is another unauthorized philosophy nourished by the concept of an impotent God. Krsna is alone the enjoyer; all others are to be enjoyed. The origin of all the enjoyed, or the asrayas (abodes of love) is Sri Radhika. To serve God under the subordination of His servant is the core of Vaisnava philosophy. Other philosophies devoid of subordination to the original shelter, or asray a, are simply covered atheism or some kind of pseudo philosophy.

Because she worships or satisfies Krsna’s desires, she is called “Radhika.” Srimad-Bhagavatam (10.30.28) states:

*anayaradhito nunam bhagavan harir isvarah*  
*yan no vihaya govindah prito yam anayad rahah*

**“Certainly this particular gopi has perfectly worshiped the all-powerful Personality of Godhead, Govinda, since He was so pleased with Her that He abandoned the rest of us and brought Her to a secluded place.”**

Without serving the one who is capable of fulfilling Krsna’s desires, it is not possible to satisfy Krsna’s senses or to serve Him. Unless a person becomes advanced in spiritual life by giving up the desire for material enjoyment, he cannot understand this.

**Bardaloi:** This means that in the course of your worship, you accept that God is a person!

**Srila Prabhupada:** So far, we have discussed that personality is eternal and spiritual variegatedness is eternal. We have also said that material variegatedness is the incomplete, partial, perverted reflection of spiritual variegatedness. Those who imagine that such variegatedness exists only during the course of sadhana \[and not after realization\], and who imagine the material world to be false, are Mayavadis as well as unauthorized. God is eternal and full of spiritual variegatedness. He has eternal names, forms, qualities, pastimes, abodes, and associates. He is the only goal of the five kinds of rasas, or spiritual relationship.

**Bardaloi:** Sankara deva accepts only worship of Krsna. He has for-bidden us to worship the demigods - what to speak of worship them, he forbade us even to see, pray, or take their remnants. Mahapurusiyas do not worship the demigods; they worship only Krsna. The Damodariyas worship the demigods by considering them different forms of the Supreme Lord.

**Srila Prabhupada:** According to the opinion of sadhakanamhitarthaya brahmano rupa kalpana - that the Lord’s form has been “created” through the imagination for the benefit of the practitioners, the temporary forms of the formless Brahman are Surya, Ganapati, Sakti, Siva, and Visnu, and their worship is called the “five-god worship.” In the language of Moksha Mular \[Max Muller\] this is more or less henotheism.It is Mayavada. It is idolatry. To worship that form which is not eternal but which is created, maintained, and destroyed by the strength of the imagination is certainly under the control of matter and is a deity subject to the creation, maintenance, and annihilation of matter. That is, it is an idol. Anything created to facilitate the senses of the living entities cannot be the supremely independent, omnipotent, transcendental personality. What the empiricists imagine to be Visnu’s names and forms, creating them according to their own needs or simply to try to make an intellectual adjustment between matter and spirit, therefore making them objects of their own enjoyment, are never in the mood of serving the Supreme Lord. We are glad to hear of Sankaradeva’s staunch worship of Visnu, but unless the eternal nature of such worship along with the eternal nature of Visnu’s worshipers and of Visnu Himself are accepted, we cannot consider what he was doing as actual service to Visnu. Such worship ends only in becoming another form of Mayavada.

\[After their long discussion, Sriyukta Bardaloi Mahasaya, seeing that Srila Prabhupada had been speaking nonstop since getting off the train, asked him to rest.\]

**Srila Prabhupada:** Speaking hari-katha is real rest - it removes all fatigue. Any endeavor other than kirtana, performed even for a moment, is averse to God. Exalted personalities and their followers always engage in glorifying topics about Hari in all respects. They have no other duty. Sri Caitanyadeva also instructed us, kirtanyah sada harih: always chant the glories of Hari. The symptom of liberated persons is that they only chant the glories of Hari at all times with body, mind, and speech.

## Conversation 6

**Srila Prabhupada and Kumara Sriyukta Saradindu Narayan,**  
**October 17, 1928**

\[During his stay at Shilong, Srila Prabhupada went to the “Age Hill” house of the learned landlord of Dinajapur, Kumara Sriyukta Saradin-du Narayan Roy, M.A. Many disciples accompanied Srila Prabhupada to Kumara Bahadura’s house. After greeting Srila Prabhupada as an acarya, Kumara Sriyukta asked, these questions.)

**Kumara**: Why is the Vaisnava sampradaya, following in the line of Mahaprabhu, called the Madhva-Gaudiya sampradaya? Was Madhvacarya a pure Vaisnava?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Because Gaudiya Vaisnavas are under the shelter of a disciplic succession coming down from Brahma-Vyasa-Madhva, they are known as the Madhva-Gaudiya or Brahma-Madhva-Gaudiya sampradaya. Even though He was the Supreme Absolute Truth, Visnu Himself, Sriman Mahaprabhu enacted the pastime of acting as an acarya and preached the ideal example of following the descending path of aural reception that we call the “disciplic succession.” By enacting the pastime of accepting Sri Isvarapuripada as His spiritual master and thus making Himself subordinate to Sri Madhavendra Puripada, and also having Sri Nityananda and Sri Advaita Prabhu take shelter of Madhavendra Puri, Sri Mahaprabhu established the eternal principle of accepting the path of disciplic succession. The four authorized sampradayas are mentioned in the transcendental literature. By setting the example of accepting one of these four, the Brahma-Madhva sampradaya, Lord Gaurasundara established in this world the honor of the sastras. The teachings and behavior described by sadhu, sastra, and acarya should not contradict one another.

The exalted spiritual masters, Sri Madhavendra Puripada and Sri Isvarapuripada, belong to the Brahma-Madhava sampradaya. By accepting them as their gurus, Mahaprabhu and the His two Prabhus identified the Gaudiyas as followers of the Brahma-Madhva sect. Srimad Anandatirthapada \[Madhvacarya\] was a pure Vaisnava acarya. Gaudiya Vedantacarya Sripada Baladeva Vidyabhusana prayed to Sriman Madhvacarya as follows:

*anandatirtha nama sukhamayadhama yatir jiyat*  
*samsararnava taranim yamiha janah kirtayanti budhah*

**“All glories to Srila Anandatirtha Madhvamuni, who is the abode of transcendental bliss. Learned scholars consider him the boat for crossing beyond the ocean of material existence.”**

**Kumara:** Then why did Mahaprabhu criticize Madhva’s philosophy at Udupi?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Mahaprabhu criticized the opinion of the then Tattvavadis. Even though the Vaisnavas belong to the Madhva sampradaya, they are not Tattvavadis. In order to distinguish the Madhva Vaisnavas from the Sankara Mayavadis, the Madhva Vaisnavas are called Tattvavadis. The Tattvavadi acaryas have established the truth of the Supreme Personality of Godhead by refuting the philosophy of impersonal Brahmavada, nourished by the fallacious argument of monism. In spite of being a follower of Sri Madhva Vaisnavism, Sriman Madhavendra Puri preached loving devotional service to God, which is the ultimate goal of tattvavada, Kavikarnapura, the author of the Gaura-ganoddesa-dipika, and Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami, the author of Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, praised this same Sri Madhavendra Puri as “the middle root of the desire tree of love of God.” The attempt to uproot a main root of a sampradaya comes from the atheistic mentality of those opposed to authorized disciplic succession. Mahaprabhu never transgressed the teachings of Madhvacarya; He simply criticized the Tattvavadi sampradaya’s attachment to fruitive activities.

**Kumara:** Is the revelation of the fifth objective of life special to Sriman Mahaprabhu s teachings and so His own contribution?

**Srila Prabhupada:** It was Mahaprabhu who clearly and fully revealed the fifth objective of life. Acaryas like Sri Ramanuja instructed their followers to make advancement toward the kingdom of devotional service, but they did not disclose the topmost ecstatic love of God one experiences once one has made sufficient advancement in devotional service. The acaryas of the medieval age established theism by refuting atheism. Mahaprabhu accepted their views and helped to make their philosophy perfect. There are many good things in the instructions of Jesus Christ, too, but they have become enlarged and perfected in Mahaprabhu s instructions. Moral instructions and the establishment of theism are not the last word of Mahaprabhu s preaching; rather, He has revealed the highest ecstatic love of God. In other words, all the best advice of all the acaryas is already included in what Mahaprabhu teaches. Because he cannot hear impartially and also because he has not had the opportunity to hear Mahaprabhu’s teachings from genuine Gaudiya Vaisnavas, Mr. Kennedyhas been misguided.

**Kumara:** Had Kennedy heard Mahaprabhu’s teachings from you, he would not have rushed down the wrong path.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Sriyukta Kennedy wrote to me before he wrote his book, but perhaps he thought that our association would not help him find the flow of pure Gaudiya Vaisnava dharma - I can’t say if that is why he abstained from meeting us. One has to wonder whether inside, he just wanted to prove that the contemporary pseudo Vaisnavas and their dharma were authentic so he could expose their defects. For example, Tarkaratna Mahasaya is out to misrepresent Mahaprabhu by dumping the unauthorized smarta philosophy on His shoulders. He remains hidden, but he tries to find faults in Mahaprabhu and His devotees!

**Kumara:** Some Gosvamis seem to have affinity with Smarta philosophy.

**Srila Prabhupada:** By entering the foreign camp, the sons and descendants of the Vaisnava acaryas have also become more or less smartas and henotheists, or five-god worshipers. They too have been influenced by the philosophy of Tarkaratna.

**Kumara:** Yes, you are right. Instead of “acaryas” we should call them “so-called acaryas.” They too worship different demigods like the smartas with their five gods and perform sraddha ceremonies on Ekadasis.

I asked my family priest, “Why do you perform sraddha ceremony for a departed soul on Ekadasis like the smartas do?” He replied, “We have to live in society, so we cannot avoid doing what others want.” I have seen the widows of the gosvamis of Santipur observe an Ekadasi on the day before the Ekadasi \[according to the Smarta system\], while the gosvamis observe it the next day. I had an argument with them about this, but they could not give a satisfactory answer. Perhaps for this reason they do not allow us to associate with your camp. I told them, “Your philosophy is like that of the Pope. Why won’t you allow honestly inquisitive persons to associate with saints? This means you are guilty. Seeing that your guilt wifi be exposed, you forbid us to mix with devotees.” The way you so boldly and publicly preach the impartial truth, challenging the whole world, even in a big city like Calcutta - anyone searching after the real truth can test your impartial truth. The principles of varnasrama as preached by these gosvamis seems to come from their smarta philosophy.

**Srila Prabhupada:** We say that the proper consideration of pure varnasrama dharma cannot be found anywhere else. The characteristics of actual varnasrama-dharma have been ascertained by important texts like Srimad-Bhagavatam, Mahabharata, Sri Hari-bhakti-vilasa, and others. Because the descendants of the acaryas have grown up in the shelter of sheep, despite being lion cubs, they have forgotten their own prowess.

**Kumara:** Yes, you are right. Because they have taken shelter of a foreign camp, a religion contrary to pure Vaisnavism, they have become pathetic. I told them, “If you follow the Smarta system, then your virabhadri thak, or ladder of Virabhadra, will be crushed by the foreign camp. There is no value left in bragging that you belong to an aristocratic family. Many smartas secretly have no respect for the Six Gosvamis.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Varnasrama-dharma as the Vaisnavas understand it is the real, authorized, spiritual varnasrama-dharma. Other than this, all other understandings are demoniac. This is confirmed in the sastra:

*dvau bhuta-sargau loke ’smin daiva asura eva ca*  
*daivo vistarasah prokta asuram partha me srnu*

**“There are two classes of men in the created world: the demoniac and the godly. The devotees of Lord Visnu are the godly. All who are opposed to them are demons.” (Padma Purana)**

Due to bad association, those acarya descendants who have more or less embraced the Smarta system are accepting non-Vaisnava understanding as if they are Vaisnava understandings. They have even raised an objection to the dress of the fourth spiritual order of life, sannyasa. Following in the footsteps of Tarkaratna, they say that accepting sannyasa is prohibited in Kali-yuga.

**Kumara:** How, then, do we reconcile the scriptural statement quoted in Sri Caitanya-caritamrta on this point? The Brahma-vaivarta Purana (Krsna-janma-khanda 4.115.113) verse is as follows:

*asvamedham gavalambham sannyasam pala-paitrkam*  
*devarena sutotpattimkalau panca vivarjayet*

**“In this age of Kali, the following five kinds of karma-kanda practices are forbidden: offering a horse in sacrifice, offering a cow in sacrifice, accepting sannyasa, offering oblations of flesh to the forefathers, and a man begetting children in his brother’s wife.”**

**Srila Prabhupada:** That statement is applicable only in regard to the renunciation of karma. There can be no contradiction between the sastras and the behavior of the mahajanas. Exalted personalities explain and preach the import of scripture to the ordinary people by how they act. All of the four acaryas from the authorized sampradayas have set an ideal example by accepting sannyasa in Kali-yuga. All those whom Sriman Mahaprabhu accepted as His spiritual masters, such as Srila Madhavendra Puri, Sri Paramananda Puri, Sri Isvara Puri, and Sri Kesava Bharati, all set an ideal example by accepting sannyasa in Kali-yuga. What’s more, Mahaprabhu Himself, who is the deliverer of Kali-yuga, enacted the pastime of accepting sannyasa in Kali-yuga.

**Kumara:** Then why didn’t Mahaprabhu’s devotees accept sannyasa?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Most of Mahaprabhu’s devotees accepted the dress of paramahamsas because they were fully absorbed in worshiping the Lord through the raga-marga, They are eternally liberated associates of the Lord and as such were aloof from external obligation. Among Mahaprabhu’s devotees, Sri Vyenkata Bhatta’s brother, a resident of Kamyavana, Srila Prabodhananda Sarasvati, along with Srila Gadadhara Pandita Gosvami, and many others, enacted the pastime of accepting sannyasa.

**Kumara:** Then how do we reconcile the statement, rakta vastra vaisnavera parite na yuyaya: a Vaisnava should not wear red cloth?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Vaisnavas have no need to wear saffron cloth, because Vaisnavas do not belong to any asrama, A Vaisnava, or Paramahamsa, is one who is beyond all varnas and asramas. It is only for the paramahamsas of the raga-marga that there is no hard-and-fast rule about having to wear red cloth. This is because they understand themselves as follows:

*naham vipro na ca nara-patir napi vaisyo na sudro*  
*naham varni na ca grha-patir no vana-stho yatir va*  
*kintu prodyan nikhila-paramananda-purnamptabdher*  
*gopi-bhartuh pada-kamalayor dasa-dasanudasah*

**“I am not a brahmana, I am not a ksatriya, I am not a vaisya or a sudra. Nor am I a brahmacari, a householder, a Vanaprastha, or a sannyasi. I identify Myself only as the servant of the servant of the servant of the lotus feet of Lord Sri Krsna, the maintainer of the gopis. He is an ocean of nectar, and He is the cause of universal transcendental bliss. He is always existing with brilliance.” (Padyavali, verse 74, by Srila Rupa Gosvami, quoted in Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Madhya 13.80)**

Those Vaisnavas who think they belong to different varnas and asramas because of their humility do not imitate the dress of their predecessor spiritual masters. They think, “Are we Vaisnavas or gums? We could not become Vaisnavas; we are only the reflection of the servants of the Vaisnavas!” All members of the varnas and asramas are servants of the paramahamsas. Qualified brahmanas are supposed to serve the maha-bhagavata. This is how they behave “more humble than a blade of grass.” One mahajana said:

*ami ta’vaisnava, e-buddhi ha-ile,*  
*amain na haba ami*  
*pratisthasa asi’, hrdaya dusibe,*  
*ha-iba niraya-gami*

**“If I think I am a Vaisnava, I shall look forward to receiving respect from others. And if the desire for fame and reputation pollute my heart, certainly I shall go to hell.”**

**Kumara:** Who wrote these lines?

**Srila Prabhupada:** They are from Srimad Bhaktivinoda Thakura’s Kalyana Kalpataru.

**Kumara:** This is wonderful statement. I haven’t yet found a copy of the Kalyana Kalpataru.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Very soon you will have access to this book. The Vaisnavas never say, “I am a Vaisnava” or “I am a brahmana,” Therefore the ignorant think that the Vaisnavas are not brahmanas. Actually, only those who are not brahmanas are eager to call themselves brahmanas! The scriptures call such people “so-called brahmanas.” The brahminical qualifications of those who worship the demigods separately \[from the Supreme\] are lost. When one becomes overwhelmed by lamentation, he desires sense gratification and his mind rushes to worship imaginary demigods in order to fulfill that desire. The Bhagavad-gita (7.20-23) says:

*kamais tais fair hrta-jnanah prapadyante ’nya-devatah*  
*tam tam niyamam asthaya prakrya niyatah svaya*  
*yo yo yam yam tanum bhaktah sraddhayarcitum icchati*  
*tasya tasyacalam sraddham tam eva vidadhamy aham*  
*sa taya sraddhaya yuktas tasyaradhanam ihate*  
*labhate ca tatah kaman mayaiva vihitan hi tan*  
*antavat tu phalam tesam tad bhavaty alpa-medhasam*  
*devan deva-yajo yanti mad-bhakta yanti mam api*

**“Those whose intelligence has been stolen by material desires surrender to demigods and follow the particular rules and regulations of worship according to their own natures. I am in everyone’s heart as the Supersoul. As soon as one desires to worship some demigod, I make his faith steady so that he can devote himself to that particular deity. Endowed with such a faith, he endeavors to worship a particular demigod and obtains his desires. But in actuality these benefits are bestowed by Me alone. Men of small intelligence worship the demigods, and their fruits are limited and temporary. Those who worship the demigods go to the planets of the demigods, but My devotees ultimately reach My supreme planet.”**

The brahmanas are not less intelligent; nor has their knowledge been stolen. They are divine and godly. The demigods eternally worship Visnu’s supreme form and abode. This is the Vedic path.

*îm tad visnoh paramam padam sada*  
*pasyanti surayah diviva caksuratatam*

**“Just as those with ordinary vision see the sun’s rays in the sky, so the wise and learned devotees always see the supreme abode of Lord Visnu.”**

**Kumara**: Where is the clear evidence that one’s brahminical qualities are lost when one worships the demigods instead of Lord Visnu?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Srimad-Bhagavatam is the crest jewel of all evidence. Srimad-Bhagavatam (11.5.2-3) states:

*mukha-bahuru-padebhyah purusasyasramaih saha*  
*catvaro jajnire varna gunair vipradayah prthak*  
*óa esam purusam saksad atma-prabhavam isvaram*  
*na bhajanty avajananti sthanad bhrastah patanty adhah*

**“From the mouth of Brahma, the brahminical order has come into existence. Similarly, from his arms the ksatriyas have come, from his waist the Vaisyas have come, and from his legs the sudras have come. These four orders and their spiritual counterparts \[brahmacarya, grhastha, vanaprastha, and sannyasa\] combine to make human society complete. If one simply maintains an official position in the four varnas and asramas but does not worship the Supreme Lord Visnu, he falls down from his puffed-up position into a hellish condition.”**

**Kumara:** They will say, “We do not disregard Visnu; we worship Visnu and we worship Visnu’s other forms as well.”

**Srila Prabhupada:** There is no greater covered opposition to Visnu than this. Only Lord Visnu is fully independent. To consider the demigods, who are subordinate to Lord Visnu, as equal to Visnu, is nothing but aversion to Visnu.

*arcye visnau sila-dhir gurusu nara-matir vaisnave jati-buddhir*  
*visnor va vaisnavanam kali-mala-mathane pada-tirthe ‘mbu-buddhih*  
*sri-visnor namni mantre sakala-kalusa-he sabda-samanya-buddhir*  
*visnau sarvesvarese tad-itara-sama-dhir yasya va naraki sah*

**“One who thinks the Deity in the temple to be made of wood or stone, who thinks of the spiritual master in the disciplic succession as an ordinary man, who thinks the Vaisnava in the Acyuta-gotra to belong to a certain caste or creed, who thinks of caranamrta or Ganges water as ordinary water, or who considers the Hare Krsna maha-mantra to be a material vibration is taken to be a resident of hell.” (Padma Purana)**

*yas tu narayanam devam brahma-rudradi-daivataih*  
*samatvenaiva vikseta sa pasandi bhaved dhruvam*

**“A person who considers demigods like Brahma and Siva to be on an equal level with Narayana is to be considered an offender and an atheist.”**

**Kumara**: Then how do you explain this statement:

*sivasya sri-visnor ya iha guna-namadi-sakalam*  
*dhiya bhinnam pasyet sa khalu hari-namahita-karah*

**“\[It is an offense\] to consider the names of Lord Siva or Lord Brahma to be on an equal level with the holy name of Lord Visnu.”**

**Srila Prabhupada**: This statement refutes polytheism. One who finds a distinction between the all-auspicious holy names, forms, qualities, and pastimes of Lord Visnu, or who thinks that like material objects, Visnu’s names, forms, qualities, and pastimes are different from Visnu, or who sees demigods like Siva as Visnu’s competitors, considering them independent of or nondifferent from Him, cannot chant Visnu’s name. If he does chant the holy name, his chanting is only a pretension of chanting and is offensive to the holy name. This \[offensive mentality\] is certainly very harmful. Only those who have to concoct something about the impersonal Brahman and so consider Visnu’s names, forms, qualities, and pastimes as imaginary and temporary include Lord Visnu as simply one of the five gods or as part of an imaginary group of many gods. This thinking is covered Buddhism, atheism, and is nothing but an attempt to oppose Lord Visnu and the Vaisnavas. It is not Visnu worship. Brahminical qualification cannot live in the attempt to oppose Visnu. Brahmanas accept Lord Visnu’s eternal names, forms, qualities, and pastimes as well as His eternal worship. This is the purport of the Rg Veda mantra. The names of the many demigods are imaginary.

There is a distinction between the demigods’ names and the demigods themselves. There are distinctions between their names, forms, qualities, and pastimes and themselves. But there is no difference between Krsna’s name and Krsna Himself, Krsna’s name and Krsna’s form, Krsna’s name and Krsna’s qualities, and Krsna’s name and Krsna’s pastimes.

**Kumara**: Sound is not eternal?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Transcendental sound vibration is certainly eternal.

**Kumara**: Purva-mimamsaka considers sound temporary.

**Srila Prabhupada**: And Uttara-mimamsaka refutes that claim: tasóa ha va etasya brahmano nama satyam iti - “He is immortal and fearless. He is named Satya, the truth.”

**Kumara**: Kindly explain this to me from the rationalist’s viewpoint.

**Srila Prabhupada**: If you have genuine interest in and the patience to hear impartial truth, then you will understand the eternal nature of symbol and sound. Since the object and its name are different from each other in the world of duality, some people think the same is true of the nondual. When we consider the respective characteristics of maya and Vaikuntha, we understand that the one is full of faults and paucity and the other is free from them. In the faulty kingdom, there is a difference between an object and its “sound” or name. Therefore, in the material sky sound is temporary. But this is not true of the nondual world, Vaikuntha, where the holy name and the owner of that name are one; there is no distinction in Vaikuntha between a name and its object. Trying to confine Vaikuntha within the four walls of this world is like lopping off the branch of the tree on which we are sitting. The nature of sounds and their objects in Vaikuntha is diametrically opposite to the nature of sounds and their objects in the material world.

**Kumara**: Is this true of any sound in this world, even names like Kali, Durga, Ganesa, Surya, and others that are not transcendental names?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Where there is a distinction between the sound - names such as Kali, Durga, Ganesa, etc. - and the actual owners of those names, and when people have concocted temporary natures for the deities they worship, how can those names or persons be transcendental? A transcendental sound and its transcendental object cannot be temporary. For those who think that the names of Kali, Durga, Ganesa, Surya, Siva, and Visnu and their respective forms are temporary \[as impersonalists do\], what is the question of their accepting the name and its owner as transcendental? All these concocted philosophies are different names for pantheism and blasphemy.

**Kumara**: Previously I used to think, “As many opinions as many ways.”

**Srila Prabhupada**: Opinion is nothing but mental speculation. Bhin- na rucirhi lokah: there are as many thoughts and tastes as there are people. The sick have a taste for a strong diet, people with anarthas have a taste for sense gratification, and all the paths that have been invented, are being invented, or will be invented in the future to fulfill all these tastes are not atma-dharma, sanatana-dharma. There have been and will always be countless ways to speculate in this world. But the spotless Purana, Srimad-Bhagavatam (1.2.6) declares:

*sa vai pumsam paro dharmo yato bhaktir adhoksaje*  
*ahaituky apratihata yayatma suprasidati*

**“The supreme occupation \[dharma\] for all humanity is that by which men can attain to loving devotional service unto the transcendent Lord. Such devotional service must be unmotivated and uninterrupted to completely satisfy the self.”**

That is, “as many opinions, as many ways” is born from knowledge acquired through the senses. All these opinions are produced from sensory input, and the ways that follow out of those opinions are favorable to more sensory engagement with the world. But uninterrupted, unmotivated devotional service to the transcendental Personality of Godhead is the supreme religion for all living entities. It is atma-dharma. And the soul becomes completely pleased by performing it. Since other religious opinions and ways bring satisfaction to the material body and mind, sense enjoyers and mental speculators accept them as true. All of this has been vividly described in our Gaudiya magazine. It is very true that one has to hear the actual truth from a living source. Only then can people be ultimately benefited. Otherwise, people can be misguided at every moment.

**Kumara:** There is a personal magnetism in the living source.

**Srila Prabhupada:** A neophyte, a person with tender faith, cannot actually understand worship of the Lord’s murti because he cannot fully give up his conception that the Deity is made of matter. Also, he is not aware of the actual position and characteristics of the Vaisnavas. This is why the madhyama-adhikari devotees always advise neophytes to associate with pure devotees. Without the association of Vaisnavas, human beings can never achieve eternal ultimate benefit, and the worship of the Lord’s murti cannot be performed.

**Kumara:** Our spiritual masters say that Deity worship is a means to an end.

**Srila Prabhupada:** This is also a grave blasphemy. Sri Mahaprabhu says:

*prakrta kariya mane visnu-kalevara*  
*visnu-ninda ara nahi ihara upara*  
*isvarera sri-vigraha sac-cid-anandakara*  
*se-vigrahe kaha sattva-gunera vikara*  
*sri-vigraha ye na mane, sei ta’pasandi*  
*adrsya asprsya, sei haya yama-dandi*

**“One who considers the transcendental body of Lord Visnu to be made of material nature is the greatest offender at the lotus feet of the Lord. There is no greater blasphemy against the Supreme Personality of Godhead. The transcendental form of the Supreme Personality of Godhead is complete in eternity, cognizance and bliss. However, you describe this transcendental form as a product of material goodness. One who does not accept the transcendental form of the Lord is certainly an agnostic. Such a person should be neither seen nor touched. Indeed, he is subject to be punished by Yamaraja.” (Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Adi 7.115 and Madhya 6.166-167)**

Visnu’s form is spiritual. Lord Visnu is not man-made, like the idols of ordinary demigods. Imagining a form is idolatry. We find idolatry described in statements like, sadhakanam hitarthaya brahmano rupa kalpana \[the Lord’s form is imagined for the convenience of the worshiper\]. The Lord’s Deity form is not based on imagination. Pure Vaisnavas are not henotheists. Henotheists are idolaters. Idolators distinguish between the means and the goal. As soon as their needs are met, they break their idols. Every impersonalist imagines the material world to be false, but every Vaisnava acarya concludes that the material world is spiritual truth. The object that we can perceive with our material senses is not Visnu or Krsna (there is no difference in reality between Visnu and Krsna).

**Kumara**: But the sastras seem to distinguish between Krsna and Visnu. Are Narayana, Ramacandra, Matsya, Karma, Varaha, and Sri Krsna all one and the same?

**Srila Prabhupada**: There is no difference in tattva between Them, although there are differences in Their pastimes. But in visnu-tattva, the distinction between the Lord and His form, uilike the case between the jivas and their forms, simply does not exist.

*deha-dehi-vibhedo ’yam nesvare vidyate kvacit*

**“There is no difference between the Supreme Lord’s form and the Supreme Lord Himself.” (Karma Purana)**

*The* Maha-varaha Purana *explains:*

*sarve nityah sasvatas ca dehas tasya paratmanah*  
*hanopadana-rahita naiva prakrti-jah kvacit*

**“The transcendental forms of the Supreme Personality of Godhead are eternal and imperishable. They were not created at a certain point, and they will never be destroyed. They are not products of the material energy.”**

*paramananda-sandoha jnana matras ca sarvatah*  
*sarve sarva-gunaih purnah sarva-dosa-vivarjjiah*

**“All the forms of the Supreme Personality of Godhead are completely filled with transcendental bliss, knowledge, and all-auspicious qualities. These spiritual forms of the Lord are free from all material defects.”**

*manir yatha vibhagena nila-pitadibhir yutah*  
*rupa-bhedam avapnoti dhyana-bhedat tathacyutah*

**“When the jewel known as vaidurya touches other materials, it appears to separate into different colors. Consequently the Lord’s forms also appear to be different. Similarly, according to the meditational ecstasy of the devotee, the Lord, who is known as Acyuta (infallible), appears in different forms, although He is essentially one.”**

*siddhantatas tv abhede ’pi srisa-krsna-svarupayoh*  
*rasenotkrsyate krsna-rupam esa rasa-sthitih*

**“According to transcendental realization, there is no difference between the forms of Krsna and Narayana. Yet in Krsna there is a special transcendental attraction due to the conjugal mellow, and consequently He surpasses Narayana. This is the conclusion of transcendental mellows.” (Sri Caitanya-caritamrta*****, Madhya 9.146)***

This is the provision of the science of rasa Srila Rupa Gosvami Prabhu reveals to us in the Laghu-bhagavatamrta (5.86-90), where he refutes the atheistic view that there is any real difference between Krsna and Narayana other than in the nature of Their pastimes:

*tasmat katham taratamyam tesam vyakhyayate tvaya*  
*atrocyate paresatvat purna yadyapite ’khilah*  
*amsatvam nama saktinam sadalpamsa-prakasita*  
*purnatvam svecchayaiva nana-sakti-prakasita*  
*saktir aisvarya-madhurya krpa-tejo-mukha gunah*  
*sakter vyaktis tathavyaktis taratamyasya karanam*

**“Therefore, why do you say there is a hierarchy of the Lord’s forms? In answer it may be said that because He is the Supreme Lord, all His forms are perfect and complete. When a small measure of His potencies is manifest, His form is called an amsa-avatara. When by His wish many different potencies manifest, His form is called a purna-avatara, Opulence, sweetness, mercy, and strength are prominent among His many potencies. The different forms of the Supreme Lord are considered more or less complete according to the extent to which His various potencies are manifest or not manifest.”**

We have received two important points from Srimad Bhaktivinoda Thakura that mundane sahajiyas cannot comprehend: The first thing he taught is that Rama, Nrsimha, and other incarnations that are known as amsas, or parts of a plenary incarnation, are not incomplete or separate parts, like the portions of things in the world of duality. Those plenary portions have no intrinsic or inherent difference from the complete whole Sri Krsna. Sri Ramacandra, Sri Nrsimha, and Sri Varaha are not different or separate from Sri Krsna. Sri Rama is Sri Krsna. Variety in pastimes or rasa does not constitute a “difference.” It so happened that in 1903, when I used to recite Sri Caitanya-caritamrta before Srimad Bhaktivinoda Thakura, he nicely explained all these things to me.

And his second point is that one need not disregard texts like the Rg Samhita, Mahabharata, or Bhagavad-gita, because they are actually non — different from Srimad-Bhagavatam. Vaisnavas do not become bewildered by the fact that these texts discuss different topics than Srimad-Bhagavatam, topics more suitable for karmis, jnanis, and pseudo devotees, and to bewilder demons. Rather, one needs to search out the enlightened viewpoints of the mantras in the Rg Samhita and other texts.

**Kumara:** Many people call Vaisnava dharma mythological because there is no mention of Vaisnava dharma in the Vedas.

**Srila Prabhupada**: In the Sruti, or Vedas, there is nothing but discussion of Vaisnava dharma! The Rg Samhita, which is unanimously accepted as the oldest text in the world, glorifies Lord Visnu and the Vaisnavas in its beginning, middle, and end. The Vedas glorify Lord Visnu and the Vaisnavas. The subject of Vaisnava dharma is elaborately discussed in texts like the Isa, Kena, Katha, Prasna, and other Upanisads such as the Svetasvatara. Any impartial reader can realize this.

The Svetasvatara Upanisad (6.23) states:

*yasya deve para bhaktir yatha deve tatha gurau*  
*tasyaite kathita hy arthah prakasante mahatmanah*

**“Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.”**

The purport of the Vedas and Upanisads does not manifest in a person who lacks the same staunch devotion for the Vaisnava spiritual master that he has for Lord Visnu. The Mundaka Upanisad (3.2.3) states, yamevaisa vrnute tena labhyah: God loves a man because the man loves Him. It is mutual and reciprocal. Brahma, the grandfather of all beings, Mahadeva the lord of the demigods, sages like Narada, goddesses like Parvatidevi, who is worshiped by the whole universe - all cultivate Vedic Vaisnava dharma.

Srimad-Bhagavatam (1.2.25-29) states:

*bhejire munayo ’thagre bhagavantam adhoksajam*  
*mumuksavo ghora-rupan hitva bhuta-patin atha*  
*narayana-kalah santa bhajanti hy anasuyavah*  
*rajas-tamah-prakrtayah sama-sila bhajanti vai*  
*pitr-bhuta-prajesadin sriyaisvarya-prajepsavah*  
*vasudeva-para veda vasudeva-para makhah*  
*vasudeva-para yoga vasudeva-parah kriyah*  
*vasudeva-param jnanam vasudeva-param tapah*  
*vasudeva-paro dharmo vasudeva-para gatih*

**“Previously all the great sages rendered service unto the Personality of Godhead due to His existence above the three modes of material nature.**

**Those who are serious about liberation are certainly nonenvious, and they respect all. Yet they reject the horrible and ghastly forms of the demigods and worship only the all-blissful forms of Lord Visnu and His plenary portions. Those who are in the modes of passion and ignorance worship those in the same category - namely the forefathers, other living beings, and the demigods who are in charge of cosmic activities - for they are urged by a desire to be materially gifted with women, wealth, power, and progeny. In the revealed scriptures, the ultimate object of knowledge is Sri Krsna, the Personality of Godhead. The purpose of performing sacrifice is to please Him. Yoga is for realizing Him. All fruitive activities are ultimately rewarded by Him only. He is supreme knowledge, and all severe austerities are performed to know Him. Religion \[dharma\] is rendering loving service unto Him. He is the supreme goal of life.”**

The word purana means “the oldest.” That which was present before creation, before the division of the Vedas, and even before the commencement of yugas is called purana. This ancient truth is not separate from the Vedas. That is why the Puranas are called the fifth Veda. Also, Puranas are so called because they are purana or “complements” to the Vedas.

**Kumara**: The language of the Puranas appears modern to me.

**Srila Prabhupada**: When the oldest expressions are lost, then the Puranas are compiled in modern language, making them suitable for the present age. Language is just a dress; the purport or intention of the text is the body. Even when the language was updated, the body was not changed. If someone describes the Supreme Lord by wearing the language-dress of twentieth century civilization, or if Vedic mantras are written with the paper, pen, and ink of the twentieth century, would we say that they cannot be called Vedic mantras? This type of orthodoxy is born of extremely abominable ignorance. One cannot ascertain whether the body is modern or ancient by looking at the age of the dress. The original story of the Rg mantra tredha nidadhe padam \[“I placed three steps”\] is present in the oldest recorded Puranas of the Eocene \[prehistoric\] epoch. But when previous texts become archaic and gradually lost, they were written in a modern comprehensible language.

To think that the sun we see this morning is not the same sun my grandfather saw but is a fresh new sun that has only taken birth today is simply childish. The Puranas are the oldest texts, and they appear and disappear. Those who consider “appearance” birth, and therefore think that the scriptures are modern texts, have been deceived.

For example, there is a racetrack on the land below your home. You can see through the small window in your house a jockey and horse suddenly running before your eyes and then disappearing. Would you think that the moment you saw the jockey and horse was the moment that they were being born, and the moment they disappeared from your sight was the moment of their death? No intelligent persons would think that. They would know that the horse and jockey were running before they came into and view and continued to run once they are out of sight, but that it is not possible to see the whole race from a small window. Similarly, if human beings with imperfect senses try to judge the appearance of the transcendental, sunlike Puranas with their mundane senses and insignificant intelligence, they will surely be misguided. They are deceived if they think what is “ancient” is actually “new” or “modern.”

**Kumara**: I understand. But why then are there so many discussions of demigod and goddess worship and so many different opinions expressed in texts like the Puranas?

**Srila Prabhupada**: There are three kinds of Puranas: those in the mode of goodness, those in the mode of passion, and those in the mode of ignorance. In the Puranas in the mode of ignorance, the process of worshiping ignorance-based, narrow demigods are taught to readers qualified for that. Similarly, in the Puranas in the mode of passion, readers qualified for it are taught how to worship the passion-based demigods. In the Puranas in the mode of goodness, worship of Lord Visnu is described.Srimad-Bhagavatam (1.2.23-24) states:

*sattvam rajas tama iti prakrter gunas fair*  
*yuktah parah purusa eka ihasya dhatte*  
*sthity-adaye hari-virinci-hareti samjnah*  
*sreyamsi tatra khalu sattva-tanor nrnam syuh*

*parthivad daruno dhumas*  
*tasmad agnis trayimayah*  
*tamasas tu rajas tasmat*  
*sattvam yad brahma-darsanam*

**“The transcendental Personality of Godhead is indirectly associated with the three modes of material nature, namely passion, goodness, and ignorance, and just for the material world’s creation, maintenance, and destruction He accepts the three qualitative forms of Brahma, Visnu, and Siva. Of these three, all human beings can derive ultimate benefit from Visnu, the form of the quality of goodness.**

**“Firewood is a transformation of earth, but smoke is better than the raw wood. And fire is still better, for by fire we can derive the benefits of superior knowledge \[through Vedic sacrifices\]. Similarly, passion \[rajas\] is better than ignorance \[tamas\], but goodness \[sattva*****\]*** **is best because by goodness one can come to realize the Absolute Truth.”**

**Kumara:** Discussions of demigod worship are also found in the sattvika Puranas?

**Srila Prabhupada:** The sattvika Puranas prohibit worship of the demigods as independent agents. In other words, these Puranas conclude that Lord Visnu is the supremely independent Personality of Godhead, and the demigods are His subordinate parts and parcels. The demigods are Lord Visnu’s secretaries, entrusted with the responsibility of carrying out various universal affairs. To worship them as subordinates of Lord Visnu is the proper way to worship them.

The Bhagavad-gita (9.23) states:

*ye ’py anya-devata-bhakta yajante sraddhayanvitah*  
*te ’pi mam eva kaunteya yajanty avidhi-purvakam*

**“Those who are devotees of other gods and who worship them with faith actually worship only Me, Î son of Kunti, but they do so in a wrong way.”**

**Kumara:** But why are there differences of opinion among various spiritual texts?

**Srila Prabhupada:** One sees conflict when one views these apparently opposing opinions with one’s material vision, but in the spiritual or enlightened understanding, perfect harmony and resolution is found to all such conflicts. In order to bewilder the demons and to protect the confidential treasury from unauthorized people, a contradictory picture is painted from the outside. This only reflects the author’s intention to maintain the confidential nature of scripture. Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami writes in Sri Caitanya-caritamrta (Adi 4.236):

*ye lagi kahite bhaya, se yadi na jane*  
*iha va-i kiba sukha ache tribhuvane*

**“For fear of them I do not wish to speak, but if they do not understand, then what can be happier in all the three worlds?”**

In some Puranas there are discussions of mixed goodness, but Srimad-Bhagavatam is the spotless Purana and is fully transcendental to the material modes of nature. Since the topics about the transcendental personality, Lord Visnu, who is the source of goodness, and the transcendental topics of Sri Krsna, who is origin of Lord Visnu, are described in Srimad-Bhagavatam, it is the embodiment of pure goodness - the direct incarnation of the Supreme Lord. Srimad-Bhagavatam (1.3.40—42) states:

*idam bhagavatam nama puranam brahma-sammitam*  
*uttama-sloka-caritam cakara bhagavan rsih*  
*nihsreyasaya lokasya dhanyam svasty-ayanam mahat*  
*tad idam grahayam asa sutam atmavatam varam*  
*sarva-vedetihasanam saram saram samuddhrtam*  
*sa tu samsravayam asa maharajam pariksitam*  
*prayopavistam gangayam paritam paramarsibhih*

**“This scripture named Srimad-Bhagavatam is the literary incarnation of God, and it is compiled by Srila Vyasadeva, the incarnation of God. It is meant for the ultimate good of all people, and it is all-successful, all-blissful, and all-perfect. Sri Vyasadeva delivered it to his son, who is the most respected among the self-realized, after extracting the cream of all Vedic literatures and histories of the universe. Sukadeva Gosvami, the son of Vyasadeva, in his turn delivered the Bhagavatam to the great Emperor Pariksit, who sat surrounded by sages on the bank of the Ganges, awaiting death without taking food or drink.”**

*lokasyajanato vidvams cakre satvata-samhitam*

**“But the mass of people do not know this that the material miseries can be mitigated by devotional service and therefore the learned Vyasadeva compiled this Vedic literature, which is in relation to the Supreme Truth.” (Srimad-Bhagavatam** ***1.7.6)***

*yasyam vai sruyamanayam krsne parama-puruse*  
*bhaktir utpadyate pumsah soka-moha-bhayapaha*

**“Simply by giving aural reception to this Vedic literature, the feeling for loving devotional service to Lord Krsna, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, sprouts up at once to extinguish the fire of lamentation, illusion, and fearfulness.” (Srimad-Bhagavatam 1.7.7)**

*sarva-vedanta-saram hi sri-bhagavatam isyate*  
*tad-rasamrta- trptasya nanyatra syad ratih kvacit*

**“Srimad-Bhagavatam is declared to be the essence of all Vedanta philosophy. One who has felt satisfaction from its nectarean mellow will never be attracted to any other literature.” (Srimad-Bhagavatam 12.13.15)**

*srimad-bhagavatam puranam amalam yad vaisnavanam priyam*  
*yasmin paramahamsyam ekam amalam jhanam param giyate*  
*tatra jhana-viraga-bhakti-sahitam naiskarmyam aviskrtam*  
*tac chrnvan su-pathan vicarana-paro bhaktya vimucyen narah*

**“Srimad-Bhagavatam is the spotless Purana. It is most dear to the Vaisnavas because it describes the pure and supreme knowledge of the paramahamsas. This Bhagavatam reveals the means for becoming free from all material work, together with the processes of transcendental knowledge, renunciation, and devotion. Anyone who seriously tries to understand Srimad-Bhagavatam, who properly hears and chants it with devotion, becomes completely liberated.” (Srimad-Bhagavatam 12.13.18)**

**Kumara:** Now it seems I will have unlearn whatever I have learned in my life.

**Srila Prabhupada:** By hearing from the book bhagavata and the devotee bhagavata, a great revolution takes place in life. All previous histories, experiences, and conclusions are dismantled, and in their place is established a strong foundation on which one can cultivate God consciousness. This is called diksa, or the transmission of transcendental knowledge.

**Kumara:** Has Babu changed his religion?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Babu has been given a responsibility. It is necessary to know everything in detail about the internal matters of the followers of other religions, so he has accepted the guise of that religion. Unless one becomes Peter the Great, no one can excel in a shipping company in Russia. That is why even the princes of the Western countries take lessons \[from the Russians\] in navigation.

One person has taken the responsibility to find out how to propagate pure devotional service, as Mahaprabhu taught it, in Europe. This has created some doubts in the minds of some gosvamis and others. During the last Ratha-yatra at Puri, the King of Kashimbazar, Sriyukta Manindra Candra Nandi, kindly came to meet me alone with his personal bodyguard. I gave him the same answer - to which he said, “You are always trying in various ways to preach Mahaprabhu’s teachings.”

**Kumara**: The family descendants of the spiritual master of the King of Kashimbazar follow and support Gauranga-nagarivada. Gauranga-nagarivada is modern and not approved by the Six Gosvamis.

**Srila Prabhupada**: Gauranga-nagarivada is born from the same foolishness as the mood of trying to enjoy Gaura. You will see in the Gaudiya how we have smashed to pieces the philosophy of Gauranga-nagarivada in the protest against the article written by the late Sarvabhauma Madhusudana Gosvami. The other day, I told the King of Kashimbazar that we should let intelligent persons read the Bible very nicely. Caitanyadeva’s teachings are so great that all other topics of all the great persons of the world are and will always be dim before them. There is no doubt about this. All scriptures, all great personalities, all acaryas, and all the world’s preachers have more or less tried to describe the Lord, but they could not do it properly. If weakhearted persons cannot understand this supreme truth and, while denying the brilliance of the midday sun think Caitanyadeva’s teachings narrow or insignificant, they will be cheated. Let them become degraded as the price of their foolishness.

**Kumara**: This is very nice. I am becoming more and more surprised, hearing your words. It is you who is really preaching the pure teachings of Mahaprabhu to the educated society by protecting the Vaisnava society from the stain of the Neda-Nedis.

**Srila Prabhupada**: Our job is not reformation but reestablishment. It is not for us to reform the pure, eternal religion preached by Sri Mahaprabhu, but as the shoe-carriers of His servants our duty is to reestablish that lost religion. It is certainly our duty to deliver the Gaudiya Vaisnava dharma from the unlawful stain imposed on it by foolish people. It is true that I am insignificant, foolish, abominable, more sinful than Jagai and Madhai, wretched, fallen, and a dog-eater, but my Vaisnava spiritual masters are not insignificant, foolish, fallen, dog-eating sinful wretches; they are greater than the greatest. Mundane sahajiyas have forgotten these things. They make a show of presenting themselves as being lower than a blade of grass, but internally, working hard to maintain their strong pride, they place their spiritual master in the same category as the fallen, wretched dog-eaters. Therefore, they have no power to protest against those who are envious.

My spiritual master was a wonderful person. By his mercy, his principles entered into my head a little. He lived next to the public latrine in Navadvipa City, where people passed stool and urine, thinking, “Let the materialistic human beings pass stool on me.” He told me many times, “Worship Hari by deceiving other people.” We had the good fortune to sit by the lotus feet of such a great spiritual master.

**Kumara:** Has his biography been published?

**Srila Prabhupada:** We have partially described his characteristics in Sajjana Tosani and the Gaudiya. He did not present himself like a babaji of that adulterous society that dresses only in a loincloth. Gosvamis did not have a clue about him. Once, a hired, professional gosvami visited his cottage and made great supplications, but after the gosvami left, my spiritual master had the place where he had sat dug up and smeared with cow dung.

**Kumara:** So far we were under the impression that gosvami means one who comes from a seminal gosvami family, but you have revealed through scripture that the word refers to a self-controlled, exalted personality.

**Srila Prabhupada:** That is what Sri Rupa Gosvami Prabhu writes in *the* Upadesamrta *(1):*

*vaco vegam manasah krodha-vegam*  
*jihva-vegam udaropastha-vegam*  
*etan vegan yo visaheta dhirah*  
*sarvam apimam prthivim sa sisyat*

**“A sober person who can tolerate the urge to speak, the mind’s demands, the actions of anger, and the urges of the tongue, belly, and genitals is qualified to make disciples all over the world.”**

**Kumara**: Where is tridanda discussed?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Tridanda is discussed in various places in texts like the Javalopanisad, Harita Samhita, Manu Samhita, Sattvata Samhita, Skanda Purana, Padma Purana, Srimad-Bhagavatam, Caitanya-caritamrta, Upadesamrta, Ekadasi tattva, Bhavartha Dipika, and so on. Sri Visnusvami, Sridhara Svami, and the Vaisnava acaryas of the Sri sampradaya, such as Sri Ramanuja, have all glorified tridanda sannyasa.

**Kumara**: Is it mentioned in both Srimad-Bhagavatam and Sri Caitanya-caritamrta?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Yes. The songs of the tridandi-bhiksu of Avanti-nagara are described in the twenty-third chapter of the Eleventh Canto of Srimad-Bhagavatam. We have visited the place of that tridandi-bhiksu at Avanti and have a plan to establish a Tridandi Matha there.

**Kumara:** Where does it talk about tridanda in Sri Caitanya-caritamrta

**Srila Prabhupada**: Soon after Mahaprabhu accepted sannyasa, He displayed the ideal of thinking Himself a tridandi by wandering around Radhadesa for three days while singing the tridandi-bhiksu-giti.

*prabhu kahe—sadhu ei bhiksura vacana*  
*mukunda sevana-vrata kaila nirdharana*  
*paratma-nistha-matra vesa-dharana*  
*mukunda-sevaya haya samsara-tarana*  
*sei vesa kaila, ebe vrndavana giya*  
*krsna-nisevana kari nibhrte vasiya*

**“Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu approved the purport of this verse on account of the determination of the mendicant devotee to engage in the service of Lord Mukunda. He gave His approval of this verse, indicating that it was very good. The real purpose of accepting sannyasa is to dedicate oneself to the service of Mukunda. By serving Mukunda, one can actually be liberated from the bondage of material existence. After accepting the sannyasa order, Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu decided to go to Vrndavana and engage Himself wholly and solely in the service of Mukunda in a solitary place.” (Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Madhya 3.7-9)**

Only those who have controlled their body, mind, and speech by engaging them in the service of the Supreme Lord are tridandis and gosvamis.

*vagda do ’tha manodandah kaya-dandas tathaiva ca*  
*yasyaite nihita buddhau tridanditi sa ucyate*

**“One is called a true tridandi in whose mind these three - control over his speech, control over his thoughts, and control over his body - are firmly fixed.” (Manu-samhita 12.10)**

*ridanda-bhrd-yo hi prthak samacarecchanaih sanair-yastu ba-*  
*hir-mukhaksah san-mucya samsara-samasta-bandhanat sa yati*  
*visnoramrtatmanah padam*

**“The tridandi sannyasa gradually withdraws his senses from any connection with sight, sound, taste, smell, etc., and becomes indifferent. His mind is gradually freed from any trace of involvement with material enjoyment, and his behavior becomes similarly pure. Such a sannyasi has freed himself from family bondage and, becoming liberated, he tastes the nectar of spiritual bliss at the lotus feet of Sri Visnu.” (Harita-samhita 6.23)**

In the Ekadasi Tattva, Raghunandana Bhattacarya quotes a verse from the smrti:

*devata-pratimam drstva yatincaiva tridandinam*  
*namaskaram na kuryacet upavasena suddhati*

**“After seeing the deity of the demigods and a tridandi sannyasi, if one does not offer his obeisances, then he has to fast one day to be purified of his sin.”**

**Kumara**: Texts like the Javalopanisad are the favorites of Sankaracarya.
