**Srila Prabhupada**: Does it mean that just because Sankaracarya wrote a commentary on the Vedas that the Vedas are now Ms texts? Nowadays, even Moksa Mular \[Max Muller\], the late Rameshcandra Dutta Mahasaya, and others have written explanations on the Vedas. Does it mean we have to say that the Vedas belong to these mlecchas and sudras? Sewage water and Ganga water are separate; one is impure and untouchable and the other supremely sanctified and capable of purifying everything it touches. But when sewage water enters the Ganges, then no one can see the difference between them. If one has a sincere desire to serve the Supreme Lord, then he can serve Him with each and every object. And if there is duplicity in his heart, then instead of serving the Salagrama-sila he will dare to steal the salagrama-sila’s thread or use the Salagrama-Sila to break nuts. Even if one is from Kamaskatka \[Scandanavia\], if his atheistic mentality is removed and he is made to engage in the service of the Supreme Lord, will we be forced to reject him?

As servants of Mahaprabhu we will preach the holy name of Mahaprabhu all over the world. Mahaprabhu said:

*prthivite ache yata nagaradi grama*  
*sarvatra pracara haibe mora nama*

**“In all the villages and towns all over the world, everywhere, this sankirtana movement will be preached.” (Sri Caitanya-bhagavata, Antya 4.126)**

Now imagine if the great instructor Mahasaya or his followers do not allow us to cross the ocean, or think that by crossing the ocean we will lose our caste. Will the preachers of devotional service to the Supreme Lord not propagate Mahaprabhu s teachings? Will they abstain from Mahaprabhu s mission and not follow His orders? Vaisnavas do not have the same concerns as attached, fruitive smartas. The smartas would never inquire about a Hindu festival from a Muslim. Vaisnavas, however, are independent in their desire to serve.

Mahaprabhu converted everyone to Vaisnavism. He made Buddhists, Pathans, Moghuls, smartas, and others give up their contrary religious systems; He brought about a spiritual change within them by initiating them into eternal bhagavata-dharma. Sri Caitanya is Godhead par excellence. Few people around the world know this fact. I will do whatever it takes so that gradually, the people of the whole world can know Sri Caitanya s teachings.

Very soon, fifty lakhs of people are coming who will preach Mahaprabhu’s teachings all over the world. Sri Caitanyadeva established bhagavata-dharma by answering the questions of innumerable philosophers. Moreover, we are trying to solve the questions put forth by the newly concocted unauthorized philosophies opposed to Sri Caitanya by following in the footsteps of Sri Caitanyadeva.

Once, a few smartas attacked Yamunacarya, who in turn smashed their philosophy by quoting evidence from the Vedic literature. Just the other day, Pandita Madanamohana Malaviya, Piyush Babu, and the son of the great instructor, Pancanana Tarkaratna Mahasaya, whose name is Sriyukta Jiva Nyaya Tirtha, M.A., came to Sri Gaudiya Matha, and I had the opportunity to explain to them certain Vedic evidence.

**Kumara**: The great instructor Pancanana Tarkaratna Mahasaya once said, sakta eva dvijah sarve: only saktas are brahmanas. On another occasion I found a book in Kasi that explained how Ramacandra and Sri Krsna are ksatriyas and therefore cannot be an object of worship for brahmanas.

**Srila Prabhupada**: Sakta eva dvijah sarve - this is correct. But not pseudo saktas, who are interested only in bananas, fruits, and roots, or who eat the meat of goats, thinking it Devi prasada. Those saktas who are subordinate to the gopis, those pure saktas who are servants of the Lord’s internal spiritual potency, are the real brahmanas. So this statement is in our favor.

Pure saktas, who worship with the Kama Gayatri mantra are not so- called brahmanas but Vaisnavas. Brahmanism is included in Vaisnavism. Pure spiritual brahmanism belongs only to them. According to the smrti:

*asuddhah sudra-kalpa hi brahmanah kali-sambhavah*  
*tesam agama-margena suddhir na srota-vartmana*

**“The brahmanas born in the Age of Kali are merely sudras. Their so- called Vedic path of karma is polluted and cannot purify them. They can only be purified by following the path of the Agamas or Pancaratrika-vid-dhi.” (Visnu Yamala, quoted in Sri Hari-bhakti-vilasa 5.5)**

*yo va etad aksaram gargy aviditvasmal lokat praiti sa krpanah*  
*ya etad aksaram gargi viditvasmal lokat praiti sa brahmanah*

**“He is a miserly man who does not solve the problems of life as a human and who thus quits this world like the cats and dogs, without understanding the science of self-realization. He is a wise brahmana who leaves this world knowing the solution to life’s problems.” (Brhad-aranyaka Upanisad 3.8.10)**

*na sudra bhagavad-bhaktas tesam bhagavata matah*  
*sarva-varnesu te sudra ye na bhakta janardane*

**“A devotee should never be considered a sudra. All the devotees of the Supreme Personality of Godhead should be recognized as bhagavatas. If one is not a devotee of Lord Krsna, however, even if born in a brahmana, ksatriya or Vaisya family, he should be considered a sudra.” (Padma Purana)**

*brahmananam sahasrebhyah satrayaji visisyate*  
*satrayaji-sahasrebhyah sarvavedanta-paragah*  
*sarva-vedanta-vit-kotya visnu-bhakto visisyate*  
*vaisnavanam sahasrebhyah ekantyeko visisyate*

**“It is said that out of thousands of brahmanas, one is qualified to perform sacrifices, and out of many thousands of such qualified brahmanas expert in sacrificial offerings, one learned brahmana may have passed beyond all Vedic knowledge. He is considered the best among all these brahmanas. And yet, out of thousands of such brahmanas who have surpassed Vedic knowledge, one person may be a visnu-bhakta, and he is most famous. Out of many thousands of such Vaisnavas, one who is completely fixed in the service of Lord Krsna is most famous. Indeed, a person who is completely devoted to the service of the Lord certainly returns home, back to Godhead.” (Hari-bhakti-vilasa 10.117 and Bhakti-sandarbha 177)**

The attempt to make demigods and ordinary humans equal to Lord Visnu and Vaisnavas is a hellish mentality. The east is not the mother of the sun even though the sun rises from the east. Similarly, no particular family or caste is the cause of the appearance of Lord Visnu and His pure Vaisnavas. To call Ramacandra, Sri Krsna, Matsya, Kurma, Varaha, Nrsimha, and other avataras ksatriyas, or Matsya, Kurma, and Varaha animals, or Srila Thakura Haridasa, Srila Raghunatha dasa Gosvami Prabhu, Srila Sanatana Gosvami Prabhu, Sri Bhatta Raghunatha Gosvami Prabhu, Srila Uddharana Datta Thakura, Srila Narottama Prabhu, Srila Syamananda Prabhu, and similar Vaisnavas Muslims or kayasthas or goldsmiths or any other term denoting a connection with a mundane seminal family, is foolish and atheistic. Those who know the nagna-matrka-nyaya (logic of the naked mother) should not have the audacity to consider any Vaisnava a mleccha, sudra, or worldly fruitive brahmana.

Nagna-matrka-nyaya goes like this: Someone’s mother used to run naked as a child when she lived at her father’s house, but when she learned to wear a sari, got married, and had children, if her son, reminiscing about his mother’s childhood, calls her a “naked woman,” it is a ridiculous statement among the civilized. Similarly, if someone judges a duly initiated devotee according to the position he had before his spiritual initiation, then learned people will condemn that attempt to equate the Vaisnava with his mundane past.

**Kumara**: You are the only one who is propagating Mahaprabhu’s transcendental religion of love of God among learned persons by refuting philosophies like the attached fruitive smarta ideas, mayavada, and so on, all of which are opposed to bhagavata-dharma. Today, our lives have become glorious. The foot dust from a great personality like you has decorated this small house of someone as fallen and materialistic as us. Today my house has become sanctified and transformed into a holy place. We are greatly honored. “I came to scoff, but remained to pray.”

## Conversation 7

**Srila Prabhupada and Kumara Prajna, Mr. Singh, Mr. Sen, and others**  
**October 24, 1928**

**Kumara**: What is the difference between the methods of worship adopted by the Saktas and that of the Vaisnavas?

**Srila Prabhupada**: The Vaisnavas consider the Supreme Lord the son. The Supreme Lord can never be the mother figure. Although the Supreme Lord contains the mother figure, Mother Nature, service to the mother is not the same as service to the Supreme. Wherever there is a relationship between father and mother, there has to be a tinge of matter in it. The fatherhood of God that Christ accepts is imbued with awe, reverence, and a sense of duty. It can be compared to dasya-rasa. But Srimad-Bhagavatam teaches a much higher conception than that. Srimad-Bhagavatam teaches that God Himself accepts the role of the son.

**Kumara**: The great instructor Pancanana Tarkaratna Mahasaya tried to establish Sakta philosophy from the janmadyasya yatah verse in the Vedanta-sutra.

**Srila Prabhupada**: From beginning to end, the Brahma-sutra refutes Sakta philosophy. In that first verse of Srimad-Bhagavatam, the word atha means “thereafter,” athah means “therefore,” and brahma jijnasa means “inquiry about the Almighty.” In other words, when all one’s other inquiries about limited material objects are finished, or when interest in the karma-phala propounded by the Purva-mimamsaka sage Jaimini (which Jaimini calls dharma jijnasa) is finished, then one’s brahma-jijnasa - inquiry into the nature of the Supreme Lord - can begin. The word brahma means “great.” He is great because everything emanates from Him.

The Brahma-samhita glorifies the Supreme Lord as “the cause of all causes.” He is the source of Sakti, the material energy; Sakti emanates from Him. That complete energetic Personality of Godhead, He who is the possessor of all energies, is called para brahma. This fact is explained in the Sankarsana-sutra, which states that the word brahma refers to Lord Visnu. In our practical experience, we see three things in the course of time: creation, maintenance, and annihilation. If an object comes into existence before my eyes, we call it the “birth” of that object; when that object exists for some time, we call that state “maintenance”; and when that object dissolves, we call it the object’s “annihilation.”

**Mr. Â. K. Sen**: Then everything we see is finite?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Yes. Every object in space and time is finite. But when an object beyond all space and time appears in this world out of its inconceivable power and for its own reason - appearing to ordinary people as if it belongs to space and time - and then we consider that eternal object temporary and finite, then our understanding is faulty. Although the object “Krsna” may appear as if He belongs to material time and place, as if He is subject to creation, maintenance, annihiliation, He is not. Krsna existed before the material creation, and time, in fact, emanates from Him. He is not subject to time. Neither is He ever annihilated. Rather, He continues to exist after even an unlimited amount of time.

For example, we can see the racetrack below Kumara Bahadur’s house. If a jockey runs on this track and we see him only through a small window, what will we see? If we think that when the jockey comes before our vision he is taking birth, while he remains within our vision he is alive, and once he has moved past our vision he has been annihilated, we will be wrong.

**Mr. Â. K. Sen**: Does this mean the Supreme Lord has no birth?

**Srila Prabhupada**: The Supreme Lord is self-manifest. A sunrise is not the same as the sun’s birth. The sun rises in the east, but that does not make the east the sun’s mother.

**Kumara**: Returning to the question of the Saktas, why can’t the cause of all causes be the mother? Isn’t everything emanating from Sakti, or energy?

**Srila Prabhupada**: The utpattya-sambhavadhikarana section of Vedanta refutes this sakteya philosophy. Women cannot produce offspring on their own. They must have a power connection with men.

**Kumara**: This may be true when it comes to the conceivable matter in this conceivable world, but why can’t there be an exception when it comes to the inconceivable matter of the inconceivable world - for God?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Whatever exists in the inconceivable world as complete, most relishable, and unchangeable, is present in this conceivable world as incomplete, abominable, and perverted. If we consider this subject from another side, we can see that if an object lacks a particular ingredient, it cannot produce out of itself what it is lacking. We can try thousands of times, but we cannot churn yogurt out of water. Water can only be turned into ice. It is milk that is transformed into yogurt.

Sakti, or energy, does not have the capacity to be the cause of all causes. That energetic Personality under whom all energies rest is the cause of all causes. We can talk about flowers in the sky, the horns on hares, or hairs on tortoises, but flowers never grow in the sky, hares do not grow horns, and tortoises are hairless. To say that Sakti the cause of the universe is like talking about something imaginary. No one can proceed in the rational world by going against the theory of cause and effect.

Investigation of a prime cause is done through one of two systems. The first is through the principle of chaos, and the second is through absolute knowledge. The first requires an empirical leap into the dark, and the second means searching for the Absolute Truth using the descending method.

**Yogen Babu:** Why can’t we discover the prime cause through empiricism? Can’t we go on considering and concluding “not this,” “not that” until we finally arrive at the prime cause?

**Srila Prabbupada:** That which the Vedanta calls asya \[“of the manifested universes”\] is the world of sense perception, or conceivable matter. Different perceptions in this world, such as the “vibgyor” colors,or sounds based on alphabets like Ṁ, Â, C, D or alpha, beta, gamma, delta, or ka, kha, ga, gha, have the capacity to dominate our eyes and ears. They can delude us at every moment - they have the power to do so. In philosophical language this is called the covering potency of Mayadevr. The covering potency covers our senses. Maya has another potency, called the repelling energy. In philosophical language this is called the throwing potency. Both of these potencies can distract us from absolute knowledge by covering our senses.

Every object in the material world is invested with the power to delude and distract. In this world, one person sees what another person shows. Our senses act like a mother, and the visible objects act like a father. When the mother and father combine - that is, when the senses and their sense objects combine - children, in the form of material knowledge, are produced. These children are all mundane. This is empiricism. With the help of this incomplete knowledge, one cannot possibly search after the complete whole, the prime cause, the cause of all causes.

Whatever we see in this world - we should know that those objects are mastering us, controlling us. In other words, the objects become the master and our senses their servants. But those who receive the light of absolute knowledge from the prime cause, do not find that their senses are slaves to sense objects; such persons are not deceived by the objects of the senses. That is, Maya s covering and throwing potencies do not rule them. This is why they are able to see everything as Krsna or related to Krsna - chairs, couches, tables, elephants, horses, houses... they see all of these and everything else as related to Krsna. What this means is that they see everything as something to be used for Krsna s service. In this way, every atom has a part in Krsna’s pastimes.

**Kumara:** Then how did Sakta philosophy become so prominent?

**Srila Prabhupada:** This material world has emanated from the sak- timan Supreme Lord Visnu s external energy. It is called Devidhama. Therefore Srimad-Bhagavatam (10.1.25) says, visnor maya bhagavati yaya sammohitam jagat: “The potency of the Lord, known as visnu-maya, who is as good as the Supreme Personality of Godhead, will also appear with Lord Krsna. This potency, acting in different capacities, captivates all the worlds, both material and spiritual.”

Mahamaya Durga is the predominating goddess of this place. Mahamaya has tempted everyone in the prison house of this material world by providing them various sucking toys. Therefore, the inhabitants of this place worship her.

The Sakta philosophy is part of Saivism. Rudra is the lord of destruction. Saivism is ultimately impersonal, or something the Buddhists define as voidism. Saktism is little more developed than Buddhism. But Saktism was born from a thirst for sense enjoyment. I may think, “I will enjoy him,” and he may think, “He will enjoy me.” As we try to enjoy intoxication, the intoxication begins to control us. Intoxication then becomes our master; our senses are enslaved by it. The enjoyer-master then dances wildly, riding me. This is how the ideas inherent in Sakta philosophy were born.

Only when we can search for the root of truth using a proper method can we expect a proper result.

The external world misguides us into thinking we are its enjoyers. It does this by presenting itself as the object of our enjoyment. Since the external world provided my gross body and mind, I call the world “mother.” Moreover, as I demand different things from this mother, I turn her into my lover and thus lose my so-called worshipable mood. But even as I am trying to become the enjoyer, I end up being enjoyed by material nature. Material nature then becomes the enjoyer and my senses the enjoyed. This kind of enjoyer- enjoyed mentality is born from the living beings’ attempt to find pleasure.

**Kumara**: How can we be saved from all these philosophies?

**Srila Prabhupada**: All these philosophies are different types of mental speculation. We can save ourselves from them by receiving the mantra.

**Kumara**: Many of us accept or have accepted mantras, but our tendency to speculate has not been vanquished.

**Srila Prabhupada**: We do not receive mantra. Mantra does not mean something blown from the mouth or a whispering in the ear. Mantra diksa means the receipt of transcendental knowledge. Transcendental knowledge smashes down to their foundation all our seemingly beautiful philosophical mansions, built on empiricism, and erects in their place an actual foundation of eternal transcendental knowledge, cleansing the ground of all contaminations. When the Supreme Lord taught Brahma such transcendental knowledge, He said:

*yavan aham yathabhavo yadrupa gunakarmakah*  
*tathaiva tattva vijnanam astu te mad-anugrahat*

**“All of Me, namely My actual eternal form and My transcendental existence, color, qualities, and activities - let all be awakened within you by factual realization, out of My causeless mercy.” (Srimad-Bhagavatam 2.9.32)**

In other words, the Supreme Lord told Brahma: “I am the Absolute Truth. This Absolute Truth is manifested through My energy, and that energy is the spiritual master.” Many agents and messengers come to this world, but that most powerful messenger, sent by God to suit the adaptability of each recipient - the name of that sole agency is “guru.” Revelation is made possible through that expert. He can free us of mental speculation and bring about within us a revolution of natural spiritual propensity.

**Yogen Babu**: We can use our own knowledge to find the Absolute Truth. Why do we need to gain knowledge from others?

**Srila Prabhupada:** This means you have not heard with full attention whatever we have discussed so far. For those who want to depend on their sensory knowledge I can cite an example. Once upon a time, a person decided to perform penance in a mountain cave, so he collected a big chunk of stone and closed the entrance of the cave to keep out tigers and other wild animals. But it did not occur to him that after fasting and practicing penance for some days that he would become too weak to move the heavy stone and get out of the cave.

Our confidence in our own sensory knowledge is as one-sided as that man’s considerations. In order to save ourselves from the teeth of wild animals, we follow the dictates of our sensory knowledge and ultimately invite our death. Those who adhere only to the knowledge they have gathered with their material senses become killers of the self.

The knowledge one receives from the spiritual master is not material knowledge but transcendental, spiritual knowledge. That knowledge does not belong to mankind or any particular thoughtful or intellectual person of this world. It is, rather, absolute knowledge, nondifferent from Krsna.

**Kumara**: Why is the Brahma-sutra called sariraka?

**Srila Prabhupada:** The Supreme Lord has two bodies or forms. One is an external form and the other is an internal form. Since the Supreme Brahman has two forms (bodies), the Vedanta-sutra, which is nondifferent from Him, is called sariraka. Those who worship the impersonal Brahman are bewildered by the influence of the external body of the sariraka, but those who meditate on Him as satyam param see the spiritual variegatedness in the Supreme Brahman’s internal form.

**Kumara**: Radhika is also called an energy (sakti). What is the difference between Radha and saktis like Durgadevi?

**Srila Prabhupada**: Srimati Radhika, the daughter of Vrsabhanu, is the principal sakti, and Durgadevi, who is the creator of the material universe, is the inferior sakti. Durgadevi is the energy that directs the Supreme Lord’s external form. For those who are either busy measuring everything with the senses or who are not interested in discussions of the transcendental object, Mahamaya uses her covering and throwing potencies. She keeps the spiritual sky away from the sight of empiricists.

The living entities are engaged in those activities by which Mahadeva can maintain Mahamaya’s activities. Mahadeva is the lord of destruction, and Brahma is the lord of creation. One should never ascribe motherhood to the Godhead. Durgadevi’s motherhood is dependent on Lord Visnu’s fatherhood. All mothers store the properties given by the father. The mother has no property; her duty is to nurture.

**Kumara**: Christians attribute parenthood to God. What about that?

**Srila Prabhupada**: There is a reason people attribute parenthood to God. Worshipers feel a sense of duty or gratitude or other such moods. To see the Godhead as the creator means to remain far away from seeing the actual truth of the Godhead. With God as the creator, worshipers then feel grateful and dutiful, and because of this, they think of God as a parent.

When we see God as only the creator, we fail to realize God’s actual position. Our search for the cause, the cause of causes, and the cause of all causes, becomes obstructed midway. Devotion to the father, devotion to the mother - these are born of the same motivation. Devotion to God as the father or mother is not unmotivated; rather, it is based on material desire. It keeps the worshiper far away from knowing the Supreme Lord’s actual form. Persons who miss Him remain habituated to worldly ethics and tend to follow the path of sense gratification. They do not worship God out of love. Since they want to approach God in a mood of awe and reverence, they are far from knowing the Lord’s actual characteristics.

Sri Caitanyadeva preached God’s sonhood. He rejected the idea that God is our parent. Christianity can be fully developed and \[spiritually\] ethical if Christians hear Sri Caitanyadeva’s teachings.

**Kumara:** The quarrel between the Saktas and the Vaisnavas has been going on for a long time.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Real Vaisnavas do not quarrel with anybody. They do not wish to cause anxiety to any living entity in any way. They are nonenvious. And because they are nonenvious, they try to benefit all living entities. They preach the truth by which the living entities can obtain their actual fortune. Nothing but service to Visnu can benefit the soul. Living entities can find temporary physical and mental happiness and welfare by serving Visnu’s illusory energy, but in the end, such service will only cover the soul even more.

The spirit soul will be deviated from his eternal duty if he serves maya. People who are bewildered by Lord Visnu’s illusory energy, misconstrue the welfare activities of the Vaisnavas, calling the Vaisnavas attempts to help people “envy” and “quarreling.” These people then get busy quarreling with their own physicians or eliminating their physicians all together.

The Saktas can only see up to the mother, because from her they receive what they need for their material enjoyment. They do not care for the mother’s husband. As long as they are averse to Vaisnavism, they will only be able to understand the mother. It will appear to them, according to their material conception, that the energy is the source of everything.

We may think that the mother is the fountainhead, but she is really only the custodian of our physical frame, not of our soul. The soul is unborn; it has no mother. The soul’s propensity is not to enjoy matter or even to renounce it. There is no “give me, give me’’ in the spirit soul’s nature. The soul is the associated counterpart of the Absolute Truth, Visnu. The soul’s only self-interest is to desire happiness for the Absolute Truth. Pure Vaisnavas always search with heart and soul after what will make the Absolute Truth happy.

**Aksaya Babu:** Because she is the custodian of our physical frame, we demand many things from the mother such as wealth or our enemies’ defeat or a beautiful wife, and so on.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Yes, that is true.

**Yogen Babu:** Do trees and creepers have consciousness?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Certainly. But their consciousness is not fully developed. Their eternal propensity is dormant along with their consciousness. Therefore, at present they display the characteristics of undeveloped consciousness.

**Yogen Babu:** They don’t have free will, or do they?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Yes, they do have free will. If you try to beat an animal, it will run away. Nowadays, many of you have seen, by the help of science, that if someone is cutting grass or creepers, those plants try to resist.

**Yogen Babu:** Why can’t action performed out of duty be called devotional service?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Duty is only a regulation. Something that is not done out of pure love cannot be called devotional service. Actions performed only out of a sense of duty have their effect only on the mind, intelligence, and false ego, whereas actions done in love and devotion - attachment to the Supreme - have their effect on the soul. Pure attachment, devotional service, emanates from the soul. Devotional service is the natural propensity of the soul, but the propensity of the mind is to act out of duty, gratitude, and so on.

There is no path that can benefit us other than the cultivation of spiritual consciousness. The whole world is filled only with mental speculation and physical enjoyment. Only Sri Caitanyadeva propagated the elevated teachings of spiritual realization and the soul’s natural propensity. Animals, birds, grass, creepers, human beings, and all the other living entities, became spiritually enlightened by Sri Caitanyadeva’s preaching about spiritual consciousness. All of you should also consider His unique compassion.

## Conversation 8

**Srila Prabhupada and Pandita Gaurinath Sastri**  
**October 30, 1928**

\[Srila Prabhupada was staying at the palace of the Independent King of Sidli, Abhay Narayana Deva Bahadura, in Dhubri, Assam, where he was scheduled to speak *hari-katha.* At that time, Pandita Sriyukta Gaurinath Sastri, B. L. Mahasaya arrived at the feet of Srila Prabhupada. After Sastri Mahasaya offered his obeisances to Srila Prabhupada and took his seat, the *hari-katha* began.\]

**Srila Prabhupada:** You must have discussed Srimad-Bhagavatam a lot.

**Sastri:** What can I say about Srimad-Bhagavatam? I have only gone through some verses of it.

**Srila Prabhupada:** When you were in Gaya, you must have discussed Mahaprabhu.

**Sastri:** Usually I speak from Caitanya-Bhagavata, Caitanya-caritamrta, and so on.

**Srila Prabhupada:** It will be audacity on my part to speak something before a learned scholar like you.

**Sastri:** You are a greatly learned scholar, a maha-bhagavata. Kindly instruct me so that I may achieve some benefit in life. I do not know anything about this subject matter. Therefore, I have come to you to learn these things.

**Srila Prabhupada:** We can only serve you the topics my spiritual master discussed, as his remnants. Apart from this, we have no qualification.

The Supreme Lord is transcendental to the material senses. In the explanation of the world adhoksaja, Srila Jiva Gosvamipada said, adhah krtam aksajam indriyaja jnanam yena. The transcendental object is not accessible to fruitive workers; he cannot be understood with one’s material senses. Were He so accessible, He would be just another one of the objects of mundane enjoyment. Rather, He is the center of all love, and I am His part and parcel of indefinite all loved.

**Sastri:** How am I His part and parcel?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Just like the sun and its ray. A particular sunray is not the complete sun, but at the same time it is not separate from the sun. It is an inseparable part of the sun. When there is a solar eclipse, Rahu does not cover the sun, it covers our vision of the sun. Our vision has been covered, so we are unable to see the sunlike Supreme Lord. The molecules that make up matter create an impediment to our vision. Therefore we have become detached from Him. The covering and throwing potencies of maya have covered and distracted us from the Supreme Lord.

**Sastri:** This means first covering and then throwing?

**Srila Prabhupada:** There is no such thing as covering first and throwing later. Covering and throwing take place simultaneously.

**Sastri:** There has to be a reason before being thrown.

**Srila Prabhupada:** If we look at it from an external point of view, then we would have to say we are thrown first and then covered. For example, if there are two objects placed so close to each other that there is no space between them, how can there be covering? There has to be even a tiny bit of space where one can put the covering. How can there be covering if there is no space at all and both of them are in a close embrace? The covering comes as soon as there is a space created by deviation from service. According to the logic of “piercing one hundred lotus leaves simultaneously,” even if it appears that all the leaves have been pierced at the same time, it really took an imperceptible moment to pierce each leaf separately. Even if covering and throwing are practically simultaneous, the opportunity to throw comes first, and taking advantage of that, then the covering takes place.

According to another understanding, covering can take the shape of throwing. For example, if two objects are closely connected and a nail is inserted between them, then the nail can bring about a covering by separating the two objects. Similarly, the nail of maya’s covering potency can insert itself between the living entities and God and distract the living entities from their position of remaining engaged in God’s service.

**Sastri:** Then there has to be a cause before being thrown and covered.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Yes. Unless there is a reason, it is impossible to separate the two attached objects. Suppose a man is walking on the street. Suddenly, another man comes from the other direction and rams into him. Immediately there is a fight.

**Sastri:** Then why are we thrown?

**Srila Prabhupada:** The reason we are thrown is included in our nature.

**Sastri:** Why do we have such a nature?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Our independence alone causes it.

**Sastri:** From where did the dependent living entities get such independence?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Because we are minute spiritual particles of the supremely independent God, we contain within us a minute portion of His complete qualities. Krsna has full independence, and so the living entities have limited independence.

**Sastri:** When do covering and throwing potencies act on the living entities?

**Srila Prabhupada:** As soon as the living entities become indifferent to serving God, the time for covering and throwing begins.

**Sastri:** What is that indifference?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Indifference means to remain neutral. Neutrality can pull human beings in one direction or another. It can attract us toward the Lord’s service or to become averse to that service. We call that state “marginal.”

Neutrality is achieved after all material objects are eliminated, at the stage called brahma-bhutah prasannatma na socati na kanksati samah sarvesu bhutesu. In that stage, if one is not attracted toward transcendental devotional service, then he is captured by aversion to God.

Aversion can be of two types: the inclination for material enjoyment, and the inclination for renunciation. There are two paths: the path leading to the kingdom of material enjoyment, and the path leading to the kingdom of spiritual pastimes. Therefore, the neutral state is very dangerous. Living entities cannot remain in a state of neutrality; they will either go toward maya or toward serving God.

**Sastri:** Then how can we avoid being covered and thrown?

**Srila Prabhupada:** If one worships the Supreme Lord constantly with love and devotion, he will never be covered or thrown by maya. But the worship has to be continuous. If there is a little gap in the continuity, then maya’s covering potency takes advantage of it and covers us.

**Sastri:** What do you mean by bhajana?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Bhajana is the tie of love between the all-lover and the all-loved.

**Sastri:** That is dasya, servitorship.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Yes. To teach people, it has been called dasya. When dasya progressively improves, it transforms into sakhya, vatsalya, and madhurya. Favorable cultivation of Krsna consciousness without any covering of anyabhilasa - karma, jnana, yoga, tapah, vrata, and so on - is called bhajana. Hatha-yoga, raja-yoga, karma-yoga, jhana-yoga, and vrata-tapasya-yoga are all nondevotional processes and are therefore not fit to be called bhajana.

**Sastri:** The mind is regulated by the practice of yoga, the heart becomes purified by the performance of karma, and the mind becomes peaceful by the cultivation of jnana.

**Srila Prabhupada:** The process of yoga can never permanently regulate the mind, because the process is artificially imposed. Srimad-Bhagavatam (1.6.35) states:

*yamadibhir yoga-pathaih kama-lobha-hato muhuh*  
*mukunda-sevaya yadvat tathatmaddha na samyati*

**“It is true that by practicing restraint of the senses by the yoga system one can get relief from the disturbances of desire and lust, but this is not sufficient to give satisfaction to the soul, for this \[satisfaction\] is derived from devotional service to the Personality of Godhead. ”**

Srimad-Bhagavatam (10.51.60) also states:

*yunjananam abhaktanam pranayamadibhir manah*  
*aksina-vasanam rajan drsyate punar utthitam*

**“The minds of nondevotees who engage in such practices as pranaya- ma are not fully cleansed of material desires. Thus, Î King, material desires are again seen to arise in their minds.”**

And Srimad-Bhagavatam (11.29.2) states:

*prayasah pundarikaksa yunyanto yogino manah*  
*visidanty asamadhanan mano-nigraha-karsitah*

**“O lotus-eyed Lord, generally those yogis who try to steady the mind experience frustration because of their inability to perfect the state of trance. Thus they are weary in their attempt to bring the mind under control.”**

Complete purification of the heart is never possible by the performance of fruitive activities. You must have read all these topics vividly in

Srimad-Bhagavatam, such as in this verse:

*karmana karma-nirharo na hy atyantika isyate*  
*avidvad-adhikaritvat prayascittam vimarsanam*

**“Sukadeva Gosvami, the son of Vedavyasa, answered: My dear King, since acts meant to neutralize impious actions are also fruitive, they will not release one from the tendency to act fruitively. Persons who subject themselves to the rules and regulations of atonement are not at all intelligent. Indeed, they are in the mode of darkness. Unless one is freed from the mode of ignorance, trying to counteract one action through another is useless because this will not uproot one’s desires. Thus even though one may superficially seem pious, he will undoubtedly be prone to act impiously. Therefore, real atonement is enlightenment in perfect knowledge, Vedanta, by which one understands the Supreme Absolute Truth.” (Srimad-Bhagavatam 6.1.11)**

Or this one:

*prayascittani cirnani narayana-paranmukham*  
*na nispunanti rajendra sura-kumbham ivapagah*

**“My dear King, as a pot containing liquor cannot be purified even if washed in the waters of many rivers, nondevotees cannot be purified by processes of atonement even if they perform them very well.” (Srimad-Bhagavatam 6.1.18)**

You will have also read in the Upanisads:

*avidyayam bahudha vartamana*  
*vayam krtartha ity-abhimanyanti balah*  
*yat karmino na pravedayanti ragat*  
*tenaturah ksina-lokas-cyavante*

**“Deep in the darkness of ignorance, these fools think, ‘We have reached the goal.’ Being attached to karmic religions they fail to understand the truth. After their piety is exhausted they fall down to repeated birth and death.” (Mundaka Upanisad 1.2.9)**

And you have already heard what Mahaprabhu says in Sri Caitanya-caritamrta (Madhya 9.263):

*karma-ninda, karma-tyaga, sarva-sastre kahe*  
*karma haite prema-bhakti krsne kabhu nahe*

**“In every revealed scripture there is condemnation of fruitive activities. It is advised everywhere to give up engagement in fruitive activities, for by executing them no one can attain the highest goal of life, love of Godhead.”**

Without hearing hari-katha, one cannot totally purify one’s heart. Such purification is never possible through the practices of karma, jnana, yoga, tapa, vrata, and so on.

Srimad-Bhagavatam (2.8.4-6) states:

*srnvatah sraddhaya nityam grnatas ca sva-cestitam*  
*kalena natidirghena bhagavan visate hrdi*

**“Persons who hear Srimad-Bhagavatam regularly and are always taking the matter very seriously will have the Personality of Godhead Sri Krishna manifested in their hearts within a short time.”**

*pravistah karna-randhrena svanam bhava-saroruham*  
*dhunoti samalam krsnah salilasya yatha carat*

**“The sound incarnation of Lord Krsna, the Supreme Soul \[i.e., Srimad-Bhagavatam \], enters into the heart of a self-realized devotee, sits on the lotus flower of his loving relationship, and thus cleanses the dust of material association, such as lust, anger, and hankering. Thus it acts like autumnal rains upon pools of muddy water.”**

*dhautatma purusah krsna-pada-mulam na muncati*  
*mukta-sarva-pariklesah panthah sva-saranam yatha*

**“A pure devotee of the Lord whose heart has once been cleansed by the process of devotional service never relinquishes the lotus feet of Lord Krsna, for they fully satisfy him, as a traveler is satisfied at home after a troubled journey.”**

Even if one’s mind becomes peaceful and he therefore becomes self-satisfied, if he does not engage in hearing and chanting about Hari, then such self-satisfaction will cause his degradation.

Srimad-Bhagavatam (10.2.32) states:

*ye ’nye ’ravindaksa vimukta-maninas*  
*tvayy asta-bhavad avisuddha-buddhayah*  
*aruhya kpcchrena param padam tatah*  
*patanty adho 'nadrta-yusmad-anghrayah*

**“O lotus-eyed Lord, although nondevotees who accept severe austerities and penances to achieve the highest position may think themselves liberated, their intelligence is impure. They fall down from their position of imagined superiority because they have no regard for Your lotus feet.”**

*jivan-mukta api punar bandhanam yanti karmabhih*  
*yady acintya-maha-saktau bhagavaty aparadhinah*

**“Those souls who have attained liberation while living can again become bound if they commit an offense to the inconceivably powerful Supreme Personality of Godhead.” (Vasana-bhasyodhrta, Sri Bhagava- ta-parisista-vacana)**

*jivan-muktah prapadyante kvacit samsara-vasanam*  
*yogino na vilipyante karmabhir bhagavat-parah*

**“Those who have attained liberation while living sometimes develop material attachment, but those who are fixed in bhakti-yoga, who have dedicated themselves to Krsna, will never again have to suffer repeated birth and death.” (Vasana-bhasyodhrta, Sri Bhagavata-parisista-vacana)**

*jnane prayasam udapasya namanta eva*  
*jivanti san-mukharitam bhavadiya-vartam*  
*sthane sthitah sruti-gatam tanu-van-manobhir*  
*ye prayaso ’jita jito ’py asi tais tri-lokyam*

**“Those who, even while remaining situated in their established social positions, throw away the process of speculative knowledge and with their body, words and mind offer all respects to descriptions of Your personality and activities, dedicating their lives to these narrations, which are vibrated by You personally and by Your pure devotees, certainly conquer Your Lordship, although You are otherwise unconquerable by anyone within the three worlds.” (Srimad-Bhagavatam 10.14.3)**

*sva-sukha-nibhrta-cetas tad-vyudastanya-bhavo*  
*’py ajita-rucira-lilakrsta-saras tadiyam*  
*vyatanuta krpaya yas tattva-dipam puranam*  
*tam akhila-vrjina-ghnam vyasa-sunum nato ’smi*

**“Let me offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, the son of Vyasadeva, Sukadeva Gosvami. It is he who defeats all inauspicious things within this universe. Although in the beginning he was absorbed in the happiness of Brahman realization and was living in a secluded place, giving up all other types of consciousness, he became attracted by the pleasing most melodious pastimes of Lord Krsna. He therefore mercifully spoke the supreme Purana, Srimad Bhagavatam, which is the bright light of the Absolute Truth and which describes the activities of Lord Krsna.” (Srimad-Bhagavatam 12.12.69)**

**Sastri:** Yes, devotional service is the only spontaneous dharma for Kali-yuga.

**Srila Prabhupada:** Why only Kali-yuga? Devotional service is the religion for all times, all places, and all persons. Karma, jnana, and yoga are only naimittika (occasional) dharmas; they are not the living entities’ spontaneous and constitutional propensity. Devotional service is the only eternal dharma for liberated souls. The religious systems people invent while they are still conditioned and under the influence of anartha are what we have come to call karma, jnana, yoga, tapa, and vrata. You have learned from Srimad-Bhagavatam (1.7.10, 4-7):

*atmaramas ca munayo nirgrantha apy urukrame*  
*kurvanty ahaitukim bhaktim ittham-bhuta-guno harih*  
*bhakti-yogena manasi samyak pranihite 'male*  
*apasyat ðṝrusṁḍ ðurñṁḍ mayam ca tad-apasrayam*  
*yaya sammohito jiva atmanam tri-gunatmakam*  
*paro ’pi manute ’nartham tat-krtam cabhipadyate*  
*anarthopasamam saksad bhakti-yogam adhoksaje*  
*lokasyajanato vidvams cakre satvata-samhitam*  
*yasyam vai sruyamanayam krsne ðṁrṁḍṁ-ðurusṛ*  
*bhaktir utpadyate pumsah soka-moha-bhayapaha*

**“All varieties of atmaramas, especially those established on the path of self-realization, though freed from all kinds of material bondage, desire to render unalloyed devotional service to the Personality of Godhead. This means the Lord has transcendental qualities and therefore can attract everyone, including even liberated souls.**

**“Thus he fixed his mind, perfectly engaging it by linking it in devotional service (bhakti-yoga) without any tinge of materialism, and thus he saw the Absolute Personality of Godhead along with His external energy, which was under full control.**

**“Due to this external energy, the living entity, although transcendental to the three modes of material nature, thinks of himself as a material product and thus undergoes the reactions of material miseries.**

**“The material miseries of the living entity, which are superfluous to him, can be directly mitigated by the linking process of devotional service. But the mass of people do not know this, and therefore the learned Vyasadeva compiled this Vedic literature, which is in relation to the Supreme Truth.**

**“Simply by giving aural reception to this Vedic literature, the feeling for loving devotional service to Lord Krsna, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, sprouts up at once to extinguish the fire of lamentation, illusion, and fearfulness.”**

Maya means “that which is not.” That which is, the positive, is God. Negation of the positive, God, is the negative, or maya, As soon as we connect the negative maya to the positive, personal God, considering ourselves God, we give birth to self-worship. When I understand myself as a servant of God, then I am not bewildered into serving maya. But as long as I do not become established as a servant of God, a worshiper of Lord Visnu, I will see the whole world as meant for my enjoyment. In that state, I will no longer see the world as filled with ingredients for God’s worship and I become mad to lord it over material nature. Then, being envious of others, I will slaughter goats, hens, and fish, or will try to enjoy river water, fruits, nature’s beauty, and so on. Only when we actually become wise wifi we come to know that the senses have only delegated power.

Only by transcendental knowledge can both my enjoying spirit and my sinful mentality be removed. We will not approach the class of professors that is puffed up with lust, anger, illusion, greed, pride, and envy. If we approach such people we can never gain transcendental knowledge.

We have forgotten our original nature by coming to this perverted world. Srimad-Bhagavatam (12.12.55) states:

*avismrtih êrsnṁ-padaravindayoh*  
*ksinoty abhadrani ca sam tanoti*  
*sattvasya suddhim paramatma-bhaktim*  
*jnanam ca vijnana-viraga-yuktam*

**“Remembrance of Lord Krsna’s lotus feet destroys everything inauspicious and awards the greatest good fortune. It purifies the heart and bestows devotion for the Supreme Soul, along with knowledge enriched with realization and renunciation.”**

**Sastri:** Yes, our only need is to remember the Supreme Lord all the time. *Smartavyah satatam visnur vismartavyo na jatucit.*

**Srila Prabhupada:** The moment I forget the Supreme Lord I become an acquirer. I dive into acquiring land, knowledge, money, and so on. This is the improper use of my spiritual nature, so inevitably I will commit indiscretions and develop misconceptions. I will inevitably become an empiricist, and then I will spend my life busily accumulating material objects.

**Sastri:** What do you mean by the ascending path?

**Srila Prabhupada:** The ascending path is Ravana’s policy of building a staircase to heaven. That kind of uphill work is the most puzzling task. Srimad-Bhagavatam (10.14.4) instructs us to reject this kind of uphill work, this attempt to build a staircase to heaven:

*sreyah-srtim bhaktim udasya te vibho*  
*klisyanti ye kevala-bodha-labdhaye*  
*tesam asau klesala eva sisyate*  
*nanyad yatha sthula-tusavaghatinam*

**“My dear Lord, devotional service unto You is the best path for self-realization. If someone gives up that path and engages in the cultivation of speculative knowledge, he will simply undergo a troublesome process and will not achieve his desired result As a person who beats an empty husk of wheat cannot get grain, one who simply speculates cannot achieve self-realization. His only gain is trouble.”**

Srimad-Bhagavatam (10.2.32) states:

*ye ’nye ’ravindaksa vimukta-maninas*  
*tvayy asta-bhavad avisuddha-buddhayah*  
*aruhya krcchrena param padam tatah*  
*patanty adho 'nadrta-yusmad-anghrayah*

**“O lotus-eyed Lord, although nondevotees who accept severe austerities and penances to achieve the highest position may think themselves liberated, their intelligence is impure. They fall down from their position of imagined superiority because they have no regard for Your lotus feet.”**

One method is to take up a lamp and forcibly try to find the sun at night, while the other method is to pray for sunrise and then to see the sun with the help of the sun’s light. When we desire material enjoyment, we follow the ascending path. We begin to endeavor to acquire knowledge, mystic siddhis, the results of our fruitive activities. But such an endeavor via the ascending path will always remain incomplete. A twenty-year-old civilization appears insignificant when compared to a hundred-year-old civilization. Again, a hundred years of experience will prove faulty when compared to two hundred years of experience - and that two hundred years of experience will be rejected when compared against a thousand years of experience. Therefore, intelligent persons never take the ascending path.

**Sastri:** Can the ascending process be abandoned completely?

**Srila Prabhupada:** As long as human beings depend on their own strength, pride, and experience, they cannot surrender to the lotus feet of the Supreme Lord. Until we surrender and take shelter of the Supreme Lord, we will continue to glorify the ascending path. When we understand just how insignificant our borrowed strength is, how puny our pride, and how useless our own endeavor, only then will we surrender to God and walk the descending path. You have read the story of Gajendra, king of elephants in Srimad-Bhagavatam. At the beginning, Gajendra became intoxicated and was enjoying the company of female elephants in the lake near the Rtumat forest. As he splashed around in the water, the lives of the aquatics became miserable. They were unable to live in that water. After a while, a large and powerful crocodile came and bit the intoxicated Gajendra’s leg. A fierce battle took place between the two that went on for a thousand years, each of them displaying their prowess and showing off their strength.

Over time, Gajendra’s physical strength diminished, and so his boasting pride also diminished. Finding no other way to free himself from the crocodile’s clutches, Gajendra decided to take shelter at the lotus feet of the Supreme Lord. Surrender to the Lord would alone benefit him.

As long as the living entities think, like the intoxicated Gajendra, that their insignificant ego is great, they continue to glorify the ascending path. And when the glories of taking shelter of the Supreme Lord appear in their hearts, they surrender, or follow the descending path. Saintly persons always preach the process of surrender. They do not encourage anyone to follow the ascending path. However great one may be, if he considers the ascending path to be the path of auspiciousness, his degradation is inevitable. Krsna alone is the shelter of all. The idea of accepting any other shelter can never protect us.

The Bhagavad-gita (3.27) states:

*prakrteh kriyamanani gunaih karmani sarvasah*  
*ahankara-vimudhatma kartaham iti manyate*

**“The spirit soul bewildered by the influence of false ego thinks himself the doer of activities that are in actuality carried out by the three modes of material nature.”**

Only those who are bewildered by false ego maintain a fruitive mentality, remaining always interested in material advancement. They are followers of the ascending path. The impersonalists who want liberation also want to become great by their own endeavors. They falsely think that they have become liberated. The impersonalists want to become one with God. The small thirst to become great is called “ascending.” The mystic yogis want to become a little higher than others by attaining mystic power. All these fall into the category of “ascending.” By following these processes, the living entities simply become degraded. As Srimad-Bhagavatam (10.2.32) states:

*aruhya krcchrena param padam tatah*  
*patanty adho ‘nadrta-yusmad-anghrayah*

Whatever our condition, we should not try to become impersonalists, or follow the ascending path, maintain the sinful desire of fruitive workers or mystic yogis, or be driven by the desire for material enjoyment and liberation. Instead, we should surrender body, mind, and words to the saintly persons and hear from them submissively. Only then will we be able to conquer the unconquerable Lord.

Whether we are learned or foolish, wherever we are in our present condition, we should hear transcendental topics from the mouths of the Supreme Lord’s devotees. We currently live in the kingdom of faults. If we try to discuss scripture by enhancing the topics with the fruits of our mental speculation, we will be deceived. Trying to discuss scripture driven by a desire for material enjoyment or liberation is like trying to bring scripture under our control. But the scripture is directly Krsna - an incarnation of Krsna. The Supreme Lord says in the Bhagavad-gita (4.34):

*tad viddhi pranipatena pariprasnena sevaya*  
*upadeksyanti te jnanam jnaninas tattva-darsinah*

**“Just try to learn the truth by approaching a spiritual master. Inquire from him submissively and render service unto him. The self-realized soul can impart knowledge unto you because he has seen the truth.”**

The endeavor to master maya also falls in the category of fruitive activities. Being intoxicated by the pride of being a master, if we make only a show of receiving instructions, we will certainly be cheated; the scriptures will not be revealed to us. The scriptures are revealed only to surrendered souls. The Svetasvatara Upanisad (6.23) states:

*yasya deve para bhaktir yatha deve tatha gurau*  
*tasyaite kathita hy arthah prakasante mahatmanah*

**“Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.”**

Mahaprabhu taught us:

*trnad api sunicena taror api sahisnuna*  
*amanina manadena kirtaniyah sada harih*

**“One who thinks himself lower than the grass, who is more tolerant than a tree, and who does not expect personal honor but is always prepared to give all respect to others, can very easily always chant the holy name of the Lord.”**

As long as we remain humbler than a blade of grass, we will be able to perform hari-kirtana. The moment we try to become “bigger” or “greater,” we will have to say goodbye to kirtana.

It is said in the Brahma-samhita (5.38):

*premanjana-cchurita-bhakti-viloṣanena*  
*santah sadaiva hrdayesu vilokayanti*  
*yam syamasundaram acintya-guna-svarupam*  
*govindam adi-purusam tam aham bhajami*

**“I worship Govinda, the primeval Lord, who is Syamasundara, Krsna Himself with inconceivable innumerable attributes, whom the pure devotees see in their heart of hearts with the eye of devotion tinged with the salve of love.”**

When one becomes established on the platform of advaya-jnana, there is no longer any question of rupture or conflict.

**Sastri:** In the phrase mayam ca tad-apasrayam \[“{and thus he saw} the Absolute Personality of Godhead along with His external energy, which was under full control.”\], the word ca explains that the Supreme Lord and maya are two separate truths. Is that correct?

**Srila Prabhupada:** Seeing the word ca, some people think that the Supreme Lord is one and maya is something other. But it is not like that. The reason the author uses ca is because maya is only Krsna’s energy; maya cannot be considered Krsna, yet maya has no separate existence from Krsna. This fact has been explained in the four original verses of Srimad-Bhagavatam \[2.9.34\] *as rte ’rtham yat pratiyeta na pratiyeta catmani*: maya is the Supreme Lord’s external energy.

In his commentary on this verse, Sridhara Svami writes: tadapasrayam isvarasrayam tadadhinam mayamcapasyat: The living entities are the energy of the absolute Godhead; they are not the absolute Godhead themselves. The Supreme Lord is never overwhelmed by maya. This is because maya is under His control. As stated in Sri Caitanya-caritainrta (Madhya 6.162):

*‘mayadlnsa’ ‘maya-vasa’—isvare-jive bheda*  
*hena-jive isvara-saha kaha ta’abheda*

**“The Lord is the master of His potency, and the jivas are the servants of His potency. That is the difference between the Lord and the living beings.”**

Those who think of the impoverished \[daridra\] Narayana become bewildered by Narayana’s illusory energy and end up becoming fruitive workers. Thus they fall down from the Lord’s service. Narayana is never controlled by maya. Laksmi’s husband, Narayana, is never poor. The Supreme Lord never becomes entangled or tied by the noose of maya. These things have been clearly explained by Sri Caitanyadeva. It is the insignificant living entities who sometimes think themselves poor, sometimes rich, sometimes a king, sometimes a subject, sometimes a sense enjoyer, sometimes a candidate for liberation, sometimes a yogi, and sometimes an ascetic. This is only because they forget Krsna. The minute conscious living entities have the capacity to be bewildered by maya. Srimad-Bhagavatam repeatedly refutes imaginary and sinful ideas like Narayana becoming poor or God becoming bewildered by maya.

As a result of forgetting the Supreme Lord Krsna, the living entities become illusioned by maya and think like what is described in Srimad-Bhagavatam (1.7.5):

*yaya sammohito jiva atmanam tri-gunatmakam*  
*paro ’pi manute ’nartham tat-krtam cabhipadyate*

**“Due to this external energy, the living entity, although transcendental to the three modes of material nature, thinks of himself as a material product and thus undergoes the reactions of material miseries.”**

Even though the living entities are spiritual, they glorify anarthas, or unwanted things. “I am poor,” “I am rich” - these mindsets are born from anarthas, or forgetfulness of the self. The word para means “transcendental to the three modes of material nature.” Even if the living entities belong to the transcendental mode of pure goodness, they still come to consider themselves “poor,” etc., being entangled by maya’s covering and throwing potencies. This is not Narayana’s poverty but their own forgetfulness of Krsna. Those who think Narayana poor are fully engrossed by their own anarthas. Therefore, Srimad-Bhagavatam (1.7.6) prescribes that the best way to remove this disease of anartha is to cultivate devotional service to the transcendental Personality of Godhead (*anarthopasamam saksad bhakti-yogam adhoksaje).*

To engage in the service of sense objects is simply cheating. It is impossible to achieve devotional service to the Supreme Lord by taking shelter of cheating religions like karma, jnana, or yoga, or the desires for material enjoyment or liberation. Pseudo devotional service, service covered by karma, jnana, yoga, or tapasya, is not pure devotional service. Therefore, it cannot touch the lotus feet of the transcendental Godhead. For this reason, until one renders devotional service directly to the transcendental Personality, one cannot remove one’s anarthas, and as long as one’s anarthas remain, one will speak like a madman, calling Lord Narayana “poor.”

So approaching with a desire for unalloyed devotional service, one has to hear from the saintly persons at all times. This is how to receive knowledge of the spiritual world. To consider the Supreme Lord Visnu equal to the demigods is one of the diseases of anartha-filled people. Therefore, the perfect physician, Vyasadeva, warns us in the Sri Daksinatya:

*arcye visnau sila-dhir gurusu nara-matir vaisnave jati-buddhir*  
*visnor va vaisnavanam kali-mala-mathane pada-tirthe ’mbu-buddhih*  
*sri-visnor namni mantre sakala-kalusa-he sabda-samanya-buddhir*  
*visnau sarvesvarese tad-itara-sama-dhir yasya va naraki sah*

**“One who thinks the worshipable Deity form of Lord Visnu is a stone, the bona fide spiritual master is an ordinary man, the Vaisnava belongs to a caste in the varnasrama system, the water that has washed the feet of Lord Visnu or the Vaisnavas, and that actually removes the sins of the Kali-yuga, is ordinary water, the name of Lord Visnu, which is a sacred mantra that removes all sin, is an ordinary sound, or the Supreme Lord Visnu is the same as other persons, is a resident of hell.”**

As soon as we speak like this, immediately, those who do not have strong faith in the Absolute Truth say that in the Vaisnava literature, Lord Visnu is glorified as the greatest, but in Saivite literature, Lord Siva is glorified in the same way; for the Saktas, Durga is called topmost, and for those who worship Ganapati, Ganapati is the greatest. The devotees of Surya, of course, call him the greatest. So these deities are all the same. The devotees of different demigods have claimed that their worshipable deity is topmost. In the Vedic literature, Agni, Vayu, Varuna, and Visnu have all been mentioned. Therefore, Visnu must be equal to those demigods.

But arguments like these are born from a lack of faith in the nondual Absolute Truth. Only people who have anarthas speak like this. It is nothing but sophistry or a kind of Skepticism. Sophists say, “The individual is the measure of all things.” Different men judge differently, and one man’s opinion is as good as any another’s. “So many men, so many minds.” Western philosophers call this relativism, because it makes our opinions about things relative to our mental constitutions. Relativism is skepticism or agnosticism and is born from empiricism. It means one does not have faith in the Absolute Truth, even if one makes a show of having such faith. It is simply one of many forms of godlessness.

Followers of the Absolute Truth are nonenvious. They say:

*isvarah paramah krsnah sac-cid-ananda-vigrahah*  
*anadir adir govindah sarva-karana-karanam.*

**“Krsna, who is known as Govinda, is the supreme controller. He has an eternal, blissful, spiritual body. He is the origin of all. He has no other origin, for He is the prime cause of all causes.” (Brahma-samhita 5.1)**

Krsna alone is an ocean of all transcendental mellows. We have to serve Him under the guidance of the eternal devotees, who serve Him in one of five kinds of relationships, or rasas.

*aradhyo bhagavan vrajesa-tanayas tad-dhama vrndavanam*  
*ramya kacid upasana vraja-vadhu-vargena ya kalpita*  
*srimad-bhagavatam pramanam amalam prema pum-artho mahan*  
*sri-caitanya-mahaprabhor matam idam atradaro nah na parah*

**“The Supreme Personality of Godhead, the son of Nanda Maharaja, is to be worshiped along with His transcendental abode, Vrndavana. The most pleasing form of worship for the Lord is that which was performed by the gopis of Vrndavana. Srimad-Bhagavatam is the spotless authority on everything, and pure love of God is the ultimate goal of life for all men. These statements, for which we have the highest regard, are the opinion of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu.” (Caitanya-manjusa, a commentary on Srimad Bhagavatam*****)***

He who helps us realize this is the bestower of transcendental knowledge, the spiritual master. We simply have to approach such a spiritual master. Srimad-Bhagavatam (11.3.21) states:

*tasmad gurum prapadyeta jijnasuh sreya uttamam*  
*sabde pare ca nisnatam brahmany upasamasrayam*

**“Therefore any person who seriously desires real happiness must seek a bona fide spiritual master and take shelter of him by initiation. The qualification of the bona fide guru is that he has realized the conclusions of the scriptures by deliberation and is able to convince others of these conclusions. Such great personalities, who have taken shelter of the Supreme Godhead, leaving aside all material considerations, should be understood to be bona fide spiritual masters.”**

We have to hear hari-katha, or Srimad-Bhagavatam, from this type of spiritual master Vaisnava. It is not necessary to hear from a person expert in Sanskrit or in giving instructions to others. It is the exalted devotee who practices what he preaches from whom we have to hear.

*yaha, bhagavata pada vaisnavera sthane*  
*ekanta asraya kara caitanya-carane*

**“If you want to understand Srimad-Bhagavatam,” he said, “you must approach a self-realized Vaisnava and hear from him. You can do this when you have completely taken shelter of the lotus feet of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu.”**  
**(Sri Caitanya-caritampta, Antya 5.131)**

*caitanyera bhakta-ganera nitya kara ‘sanga’*  
*tabeta janiba siddhanta-samudra-taranga*

**“Associate regularly with the devotees of Si! Caitanya Mahaprabhu, for then only will you understand the waves of the ocean of devotional service.” (Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Antya 5.132)**

We have to glorify Hari. Mahaprabhu taught us, kirtaniyah sada harih. The word sada indicates that there is no gap of time between performances of kirtana. The human beings have no other duty, no other work, except to glorify Hari at each moment. We have to glorify Hari even to the animals and birds. If fools call us crazy or ignorant, it does not matter.

*parivadatu jano yatha tatha va*  
*nanu mukharo na vayam vicarayamah*  
*hari-rasa-madira-madati-matta*  
*bhuvi viluthamo natamo nirvisamah*

**“Let the garrulous populace say whatever they like; we shall pay them no regard. Thoroughly maddened by the ecstasy of the intoxicating beverage of love for Krsna, we shall enjoy life running about, rolling on the ground, and dancing in ecstasy.” (Padyavali 73)**

I gave you so much trouble. When you study Srimad-Bhagavatam you must come across many points like this.

**Sastri:** If I get a spiritual master like you, only then will discussion of Srimad-Bhagavatam be possible. You have been so kind to me by revealing to me the actual nature of devotional service.

**Srila Prabhupada:** When I was speaking about acinmatravada, cinmatravada, and cidvilasa, the great teacher and learned scholar Sriyukta Pramatha Natha Tharkabhusana Mahasaya wanted to hear more about cidvilasavada.

**Sastri:** The great instructor Pramatha Natha Tharkabhusana is related to me by marriage.

**Srila Prabhupada:** The same meeting between Radha and Govinda that took place during the solar eclipse at Syamantapancaka - Kuruksetra - will be revived now in the same place. In order to render our service to that cause, we are going there very soon along with many devotees from Bangladesh. This time, during the solar eclipse, that same pastime from Srimad-Bhagavatam will be enacted at Kuruksetra. But I am now taking too much of your time. I don’t want to trouble you any further. We have no other work; we can spend days and nights talking about this.

**Sastri:** You have given me no trouble. Rather, I have been privileged today to receive your instruction.

\[After speaking these words, Pandita Sriyukta Gaurinath Sastri offered his obeisances to Srila Prabhupada and. got up to leave. Srila Prabhupada gave him copies of the Harmonist, Sajjana Tosam, Gaudiya, and Nadrya Prakasa magazines as gifts. On seeing the daily Nadia Prakasa, Sastri Mahasaya was surprised and said joyfully, “You have a daily news paper! A daily newspaper for spiritual subject matter! This especially is completely new in a place like Bengal!”\]

**Srila Prabhupada:** Mahaprabhu ordered, kirtaniyah sada harih: Let people hear hari-katha every day. When people of the world meet one another, they talk about the worldly things they have read in worldly newspapers. They are completely surrounded by the mundane climate. We say, “Let people hear caitanya-katha every day. When they meet, let them talk about Caitanya. Let them always live and breathe in the climate of caitanya-katha. Let there be nothing else in the whole world except caitanya-katha. In order to keep the cultivation of devotion to Caitanya constantly awake in our hearts, we have to remain engrossed in caitanya-katha at all times. Today, an arrangement is being made, with so much difficulty, facing so many obstacles, with so much hard labor and expense, just so discussions of Hari can go on constantly. The unconscious world is suffering from the disease of their own anarthas. People have become so unconscious that they refuse to take the medicine that would benefit them. They will do everything else, but they will not listen to caitanya-katha. They will risk their lives, spend their money, and use all their intelligence to hear topics unrelated to Sri Caitanyadeva and thus invite their own ruination. They will eat abominable foods and aggravate the symptoms of their disease and finally rush to hell. Still, they will not listen to discussions about Sri Caitanya, even when hearing such things is the only way for them to find auspiciousness. They promise not to adopt the path of auspiciousness. Despite this, the devotees of Caitanya publish the Nadiya Prakasa, which reports Caitanya’s message by pushing aside all the impediments presented by this world.

**Sastri:** A daily spiritual newspaper is certainly most astonishing.

\[Sastri Mahasaya once again offered his obeisances to Srila Prabhupada, then left. Later, he returned to where Srila Prabhupada was staying and presented the editor of the Gaudiya with a copy of a book he had translated and. commentated called Naradiya Bhaktisutra, asking him to give it as a gift to the lotus hand of Srila Prabhupada.\]

All glories to Sri Guru and Gauranga.

## Part 2 - Vaktrtavali. Discourses

## Discourse 1

**Spiritual Truth is Realized Only Through Aural Reception**

Place: Sri Saccidananda Matha, Cuttack,  
On the occasion of installing the Deity of Sriman Mahaprabhu at Sri Saccidananda Matha  
Time: Saturday afternoon, July 9, 1927

**Mangalacarana**

*mukam karoti vacalam pangum langhayate girim*  
*yat-krpa tam aham vande paramananda madhavam*

**The spiritual master’s words, which come down in disciplic succession, are accepted only by those with service-inclined ears.**

Whatever we hear in our day-to-day life we have to assess with our senses: is it true or false? But I have no way to understand what my spiritual master explains to me except through my ears.

Since spiritual knowledge is beyond the reach of the senses, it is a mockery to even try to use our senses to discover it. Just as a bull whose nose is pierced with a six-cubit rope cannot reach the green grass hundreds of thousands of yojanas away, or a dwarf cannot touch the moon, so it is futile to try to measure spiritual subjects with senses conditioned by material faults. If I cannot grasp something, it means that at present, I am not qualified to understand it. Had it been accessible to my senses I would certainly have tried for it.

But it is a waste of time to make such an endeavor. We will not be able to find the real truth using our logic and argument. Rather, whatever we hear from the mouth of the spiritual master we will have to understand through pranipata, full surrender, pariprasna, honest inquiry, and seva, service.

The word pranipata indicates that we should not be inattentive while hearing the guru’s words; in other words, we should hear with full attention. That transcendental object that my senses could not comprehend is accessible only through the ears. Whatever I have heard while sitting at the lotus feet of my spiritual master is the only access I have to reality. There is no other way to know reality except through pranipata, attentive hearing.

Pariprasna refers to the honest inquiry a disciple makes of his spiritual master. When we ask a question, we should not have asked it with some hidden motive, already planning not to follow the answer we are given. To ask questions like a skeptic is not “honest inquiry.” To ask questions while controlled by false pride, thinking we already know the answer, is also not “honest inquiry.” If we simply make a show of honest inquiry and then do not hear submissively, then the answer we receive will simply create a revolution in the heart and cause many more questions. Asking questions like this is also not part of “honest inquiry.”

Whatever the mundane logicians and mental speculators say about the spiritual world can only be a juggling of words from an unenlightened viewpoint. Sound has three categories: (1) rudhi, (2) yaugiki, and (3) yoga-rudhi. Rudhi is self-manifest - that is, literal or obvious, such as when someone loudly chastises someone else. Everyone understands that this is a chastisement. A cow understands it, as does a human being. Even those human beings who do not know the language in which the chastisement is delivered understand the heart of what is being conveyed.

Yaugiki refers to that which is described in the Vedic glossary - that is, the etymological meaning of what is being said. And yoga-rudhi refers to a combination of rudhi and yaugiki - that is, the words have etymylogical as well as literal, underived meanings; it refers to the connotations derived by combining two or more words. When my ignorance is prominently featured in the self-manifest (rudhi) nature of a sound, it is to be understood that I am speaking from an unenlightened viewpoint. On the other hand, when the spontaneous realization of the soul, or the realization spoken from an enlightened viewpoint, reveals the actual meaning of a sound, it is to be understood that I am expressing something enlightened.

For example, as soon as a child is born it automatically knows what to eat. A newborn calf does not need to be taught to drink its mother’s milk. There is no need to teach the calf through some derivative meaning.

**Sound and the object of sound**

There is a difference, in the mundane world, between a sound and the object that sound represents. For example, as soon as I say “tamarind tree,” the sound vibrates from my lips, echoes in the ether, and enters the ear, but that sound is still simply an indication of a tamarind tree and not a tamarind tree itself.

**When does honest inquiry begin?**

Supreme Absolute Truth, the essence of Vedanta, states that the object of transcendental knowledge can speak, even if He does not have a material tongue; He can see, even if He does not possess material eyes. Our material knowledge cannot extend to Him. Our ears cannot hear His katha. But when I hear transcendental information from my spiritual master, then automatically my urge to make honest inquiry begins.

Since the Supreme Personality of Godhead is of such a nature that the unenlightened viewpoint cannot access Him, then He must be something distinct from the object at which ordinary mundane sound aims. Here the etymologists would say that whatever is not understood or experienced by the nonaural senses is simply sound \[and not the object itself\]. This is because in this world, whatever state of being or object words refer to is supported by the existence of that state of being or object. \[We only give names to things in this world that exist in some form\]

**In the spiritual realm, the sound itself is the object**

The spiritual world is different from this world. In the spiritual world, the sound vibration is itself the object. There is no difference between a name and the object being named. If sound vibration is transcendental or spiritual, there is no difference between the name and the named. In the material world, however, there is a difference between objects and the words our senses use to define them.

**Pranipata, pariprasna, and seva are the only ways to access truth transcendental to the material senses**

The transcendental sound vibration that echoed in Brahma’s heart through the agency of Lord Krsna’s speaking, that same sound one glorifies to become delivered - I have heard these from the lotus mouth of my spiritual master. I simply had to inquire honestly about that sound vibration in order to be given it and to receive it. Apart from that, I have no other qualification. There is no way to obtain knowledge about the Absolute Truth other than by pranipata, surrender. Without sravana, or a service attitude in hearing, it is not possible to receive knowledge of the Absolute Truth. One becomes qualified to hear through pranipata; sraddha qualifies one to hear.

**Opportunity for the spirit of argument**

We are motivated by evil when we try to defeat the truth by argumentation, imagining that we can measure and examine the truth’s very existence. Even if such a thing were possible, such as in the case of dualistic objects in this qualitative world, it is not possible when applied to the transcendental, nondual Absolute Truth. There is no need to make an endeavor to know the Absolute Truth, who is one without a second, except to engage in faithful hearing. When the Supreme Absolute Truth comes personally to reveal Himself, then one should serve Him with faith and devotion.

**The only right a disciple has is the right to ask the following question**

The only right I have as a disciple is to ask, “How can the Supreme Absolute Truth be realized?”

There is no room for argumentation in this sphere of advaya-jnana \[absolute knowledge\]. Wherever there is a difference between the mode of goodness and the modes of passion and ignorance, we can understand that we have not realized advaya-jnana. Advaya-jnana refers to the Absolute Truth, which is one without a second. One need not make any distinction \[between His forms and names\] - one need not think of Him as an idol. Rather, we must understand that we are speaking about that transcendental sound vibration that is nondifferent from the object it names. Whatever we learn by depending on our senses can be defeated by various types of arguments. But there is no need to impede understanding of the indisputable advaya-jnana by using mundane logic and argument.

Mental speculation is always based on acceptance and rejection; it has two sides. But since there is no substitute for the eternally resolute advaya-jnana, there is no room for argumentation about it based on acceptance and rejection. That which is beyond logic and argument, which is inconceivable, is not subject to debate. Rather, it is better to serve the Absolute Truth, to worship the worshipable object, rather than to speculate about whether to accept it or reject it. Spiritual subjects, which fall under the term advaya-jnana, are not meant to be understood through mundane logic and argument.

**A perfect learner is one who is fixed in the practice of aural reception**

The Vedas \[Mundaka Upanisad 1.2.12\] describe, *tad-vijnanartham sa gurum evabhigacchet samit-panih srotriyam brahma-nistham*: to remain fixed without interruption is called nistha. One who has developed such nistha for the Supreme Object, is called brahma-nistham. It is not possible to bring that Object, which is fixed in Brahman, under the jurisdiction of argumentation. A srotriya is one who is expert in hearing. Since the eternal service propensity is awakened in the srotriya, he does not bother with logic and argument.

This does not mean that a srotriya is incapable of thinking logically or that he encourages wrong ideas. All he says is that the inconceivable truth does not come under the jurisdiction of our mental power. Spiritual science is not open to sensory knowing the way material objects are.

So who is this person from whom we should learn the truth, in disciplic succession? The Vedas say that person is sat, self-realized. After we hear spiritual topics from him in disciplic succession, our tendency to measure everything with our senses stops.
